MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2018-01-08 | CLIP_ID: 635 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 635

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
January 8, 2018 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Ca se, Bryan Cook,  Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Scott 
Roybal, Dr. Mark Rinne, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, 
and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney. The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 RECOGNITION OF DICKY SHANOR FOR HIS SERVICE AS CITY COUNCIL 
PRESIDENT FOR 2017.   Mayor Orr expressed appreciation to Mr. Shanor for his service as 
Council President in 2017 and presented him with a gavel plaque.  Mr. Shanor briefly reflected on 
the past year generally and thanked his family, co-elected officials and City staff for their support. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on t he agenda.)   Carol Intlekofer , City Clerk,  advised that Item #28(a) was 
inadvertently placed on the Consent Agenda  and as the item is an announcement she would be 
reading the item into  the record.  Mr. Luna moved to approve , seconded by Mr. White. Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of  the Governing Body on December 20, 2017.   Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouche rs as presented, seconded by Mr . Cook.  
Upon inquiry, Vicki Nemecek, Public Works Director, provided information on a voucher payable 
to Noel Griffith Jr. & Associates , which  was postponed for action at the December 2 0, 2017  
Council meeting, stating services were provided in conjunction with a building remodel project to 
accommodate moving municipal court into the municipal building.  She explained funding for the 
project (6th Penny Sales Tax); anticipated future contract to come before the governing body, and 
emergency procurement procedures that were followed to meet the timeline to accommodate 
moving municipal court. Concern w as expressed over the process  of paying for professional 
services of this type (under t he $35,000 threshold) without a contract approved by the governing 
body when overall services to the same vendor related to a project will exceed that amount; what 
constitutes an  emergency situation , and further review of the process.  Mayor Orr provided 
information on the temporary housing of municipal court within the municipal building due to the 
building not being financially feasible as a permanent court location due to parking and ADA 
issues, and, in response to inquiry, stated a traffic/parking analysis was not conducted prior to the 
court moving into the building as funding was not available . Motion to approve carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 Mayor Orr opened a Public Hearing to determine whether compliance of a nnexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exists for all of Tracts 31 and 32 and that 
portion of James Drive intervening; and a portion of Tract 39, Hill Heights Addition; and adjacent 
portions of Gysel Place and Van Buren Ave.; situated i n Section 23, Township 14 North, Range 
66 West of the 6 th P.M., County of Laramie, State of Wyoming (located east of and adjacent to 
Van Buren Avenue, north of Dell Range Boulevard).   Eric Hammer, Planning and Development 
office, provided information on th e property requested by the landowner to be annexed , and 
reviewed requirements of and compliance with annexation conditions contained in state statutes.   
Upon inquiry, Mr. Hammer advised there have been several phone calls received from individuals 
requesting information  on the proposed annexation  but no formal comment filings have been 
submitted.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the public hearing having 
been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion 
of Section 9, all of the W½ Section 14, all of Section 15, a portion of Section 16, and all of Section 
21; Township 13 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located to  
the southeast of the intersection of E. College Drive and Avenue C) (postponed from 12/20/2017).  
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. Cook.  Mr. Roybal moved to postpone to January 22, 2018 at the request of staff, 
seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. 
  
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Sections 1.1.6 and 2.4.1 of the Cheyenn e 
Unified Development Code (UDC),  amending Section 1.4.4.f., Industrial Use definition, of the 
UDC to clarify what land uses are subject to the Moderate Industrial and the Industrial use 
categories.  Susanna Montana, Planning and Development Department,  provided information in 
response to comments made and questions raised during the December 20, 2017 City Council and 
January 3, 2018  Public Services Committee meeting s, stating that concerns over where 
impoundment yards would be allowed have been further clarified in a proposed substitute  
ordinance dated January 8, 2018 .  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on thir d and final  reading, 
seconded by Mr. Case.  Mr. Roybal moved to amend by substitute dated January 2, 2018, seconded 
by Mr. Case.  Motion to amend failed. Voting “no” – all members of the governing body.  Mr. 
Roybal moved to amend by substitute dated January 8, 2018, seconded by Mr. Cook. Upon inquiry, 
Ms. Montana explained the zoning category for downtown is CBD (Central Business District) and 
that impoundment lots would not be allowed in that category, and clarified where changes would 
be made in UDC sections regarding the new wording relating to impoundment yards. Motion to 
amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main m otion as amended 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4214) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, all of 
Tracts 31 and 32 and that portion of James Drive intervening; and a portion of Tract 39, Hill 
Heights Addition; and adjacent portions of Gysel Place and Van Buren Ave.; situated in Section 
23, Township 14 North, Range 66 West, of the 6th P.M., County of Laramie, State of Wyoming 
(located east of and adjacent to Van Buren Avenue, north of Dell Range Boulevard – referred to 
Public Services Committee.  (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from County AR Agricultural Residential 
to MR Medium-density Residential and MUB Mixed-use Business Emphasis for all of Tracts 31 
and 32; and a portion of Tract 39, Hill Heights Addition; situated in Section 23, Township 14  
North, Range 66 West, of the 6th P.M., County of Laramie, State of Wyoming (located east of and 
adjacent to Van Buren Avenue,  north of Dell Range Boulevard) – referred to Public Services 
Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Vacating the 16 -foot alley situated in Block 329, 
Original City, and Federal Court located in Block 329, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Vacating that portion of the 16-foot alley right-of-way 
lying east of the Burlington Northern Railroad Spur Track right-of-way in Block 412, adjacent to 
Lots 1 thru 8, and 16 thru 22, Block 412, Original City, City of Ch eyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Pursuant to Sections 1.1.6 and 2.4.1 of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code (UDC), amending Sections 1.4.4.a and c, Section 5.1, Table 5 -1, 
Section 5.7.5, and Table 7-10 of the UDC to rename and clarify the definition for group dwelling, 
to combine and clarify the definitions for adult care – assisted living and adult care – nursing home 
land uses, combining assisted living facilities, nursing homes and group housing for specific use 
standards, clarifying where assisted living/nursing homes would be permitted, and adding 
residential and nonresidential zoning districts where group housing would be allowed as a 
conditional use – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Designating local banks as depositories for municipal funds.  Reporting 
for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. White moved to 
amend by substitute dated December 20, 2017, seconded by Mr.  Luna. Motion to amend carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended by substitute carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#5903) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Supporting the City of Cheyenne’s continuing participation in the Stat e 
Small Business Credit Initiative (‘SSBCI’) and Wyoming Smart Capital Network (‘WSCN’) 
Program; approving program changes through an amendment to the services agreement, including 
geographic boundaries, board, and the Credit Guaranty and Seed Capital Netw ork Program, and 
retaining the majority of the original ‘SSBCI’ rules as modified by the ‘WSCN’.   Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. (#5904) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for 
Pershing Marketplace, 2nd Filing, a replat of all of Lot 1, Block 1, Pershing Marketplace, 1st 
Filing, situated in Section 34, T.14N., R.66W., 6th P.M., City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located at the southwest corner of the intersection of N. College Drive and E. Pershing

[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat for 
Cheyenne Hills Church Subdivision, 2nd Filing, a replat of a portion of Lot 1 Block 1 of Cheyenne 
Hills Church Subdivision being situated in a portion of the N½ NE¼ of Section 25, Township 14

of and adjacent to US Highwa y 30, west of Christensen Roa d) – referred to Public Services 
Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat for 
Treadway Subdivision, a replat of Tracts 1, 2, 3 & 4, Ultrea Subdivision, situated in Section 8, 
T.13N., R.66W., 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located south of and adjacent to W. Allison 
Road, east of Walterscheid Boulevard) – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. 
Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to execute State Historic 
Preservation Office Certified Local Government (CLG) grant agree ments for three 2018 CLG 
grants – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Announcing the City of Cheyenne’s intent to expend funds to enhance 
the Depot Plaza by constructing a water feature for economic development purposes, to include a 
historically important statue – referred to Finance committee. (Sponsors – Mr. Case, Mr. Laybourn 
and Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Adopting the Wyoming Region 7 Hazard Mitigation Plan for the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] County Preliminary Plat of Hawk’s Landing Sub division, a replat of a portion of Capitol 
Tracts, Tract 8 being situated in a portion of the NE¼ NW¼, Section 18, T13N, R66W, of the 6th 
Principal Meridian, Laramie County, Wyoming (located west of and adjacent to York Avenue, 
south of College Drive) – referred to Public Services Committee. 
  
 [CA] Service Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Community Recreation and Events 
Department, and Wyoming Beverage, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for non -alcoholic beverage 
concessions for the Ice and Events Center.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to 
exceed $50,439.00 annually). (#6774) 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Police Department, and 
Top-Flight Maintenance, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the provision of cleaning services fo r th e 
Cheyenne Public Safety Center.  Motion (pe r Consent Agenda) carried  (amount not to exceed 
$4,203.25 per month). (#6775) 
 
[CA] Cooperative Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Wyoming Department of 
Transportation for epoxy striping (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Youth Alternatives Division, and State of 
Wyoming, Department of Family Services, for kinship advocacy servic es for displaced, at -risk 
youth – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-12-18 for furnishing transit vehicle wraps for the Transit Division, 
and authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to execute a Professional Services Agreement between 
the City of Cheyenne, Transit Division, an d Wyoming Signs, LLC, Mills, Wyoming, in 
conjunction with the bid – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -15-18 for furnishing one new in -stock 71-passenger bus for the 
Community Recreation and Events Department, Latchkey Division (2 015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – 
referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of RFP #13 -18 to provide a play system and safety surfacing in Holliday 
Park, and authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to execute a Professional Services Agreement 
between the City of Cheyenne, Community Recreation and Events Department, and Great Western 
Park and Playground, Inc., Wellsville, Utah, in conjunction with the RFP (Community 
Development Block Grant funds) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification No. 2 to Contract No. 6552 between the City of Cheyenne and HDR, 
Inc., Cheyenne, Wyoming, for additional design modifications, adjustment to construction 
engineering services, and any required additional services for the Prairie Avenue and Frontier Mall 
Intersection Improvements Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 6066 between the City of Cheyenne and 
BenchMark Engineers, P.C., Cheyenne, Wyoming, for services in connection with the Christensen 
Road Overpass and Corridor Project (1% Specific Pu rpose Sales and Use Tax) – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
 
 Announcement was made that due to the Martin Luther King Holiday on Monday, January 
15, 2018, regularly scheduled City Council Committee meetings will instead be held in the 
Municipal Building, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Room 104, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as follows:  Finance 
Committee will meet Tuesday, January 16, 2018 at 11:00 a.m. and Public Services Committee will 
meet Wednesday, January 17, 2018 at Noon. 
 
 Under other business: 
 
 Shawn Alexander express ed concern over burglar alarm  permitting and false alarm fees,  
and requested review of false alarm provisions. 
 
 Upon request by Mr. Johnson regarding status of the vacant Greenway Coordinator staff 
position, Mayor Orr explained the position will be opened for applications soon. 
 
 Mr. Johnson inquired as to the available number of cemetery spaces in City-owned 
cemeteries.  Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, advised she would research with Cemetery grounds staff 
and provide information to the governing body. 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:44 p.m. 
 
        Submitted by, 
 
             
        Carol Intlekofer, MMC 
        City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2018-01-22 | CLIP_ID: 637 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 637

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
January 22, 2018 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Ca se, Bryan Cook,  Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Scott 
Roybal, Dr. Mark Rinne, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, 
and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Cook and Mr. White, Agenda Item # 18 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr.  
Roybal moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of  the Governing Body on January 8, 2018.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Roybal declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Cowboy 
Dodge, Inc.) and vacated Council Chambers.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve payment of vouche rs 
as presented, seconded by  Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Roybal returned to Council Chambers. 
 
 Mayor Orr opened a Public Hearing to receive public comment regarding the intent of the 
City of Cheyenne to submit an application for funding to the Wyoming Business Council’s 
Business Ready Community, Community Readiness Grant Program, to assist with funding the F.E. 
Warren Air Force Base Enhanced Use Lease Infrastructure Project.  Robert Briggs, Planning and 
Development Director, provided information on the Enhanced Use Lease program and Project 
generally.  He discussed proposed residential and commercial development that is planned for the 
property; need for more affordable local military housing; Project water and sewer development 
needs and Business Council’s program involving a grant and loan to assist with funding; economic 
development benefitting local sales tax revenue, and the opportunity to partner with private 
developers and the military in enhancing the area as a community gateway .  Dale Steenburgen, 
Executive Director, Greater Cheyenne Chamber of Commerce, spoke in support of the application 
and Project generally.  He noted the need for affordable housing for members of the Air  Force in 
Cheyenne, benefits of the Enhanced Use Lease program to the Air Force and community, and that 
the property location is a desirable area for growth.  There being no further comments or input and 
purpose of the public hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion 
of Section 9, all of the W½ Section 14, all of Section 15, a portion of Section 16, and all of Section 
21; Township 13 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located to 
the southeast of the intersection of E. College Drive and Avenue C) (postponed from 1/08/2018).  
Casey Palma, Steil Surveying, agent for the developer, explained they have been working 
diligently with the City on the annexation agreement to make sure all areas are addressed and 
expressed appreciation for the Governing Body’s patience relative to the numerous ordinance 
postponements.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third 
and final reading, seconded by Mr. Case.  Mr. Roybal moved to postpone to February 12, 2018 at 
the request of staff, seconded by Mr. Case.   Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. 
  
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, all of 
Tracts 31 and 32 and that portion of James Drive intervening; and a portion of Tract  39, Hill 
Heights Addition; and adjacent portions of Gysel Place and Van Buren Ave.; situated in Section 
23, Township 14 North, Range 66 West, of the 6th P.M., County of Laramie, State of Wyoming 
(located east of and adjacent to Van Buren Avenue, north of Dell Range Boulevard.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to approve on second reading with staff conditions #1  through 3, seconded by Mr. Case.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
 
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning  Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from County AR Agricultural Residential 
to MR Medium-density Residential and MUB Mixed-use Business Emphasis for all of Tracts 31 
and 32; and a portion of Tract 39, Hill Heights Addition; situated in Section 23, Township 14 
North, Range 66 West, of the 6th P.M., County of Laramie, State of Wyoming (located east of and 
adjacent to Van Buren Av enue, north of Dell Range Boulevard).  Mr. Roybal moved to approve 
on second reading, seconded by Mr.  Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. 
 
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Vacating the 16 -foot alley situated in Block 329, 
Original City, and Federal Court located in Block 329, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr . Case.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
 
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Vacating that portion of the 16 -foot alley right-of-way 
lying east of the Burlington Northern Railroad Spur Track right-of-way in Block 412, adjacent to 
Lots 1 thru 8, and 16 thru 22, Block 412, Original City, City of Ch eyenne, La ramie County, 
Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr . Case.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Sections 1.1.6 and 2.4.1 of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development (UDC), amendi ng Sections 1.4.4.a and c, Section 5.1, Table 5 -1, Section 
5.7.5, and Table 7 -10 of the UDC to rename and clarify the definition for group dwelling, to 
combine and clarify the definitions for adult care – assisted living and adult care – nursing home 
land uses, combining assisted living facilities, nursing homes and group housing for specific use 
standards, clarifying where assisted living/nursing homes would be permitted, and adding 
residential and nonresidential zoning districts where group housing would be allowed as a 
conditional use.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr . Laybourn.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Rinne 
and Mr. Cook voting “no”. 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for 
Pershing Marketplace, 2nd Filing, a replat of all of Lot 1, Block 1, Pershing Marketplace, 1st 
Filing, situated in Section 34, T.14N., R.66W., 6th P.M., City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located at the southwest corner of the intersection of N. College Drive and E. Pershing 
Boulevard).  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt with staff conditions #1 and 2, seconded by Mr. White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#5905) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat for 
Cheyenne Hills Church Subdivision, 2nd Filing, a replat of a portion of Lot 1 Block 1 of Cheyenne 
Hills Church Subdivision being situated in a portion of the N½ NE¼ of Section 25, Township 14 
North, Range 66 West, of the Sixth Principal Meridian, Laramie County, Wyoming (located south 
of and adjacent to US Highwa y 30, west of Christensen Road).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#5906) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat for 
Treadway Subdivision, a replat of Tracts 1, 2, 3 & 4, Ultrea Subdivision, situated in Section 8, 
T.13N., R.66W., 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located south of and adjacent to W. Allison 
Road, east of Walterscheid Boulevard).  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt with revised plat map dated 
January 17, 2018, seconded by Mr. Laybourn.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. (#5907) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to execute State Historic 
Preservation Office Certified Local Government (CLG) grant agree ments for three 2018 CLG 
grants.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (Res#5908; Cont#6780-6782) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Announcing the City of Cheyenne’ s intent to expend funds to enhance 
the Depot Plaza by constructing a water feature for economic development purposes, to include a 
historically important statue .  Darren Rudloff, Visit Cheyenne, provided information on the 
Downtown Cheyenne Core Plan presented a couple of years ago; support for the water feature by 
local economic development groups, and prior Governing Body approval of the Core Plan.  
Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White  moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. 
Laybourn moved to postpone to February 12, 2018 to allow for further information to be received, 
seconded by Mr. Cook.  Discussion followed on benefits of the spray park as a Depot Plaza 
enhancement; benefits involving tourism, community attraction and downtown atmosphere; 
following through with plans for projects that have been previously approved; proposed bronze 
statue in conjunction with the project, and project funding.  Concern was expressed that the 
postponement timeframe may not be adequate, and with the estimated funding expense relative to 
other larger infrastructure projects in the works that are unfunded.  Motion to postpone carried .  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Shanor and and Mr. 
Luna voting “no”.  
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Adopting the Wyoming Region 7 Hazard Mitigation Plan for the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5909) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for 
Whitney Ranch, 1st Filing, a replat of all of Tracts 31 and 32 and that portion of James Drive 
intervening; and a portion of Tract 39, Hill Heights Addition, situated in Section 23, Township 14 
North, Range 66 West, of the 6th P.M., City of Cheyenne, County of Laramie, State of Wyoming 
(located east of and adjacent to Van Buren Avenue, north of Dell Range Boulevard) – referred to

[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the submission of an application to the Wyoming Business

Readiness Grant, and up to $3,000,000.00 in Business Ready Community loan funding,  for the

[CA] RESOLUTION – Re-appropriating the Fiscal Year 2018 budget of the City of Cheyenne

(Sponsor - Mr. White) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Supporting the appropriation of state funding for Wyoming cities and

[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Cheyenne Police Department to apply for Office on

 County Preliminary Plat of Hawk’s Landing Subdivision, a replat of a portion of Capitol 
Tracts, Tract 8 being situated in a portion of the NE¼ NW¼, Section 18, T13N, R66W, of the 6th 
Principal Meridian, Laramie County, Wyoming (located west of and adjacent to York Avenue, 
south of Coll ege Drive).  Mr. Roybal moved to acknowledge the Planning Commission’s 
recommendation to approve with staff conditions #1  through 4, seconded by Mr.  Case.  Motion

[CA] Cooperative Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Wyoming Department of

[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Youth Alternat ives Division, and State of

[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and SAA Enterprises – Steve Avila, for the

[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and HDR Engineering,

[CA] Sweetgrass PUD (Planned Unit Development) annexation - Master Annexation Agreement

[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Novo Benefits, LLC,

[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-12-18 for furnishing transit vehicle wraps for the Transit Division,

(#6779) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -15-18 for furnishing one new in -stock 71-passenger bus for the

Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accept bid submitted by Lewis Bus Group, North Salt Lake,

BenchMark Engineers, P.C., Cheyenne, Wyoming, for services in connection with the Christensen

Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $27,500.00). (#6066)

Design Corporation, Denver, Colorado, for additional groundwater sampling and analysis for West 
Edge Engineering Design Services / Civic Center Commons Project (FEMA Pre -Disaster 
Mitigation Grant funds) – referred to Finance Committee.

authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to execute a Professional Services Agreement between the 
City of Cheyenne, Transit Division, and Genfare, Elk Grove Village, Illinois, in conjunction with 
the purchase – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Under other business,  Mr. Case referenced Agenda Item #28(f) , P rofessional Services 
Agreement with NOVO Benefits, and encouraged attendance by interested partie s at the next 
Finance Committee meeting to receive information on proposed services to be provided by NOVO.  
He commented on service areas in which he had concerns or questions.  Pursuant to reference of 
the next meeting of the Finance Committee, Sylvia Ha ckl, City Attorney, confirmed the meeting 
was scheduled for Monday, February 5 at 11:00 a.m. 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:35 p.m. 
 
        Submitted by, 
 
             
        Carol Intlekofer, MMC

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2018-02-12 | CLIP_ID: 639 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 639

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 12, 2018 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.   Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Ca se, Bryan Cook,  Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Scott 
Roybal, Dr. Mark Rinne, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, 
and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on t he agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. White, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Cook and Mr. Roybal, Agenda Items #16 and 17 were removed from the Consent Agenda.  
Mr. Shanor stated he would abstain from voting participation on the Consent Agenda due to a 
conflict of interest involving the items being removed (business relationship involving the State 
Loan and Investment Board), and vacated Council Chambers.  Remaining  items were approved.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  present in Council Chambers.  Following 
announcement of the vote, Mr. Shanor returned to Chambers. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 22, 2018.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Cook and Mr. Roybal each declared a conflict of interest (voucher payable to Mr. Cook 
personally and business interest in voucher payable to Cowboy Dodge, Inc.  respectively) and 
vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouche rs as presented, 
seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present in Council Chambers .  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Cook and Mr. Roybal 
returned to Chambers. 
 
 Mr. Shanor moved to suspend the rules by bringing forward Agenda Item #24 (c) before 
Agenda Item #7 to allow for consideration of the Sweetgrass Master Annexation Agreement prior 
to the affiliated Sweetgrass Annexation Ordinance on third and final reading, seconded by Dr. 
Rinne.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
Sweetgrass PUD (Planned Unit Development) annexation - Master Annexation Agreement 
between the City of Cheyenne and Sweetgrass Land Co., LLC; Lummis Livestock Company, LLC, 
and Arp and Hammond Hardware Company, for an area commonly known as the Sweetgrass 
Annexation, being a portion of Sections 8 and 9, all of the W½ Section 14, a portion of the E½ E½ 
of Section 17, and all of Sections 15, 16, and 21; Township 13 No rth, Range 66 West of the 6t h 
P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Roybal moved 
to approve, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. Roybal moved to amend by substitute agreement dated 
January 31, 2018, seconded by Mr. Case.  Moti on carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. (#6784) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion 
of Section 9, all of the W½ Section 14, all of Section 15, a portion of Section 16, and all of Section 
21; Township 13 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located to 
the southeast of the intersection of E. College Drive and Avenue C) (postponed from 1/22/2018).    
Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Case.  Comment was 
made in support of development plans for the property .  Motion carried.   Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#4215) 
  
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, all of 
Tracts 31 and 32 and that portion of James Drive intervening; and a portion of Tract 39, Hill 
Heights Addition; and adjacent portions of Gysel Place and Van Buren Ave.; situated in Section 
23, Township 14 North, Range 66 West, of the 6th P.M., County of Laramie, State of Wyoming 
(located east of and adjacent to Van Buren Avenue, north of Dell Range Boulevard.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body. (#4216) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from County AR Agricultural Residential 
to MR Medium-density Residential and MUB Mixed-use Business Emphasis for all of Tracts 31 
and 32; and a portion of Tract 39, Hill Heights Addition; situated in Section 23, Townsh ip 14 
North, Range 66 West, of the 6th P.M., County of Laramie, State of Wyoming (located east of and 
adjacent to Van Buren Avenue,  north of Dell Range Boulevard).  Mr. Roybal moved to approve 
on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body. (#4217) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Vacating the 16 -foot alley situated in Block 329, 
Original City, and Federal Court located in Block 329, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal moved  to approve on third and final  reading, seconded by Mr . Case .  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4218) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Vacating that portion of the 16 -foot alley right-of-way 
lying east of the Burlington Northern Railroad Spur Track right-of-way in Block 412, adjacent to 
Lots 1 thru 8, and 16 thru 22, Block 412, Original City, City of Ch eyenne, La ramie County, 
Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr . Johnson.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4219) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Sections 1.1.6 and 2.4.1 of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development (UDC), amending Sections 1.4.4.a and c, Section 5.1, Table 5 -1, Section 
5.7.5, and Ta ble 7 -10 of the UDC to rename and clarify the definition for group dwelling, to 
combine and clarify the definitions for adult care – assisted living and adult care – nursing home 
land uses, combining assisted living facilities, nursing homes and group hous ing for specific use 
standards, clarifying where assisted living/nursing homes would be permitted, and adding 
residential and nonresidential zoning districts where group housing would be allowed as a 
conditional use.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Rinne 
voting “no”. (#4220) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Section 10.52.200, Recreational vehicle, 
trailer-defined – Parking permitted in certain places, of Chapter 10.52, Stopping, Standing and 
Parking Generally, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Announcing the City of Cheyenne’s intent to expend funds to enhance 
the Depot Plaza by constructing a water feature for economic development purposes, to include a 
historically important statue.  Darren Rudloff, Visit Cheyenne, spoke in support of the water splash 
feature and provided information and reasons for support by four local economic development 
groups, who noted the feature was included in the Downtown Cheyenne Core Plan presented to 
the governing body a couple of years ago.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White  moved 
to adopt, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. Laybourn moved to amend by substitute, seconded by Mr. 
Case.  Information on changes contained within the substitute resolution were provided by Mr. 
Shanor, noting the statue feature may be a future project but not specifically included at this time 
and that, due to other City project funding needs, funding for the project would be a public/private 
partnership.  He stated the substitute changes (reduces) the City’s funding commitment amount to 
$250,000.00 with anticipation that private donations of at least $250,000.00 would be received by 
a sunset date to help with the splash pad  costs.  Mr. Shanor confirmed the City’s obligation to 
expend its funds is conditional on $250,0 00.00 being privately raised before the sunset date of 
June 1, 2019.  Discussion included the sunset date and potential option of amending the resolution 
at that time; future maintenance , costs  and staff expertise involving the water feature; seeking 
information from other communities who have a similar feature regarding operational and 
maintenance costs; options involving project cost; providing a quality water feature, and record 
keeping regarding monies that may be donated.  During discussion, Mayor Orr briefly left the 
meeting with Council President Rinne assuming duties of the chair; duties were returned to Mayor 
Orr prior to a vote on the substitute resolution.  Upon inquiry, Teresa Moore, Director of 
Community Recreation and Events, advised an estimate of maintenance costs is pre-mature at this 
time as a design for the feature needs to be prepared and reviewed.  Motion to amend by substitute 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson and 
Mayor Orr votin g “no”.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson and Mayor Orr voting “no”. (#5910) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for 
Whitney Ranch, 1st Filing, a replat of all of Tracts 31 and 32 and that portion of James Drive 
intervening; and a portion of Tract 39, Hill Heights Addition, situated in Section 23, Township 14 
North, Range 66 West, of the 6th P.M., City of Cheyenne, County of Laramie, State of Wyoming 
(located east of and adjacent to Van Buren Avenue, north of Dell Range Boulevard).  Mr. Roybal 
moved to adopt with staff conditions #1 through 4, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. (#5911) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the submission of an application to the Wyoming Business 
Council Business Ready Community Grant and Loan Program for a $3,000,000.00 Community 
Readiness Grant, and up to $3,000,000.00 in Business Ready Community loan funding,  for the 
Happy Jack Road/F.E. Warren Air Force Base Enhanced Use Lease Infrastructure Project .  Dale 
Steenbergen, Greater Cheyenne Chamber of Commerce, spoke in support of the application, citing 
the opportunity for the City to address the lack of affordab le military housing in the community 
through the Project , and commenting that numerous military members are commuting from 
northern Colorado due to the housing shortage.  Donn Edmunds commented that individuals 
perhaps preferred living in Colorado.  Mr. White moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. 
White moved to amend by substitute  dated February 8, 2018 , seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Robert 
Briggs, Planning and Development Director, provided information on the changes contained in the 
substitute resolution, which includes wording on improvements to the public rights -of-way.  Mr. 
Shanor stated he would abstain from voting participation on the agenda item due to a conflict of 
interest (business relationship involving the State Loan and Investment Board), and  vacated 
Council Chambers.  Discussion included emergency service providers to the Project area and local 
housing / rental rates.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present in Council Chambers.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. 
Shanor returned to Chambers. (#5912) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the submission of a loan application to the Wyoming 
Business Council Business Ready Community Grant and Loan Program, providing for repayment 
and affirming compliance with applicable state laws and regulations, for a loan in an amount not 
to exceed $3,000,000.00 for the Happy Jack Road/F.E. Warren Air Force Base En hanced Use 
Lease Infrastructure Project .  Dale Steenbergen, Greater Cheyenne Chamber of Commerce, 
provided information in response to inquiry raised on the previous resolution regarding housing 
rates.  He advised the governing body may be able to assist wi th determining affordable housing 
rates as part of the communication between the City and F.E. Warren.  Mr. White moved to adopt, 
seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. White moved to amend by substitute dated February 8, 2018, seconded 
by Mr. Johnson.  Mr. Shanor stated he would abstain from voting participation on the agenda item 
due to a conflict of interest (business relationship involving the State Loan and Investment Board), 
and vacated Council Chambers.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Steenbergen provided information on the lack 
of local military housing ; estimated number of military personnel residing outside of the 
community, and market analysis that had been conducted on the topic.  Upon further query, Mr. 
Steenbergen advised an estimated 300 to 350 military housing units may be provided as part of the 
Project and, if military personnel do not require all of the units , some units may be available to 
local residents.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present in Council Chambers.  Mai n motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. 
Shanor returned to Chambers. (#5913) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of a Federal Mineral Royalty  Capital 
Construction Account Grant application to the State Loan and Investment Board on behalf of the 
Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne to purchase cameras for Cheyenne’s police cars, police 
body cameras, micro cameras and necessary support items.  Chief Brian Kozak, Cheyenne Police 
Department, provided information on :  Proposed implementation of a video and body camera 
program; research on the topic  and proposed equipment; state legislative action resulting in state 
laws addressing privacy and public records; internal committee that was formed to review the use 
of video and body cameras, and program benefits.  He noted a policy has been developed, reviewed 
and approved by outside interested parties and that the grant is a 50/50 match with the City’s 
funding portion coming from proceeds from the sale of the former Police Department building.  
Mr. White moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. Shanor stated he would abstain from 
voting participation on the agenda item due to a conflict of interest (business relationship involving 
the State Loan and Investment Board), and vacated Council Chambers.  Upon inquiry, Chief Kozak 
advised the City of Laramie has utilized a similar program for about three years.  Comment was 
made in opposition to the program.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present in Council Chambers with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  Following 
announcement of the vote, Mr. Shanor returned to Chambers. (#5913)   
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Re-appropriating the Fiscal Year 2018 budget of the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W.S. §16-4-112 & 16-4-113.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
(#5915) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Supporting the appropriation of state funding for Wyoming cities and 
towns during the 2018 budget session of the Wyoming Legislature.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#5916) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Cheyenne Police Department to apply for Office on 
Violence Against Women OVS -2018-13827 improving crimi nal justice respon ses to sexual 
assault, domestic violence, dating violence and stalking program (formally known as the grants to 
encourage arrest policies and enforcemen t of protection orders program).   Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. (#5917) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Supporting the Continuing Education Program offered by the Wyoming 
Association of Municipalities and Local Technical Services a/k/a Leadership Training Services, 
and authorizing the execution of an amended Contract for Services regarding the provision of such 
services – referred to Finance Committee (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] Sweetgrass 1st Filing Preliminary Plat Subdivision consisting of unplatted lands situated 
in the NW¼ of Section 16 Township 13 North, Range 66 West, 6th P.M., City of Cheyenne, 
Laramie County, Wyoming – referred to Public Services Committee. 
 
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and SAA Enterprises – Steve Avila, for the 
purpose of sponsoring the CABA (Continental Amateur Baseball Association) 18U Wood Bat 
World Series during the sum mer of 2018.   Jay Folks and Rick Thompson, Cheyenne Mustangs 
Baseball, spoke in opposition to the proposed agreement and provided information on past 
experience the Mustangs have had involving a contract between the Mustangs and Mr. Avila and 
his company relative to the 2017 CABA tournament held in Cheyenne  last summer .  Their 
discussion topics included contractual provisions they felt were not fulfilled, and dissatisfaction 
with the services that had been provided.  Joe Dougherty questioned several sectio ns of the 
proposed contract for clarification, including contract topics involving event cost s; registration 
fees; team participants; proposed credit arrangement outlined in the agreement, and services to be 
provided.  Jason Sanchez, Assistant Director, Co mmunity Recreation and Events, summarized a 
brief history of last year’s CABA event involving a contract between the City and Cheyenne 
Mustangs, and subsequent communication from Mr. Avila offering to provide future tournament 
services through contract with the City.  Mr. Sanchez also provided information in response to Mr. 
Dougherty’s questions.  Upon inquiry, Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney, advised details included in the 
proposed agreement were received from Mr. Sanchez and Mr. Avila, and noted there is no contract 
to force specific performance.  Mr. White reported the motion to approve the item failed at Finance 
Committee, therefore, there was no recommendation from Committee.  Mr. Case moved to 
approve, seconded by Mr. White.  Lengthy discussion followed and included the following topics:  
Lack of teams anticipated to participate in last summer’s tournament and resulting reduced event 
revenue; contract provisions that were not met; supporting youth baseball in the community; up -
front money expended by the C ity and concern with the financial “credit” proposal(s) as a pay -
back provision contained in the proposed agreement, which would not be “real money”; reputation 
of SAA Enterprises and Mr. Avila , and current status of the company; registration and affiliate d 
fees; splitting of event revenue and the amount of revenue threshold that would need to be realized 
before any real dollars would be received by the City.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Thompson confirmed 
the Mustangs’ contract with Mr. Avila was still in effect  and advised they were unaware until 
recently that Mr. Avila had contacted the City proposing a separate agreement to provide future 
CABA tournaments.  Motion to approve failed.  Voting “no” – all members of the governing body. 
 
Following a brief recess, Mayor Orr reconvened the meeting at 8:15 p.m. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and HDR Engineering, 
Inc., Cheyenne, Wyoming, for construction management services in support of the Christensen 
Road Overpass and Corridor Project (1% Specific Purpose Sales Tax) .  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $772,767.00). (#6785) 
 
 Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Novo Benefits, LLC, 
for employee benefits co nsulting and brokerage services .  Denise Freeman, Director of Human 
Resources, provided a brief history of last year’s change in the City’s employee health plan and 
involvement by the consultant, noting benefits of having an outside professional firm assist with 
evaluating and negotiati ng the City’s plan.  She advised an Employee Benefits Committee has 
been formed; the RFP process was followed in seeking professional services proposals, and that 
the Committee is recommending Novo Benefits.  Mr. White moved to approve in an amount not 
to exceed $208,500.00, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Upon inquiry, Diana Madvig, Novo Benefits, 
provided information on cost savings the company achieved for another customer.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing with the exception of Mr. Cook and Dr. Rinne voting 
“no”. (#6786) 
 
[CA] Temporary Construction Easement Agreement with George M. Zak and Leslie S. Zak, for 
the deposit of spoil dirt in conjunction with the Christensen Road Railroad Overpass and Corridor 
Project (1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] City of Cheyenne City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, Christensen Road Right-of-Way Purchase 
Agreement with Holly L. Bruegman (1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax)  – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-13-18 for furnishing one new mobile observation platform for the 
Cheyenne Police Department (Homeland Security Grant) .  Lieutenant Terrance Bell, Cheyenne 
Police Department, provided a brief background on the grant and purpose of ac quiring the 
equipment, and described platform features .  Upon inquiry, Lt. Bell confirmed only one bid had 
been received.  Mr. White moved to accept the bid submitted by Industrial Video and Control of 
Greenville, SC, in an amount not to exceed $140,000.00, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-7-18 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Public Works 
Department, and W.L. Contractors, Inc., Arvada, Colorado, for the 2018 Traffic Signal Installation 
Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-9-18 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Planning and 
Development Department, and American Contracting of Wyoming, LLC, Cheyenne, W yoming, 
for the Warren Rest House Restoration, Phase 2, Project – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S -8-18 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, 1% 
Construction Division, and Reiman  Corp. and STC Construction Co., Inc., a Joint Venture, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the FY 2018 Miscellaneous Concrete Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) 
– referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 
 [CA] Contract Modification No. 2 to Contract No. 6498 between the City of Cheyenne and DHM 
Design Corporation, Denver, Colorado, for additional groundwater sampling and analysis for West 
Edge Engineering Design Services / Civic Center Commons Project (FEMA Pre -Disaster 
Mitigation Grant funds).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6498) 
 
[CA] Contract Renewal No. 1 to Contract No. 5980 between the City of Cheyenne and Warren 
Federal Credit Union for limited public right -of-way use for fiber optic communications system 
installation – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Renewal No. 1 to Contract No. 6292 between the City of Cheyenne and the Girl 
Scouts of Montana and Wyoming for the lease of 45.93 acres of City -owned real property – 
referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modific ation No. 1 to Contract No. 6625 between the City of Cheyenne and 
Western Research and Development, Ltd., for additional design services for the FY 2017 -18 On-
Call Traffic Engineering Services Project (2015 -2018 1% Sales Tax)  – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
 Appointment of Amy M. Allen as City Engineer for the City of Cheyenne.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr  who did not vote (Mayoral ap pointee).  Mayor 
Orr announced the oath of office for Ms. Allen would be administered by Municipal Court Judge 
Mark Moran the next morning, February 13th, at 9:00 a.m. in the Mayor’s Conference Room, and 
Council members, staff and the public were welcome to attend. 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase Odyssey fareboxes for the Cheyenne Transit Division, and 
authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to execute a Professional Services Agreement between the 
City of Cheyenne, Transit Division, and Genfare, Elk Grove Village, Illinois, in conjunction with 
the purchase.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $205,777.20). 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase , by Community Recreation and Events Department, a new 
Montrose with screen restroom building for Myler Park from CXT, Inc., Spokane Valley, 
Washington (CDBG grant funds) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be hel d Monday, February 26, 2018 at 
6:00 p.m. in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for renewal 
applications for existing City liquor licenses and permits (retail, restaurant, limited retail, resort, 
bar and grill licenses and microbrewery and satellite distillery permits) filed with the City Clerk’s 
office. 
 
Announcement was made that due to the Presidents’ Day Holiday on Monday, February 
19, 2018, regularly scheduled City Council Committee meetings will instead be held in the 
Municipal Building, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Room 104, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as follows:  Finance 
Committee will meet Tuesday, February 20, 2018 at 11:00 a.m. and Public Services Committee 
will meet Wednesday, February 21, 2018 at Noon. 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 8:28 p.m. 
 
        Submitted by, 
 
             
        Carol Intlekofer, MMC 
        City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2018-02-26 | CLIP_ID: 642 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 642

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 26, 2018 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.   Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Scott Roybal, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Absent – Bryan Cook.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City 
Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  Mayor Orr recognized members of Boy Scout Troop #150 
who were present at the meeting and who led the pledge of allegiance. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Luna moved to approve the Consent Agenda, seconded by 
Mr. Johnson.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 12, 2018.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on February 16, 2018.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
 Mayor Orr opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for renewal applications 
for existing City liquor licenses and permits (retail, restaurant, limited retail, resort, bar and grill 
licenses and microbrewery and satellite distillery permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office.  There 
being no comments or input and purpose of the public hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed 
the hearing and referred the applications to Finance Committee. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Section 10.52.200, Recreational vehic le,

Wyoming.  Mr. Case, as sponsor of the ordinance, requested the it em be withdrawn from the 
agenda, citing the need to further review the topic.  Following query to members of the governing 
body and with no objection being voiced, Mayor Orr announced the item was being withdrawn 
from the agenda.

[CA] City of Cheyenne City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, Christensen Road Right-of-Way Purchase 
Agreement with Holly L. Bruegman (1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax) .  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $14,131.00). (#6792) 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between Laramie County, City of Cheyenne and the Board 
of Public Utilities for the purpose of purchasing a small unmanned aerial system and necessary 
software through the Cheyenne/Laramie County Geographic Information Systems Co operative, 
and providing rules for governing the operation of the system – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Trihydro Corporation,

fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Purchase Agreement and Settlement and Release of All Claims between the City of 
Cheyenne and Mary Rose Ramirez for acquisition of real property located at 2247 East 19th Street, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, in conjunction with the Pershing Boulevard/Converse Avenue/19th Street 
Roundabout Project (2007-2010 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of RFP-14-18 for the new site of the City of Cheyenne Municipal Court (1% 
Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax).  Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works, reviewed the 
history of the May 2017 1% Specific Purpose ballot question, which voters approved for funding 
the new Municipal Court project; utilization of outside consultant services, pursuant to a Request 
for Proposals (RFP) process, to temporarily relocate the Court to the Municipal Building; issuance 
of an RFP requesting proposals for a new Municipal Court site; elements contained in the RF P, 
and evaluation criteria utilized by a consultant and the City’s project team in reviewing site 
proposals.  She noted six proposals were received for the following locations:  Carey  Building 
(f/k/a Fowler’s and Z’s building) at 308 W. 17th St. (2 proposals); Hynds Building at 1602 Capitol 
Avenue; Mayflower Building at 112 West 17 th Street; Teton Building at 1803 Capitol Avenue 
(which proposal was subsequently withdrawn), and a vacant lot at 18 th Street/Bent and O’Neil 
Avenues.  She explained the criteria  (i.e. same criteria used for all RFP proposals received)  
reviewed by the project team in evaluating a site adjacent to or upon the grounds of the Municipal 
Building complex, 2101 O’Neil Avenue,  which resulted in the team not recommending the site, 
noting the site would not meet current UDC (Uniform Development Code) parking requirements 
and concerns with insufficient drainage and other site obstacles.   Ms. Nemecek stated the project 
team is recommending the proposal submitted for the Carey Building for the purchase amount of 
$275,000.00.  In response to inquiries from the governing body, Ms. Nemecek provided detailed 
information on the RFP’s that had been issued; consultant services by Noel Griffith Jr. and 
Associates; RFP process and time frames; review of proposals, and City purchasing policies.  Also 
pursuant to inquiry, Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney; Teresa Moore, Director of Community 
Recreation and Events, and Mayor Orr provided information on Municipal Building Complex and 
Ice and Events Cente r ADA compliance; need for funding an analysis of certain municipal 
buildings to compile a master renovation plan; purchase orders serving as a form of contract, and 
disclosure to the governing body of all proposals’ information not just the proposal recommended 
by the project team.  Noel Griffith, Noel Griffith Jr. and Associates, responded to queries on status 
of the Carey Building relative to water entry/damage; building structural soundness concerns, and 
contamination.  Wendy Volk, #1 Properties, advise d a proposal was submitted after the RFP 
deadline for 1920 Thomes Avenue, City Center Building site, and commented on the building’s 
square footage and parking lot availability.  Ben Trautwein, property owner representative, also 
advised a late proposal wa s submitted for 508 West 19 th Street, Pioneer Printing building site.  
Speaking in support of revitalizing the downtown were Astrid, Plains Hotel owner, and Dale 
Steenbergen, Executive Director, Greater Cheyenne Chamber of Commerce, who also expressed 
concern with finding viable public/private partnerships to fix downtown blight ed building  
problems.  Chuck West, Carey Building property owner representative, explained the two 
proposals that had been offered, one involving the Affinity Group, noting the Group  has until 
March 9th to decide whether to purchase the Carey Building.  Upon inquiry, Mr. West advised the 
property owner would be willing to provide a reasonable time frame in which to enter into a 
purchase contract with the City  if the Affinity Group did not decide to move forward with 
purchasing the building , and provided information on construction costs.  Blythe McAndrew, 
Historic Preservation Board, noted the Carey block’s historic significance and requested Board 
involvement related to historic aspects involving potential building demolition.  Joe Dougherty 
spoke in support of restoration of the Carey Building, due to its historic significance, vs. 
demolishing the building.  Jim Weaver, #1 Properties, discussed RFP content relative to no specific 
prices being required as related to a proposal, and Affinity Group’s proposal, which would include 
a “turn key” option, and suggested that additional proposals be accepted.  Expressing St. Mary’s 
school student safety and traffic concerns, the following commented in objection to the Municipal 
Court possibl y being located on property adjacent to or in the north parking lot area of the 
Municipal Building:  Joel Hickerson, Pat Lane, Nick Serkedakis, Chanda Spears, Natalie Bell and 
Marcus Crowl.  Mike Celese and Steve Borin commented in support of the Carey Building site.  
During comments, additional information was provided by Ms. Nemecek, Mr. Griffith and Mayor 
Orr.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White stated there was no recommend ation on the 
item from Committee.  Mr. Roybal moved to accept the proposal (i.e. submitted by Chuck West, 
#1 Properties, Inc. of Cheyenne, in an amount not to exceed $275,000.00), seconded by Mr. Luna.  
Discussion topics included:  RFP site review process and procedures; transparency; wording of 
ballot language; traffic; renovation of the Municipal Building; rejecting the proposal and reviewing 
additional options, and relocation of Municipal Court being a Court issue and not a downtown 
redevelopment issue.  Motion failed.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Luna, Mr. Roybal, Mr. White and Mayor 
Orr.  Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Shanor. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-7-18 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Public Works 
Department, and W.L. Contractors, Inc., Arvada, Colorado, for the 2018 Traffic Signal Installation 
Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed

[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-9-18 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Planning and 
Development Department, and American Contracting of Wyoming, LLC, Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
for the Warren Rest House Restoration, Phase 2, Project .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried 
(amount not to exceed $37,687.00). (#6794) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S -8-18 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, 1% 
Construction Division, and Reiman Corp. and STC Construction Co., Inc., a Joint Venture, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the FY 2018 Miscellaneous Concrete Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $414,087.13). (#6795) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S -6-18 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Community 
Recreation and Events Department, and Reiman Corp., Cheyenn e, Wyoming, for the Lions Park 
Pavillion Project (Land and Water Conservation grant and Parks Projects funds)  – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S -4-18 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Community 
Recreation and Events D epartment, and Borderline Fencing, Inc., Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the 
Backstop Fence Removal and Replacement at Converse Athletic Complex Project (Cheyenne 
Community Recreation District and Parks Projects funds) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Renewal No. 1 to Contract No. 5980 between the City of Cheyenne and Warren 
Federal Credit Union for limited public right -of-way use for fiber optic communications system 
installation.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (revenue to the City in the amount of $750.00). 
(#5980) 
 
[CA] Contract Renewal No. 1 to Contract No. 6292 between the City of Cheyenne and the Girl 
Scouts of Montana and Wyoming for the lease of 45.93 acres of City-owned real property.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried (revenue to the City in the amount of $200.00). (#6292) 
 
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 6625 between the City of Cheyenne and 
Western Research and Development, Ltd., for additional design services for the FY 2017 -18 On-
Call Traffic Engineering Ser vices Project (2015 -2018 1% Sales Tax) .  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $33,222.00). (#6625) 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase , by Community Recreation and Events Department, a new 
Montrose with screen restroom building for Myler  Park from CXT, Inc., Spokane Valley, 
Washington (CDBG grant funds).   Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed 
$130,326.00). 
 
 Under other business: 
 
 Steve Earnshaw, Frontier United Methodist Church, stated he was speaking on behalf of a 
concerned group of individuals regarding residents and living conditions at the mobile home park 
located at 316 Central Avenue, which is involved in the City’s Fight the Blight program.  He 
voiced support for a sustainable solution and requested the City p rovide notice on its plans 
involving code enforcement so the group could assist where possible with residents’ needs.  Input 
from Mayor Orr and City Council members was provided in response to Mr. Earnshaw’s 
comments.  Donald James Perrott Macneil commented on due process and equal protection rights, 
and Sally Meeker urged the governing body to consider the people involved in the mobile home 
situation.  Mayor Orr and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney, provided information on due process and 
the abatement process, and it was noted any future legal notices issued by the City on the matter 
could be provided to Mr. Earnshaw .  [City Clerk’s note:  During this portion of other business, 
Mayor Orr briefly left the meeting with Council President Rinne assuming duties of t he chair; 
duties were returned to Mayor Orr during comments and prior to the next item]   
 
 Mr. Laybourn complimented Mr. Case’s involvement with an abatement clean -up project 
for a property on Baldwin Drive. 
 
 Mr. Johnson inquired about recent alcohol catering permits issued for events at the Civic 
Center, and recent information on downtown parking permits as issued by the Downtown 
Development Authority.  In response, Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, provided infor mation on the 
alcohol permits, and Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department, provided information on parking 
violation warning notices that were currently being issued in the downtown area. 
 
 Mr. Case requested staff submit an update to City Council regarding status of the 
Christensen Road Project. 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 8:50 p.m. 
 
        Submitted by, 
 
             
        Carol Intlekofer, MMC 
        City Clerk
