MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2018-03-12 | CLIP_ID: 644 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 644

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
March 12, 2018 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Ca se, Bryan Cook,  Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Scott 
Roybal, Dr. Mark Rinne, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, 
and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on February 19, 2018.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 26, 2018.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Cook declared a conflict of interest (personal interest in voucher payable to Mr. Cook) 
and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouche rs as presented, 
seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Cook returned to 
Chambers. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Section 1.16.050, Off -Site improvements – 
Requirements, of Chapter 1.16, Annexation and Infrastructure Improvements, of Title 1, General 
Provisions, of the code o f the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming . Reporting for Public Services 
Committee, M r. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson. Mr. 
Roybal moved to amend Section 1.16.050 (B) Section #4 by removing the language “…  at the 
time of the construction of the off-site infrastructure improvements” and in its place inserting “… 
in the amount deposited with the City consistent with this chapter.”, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  It 
was noted if the amendment was approved the section would read:  If such refund is paid and the 
recipient is no longer the current record property owner, such person or persons shall not be 
relieved from the responsibility for the cost of the infrastructure improvements in the amount 
deposited with the City consistent with this chapter.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend Section #3 to reflect s imilar 
amendment language regarding the amount deposited  with the City, sec onded by Mr. Roybal.  
Motion to amend carried. Voting “yes” all members of the governing body.  Main motion, as twice 
amended, carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 [CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City of Cheyenne to approve and f ile the Wyoming 
Division of Victim Services funding application State Fiscal Year 2019 and 2020, July 1, 2018 to 
June 30, 2020.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5919) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat for 
Mountain View Homes Subdivision, 2nd Filing, a replat of Tracts 3 and 4 of Mountain View Homes 
Subdivision being situated in a portion of the SW¼ Section 12, Township 13 North, Range 67 
West, of the Sixth Principal Meridian, Laramie County, Wyomi ng (located west of and adjacent 
to Southwest Drive, north of West College Drive)  – referred to Public Services Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Creating the Municipal Court Bu ilding Process Review Committee – 
referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Laybourn) 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between Laramie County, City of Cheyenne and the Board 
of Public Utilities for the purpose of purchasing a small unmanned aerial system and necessary 
software through the Cheyenne/Laramie County Geographic Information Systems Co operative, 
and providing rules for govern ing the operation of the system.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried (amount as specified in the MOU). (#6797) 
 
 Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Trihydro Corporation, 
Laramie, Wyomi ng, for Happy Jack Landfill environmental monitoring services (Solid Waste 
fund).  Craig LaVoy, Public Works Department, provided information on the number of proposals 
received through the Request for Proposals process and the recommendation to enter into  an 
agreement with TriHydro.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Luna moved to approve in an 
amount not to exceed $153,930.00, seconded by Mr. Case.  Mr. Luna moved to amend by substitute 
dated March 1, 2018 in an amount not to exceed $171,912.00, second ed by Mr. Johnson.  Upon 
inquiry, Mr. LaVoy explained the increase in the amount was due to the initial sampling amount 
numbers not being correctly multiplied relative to the number of tests required.  Motion to amend 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion , as amended, carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#6798) 
 
 Purchase Agreement and Settlement and Release of All Claims between the City of 
Cheyenne and Mary Rose Ramirez for acquisition of real property located at 2247 East 19th Street, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, in conjunction with the Pershing Boulevard/Converse Avenue/19th Street 
Roundabout Project (2007-2010 1% Sales Tax) .  Mary Rose Ramirez read a statement detailing 
her experiences and concerns as a homeowner and resident having a home adjacent to the Converse 
roundabout intersection.  Her concerns included: Personal safety; lack of parking; property 
access/egress; number of roundabout vehicle crashes; loss of property value, and pas t 
communications with the City that have not resolved her concerns.  Mr. Luna moved to approve 
in an amount not to exceed $185,000.00, seconded by Mr. Case.  Discussion included the following 
topics:  Roundabout signage; City sanitation worker and general safety concerns; “ righting a 
wrong”, other properties adjacent to the roundabout that had been purchased for the project, and a 
claim filed by Ms. Rami rez that had been d enied.  Upon inquiry, Ron Downey, City Treasurer, 
advised of the amount of project fun ds that were still available.  Mr. Case moved to amend by 
substitute dated March 5, 2018 which would increase the amount of compensation to $198,950.00, 
seconded by Mr. Luna.  Sections proposed to be changed pursuant to the proposed substitute were 
reviewed.  Discussion on the proposed amendment included:  Payment of Ms. Ramirez’ attorney 
fees; market analysis vs. property appraisal ; timing of the roundabout design involving the 
property; unsubstantiated documentation involving the amount to be paid by the City relative to 
attorney fees and personal loss, and property access concerns.  During discussion and upon inquiry, 
Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney, provided information available regarding payment of attorney fees; 
roundabout design involving Ms. Ramirez’ property and two other properties the City purchased; 
history of Ms. Ramirez’ concerns expressed to the previous administration, and discussions that 
had been held with Ms. Ramirez and her attorney on the additional expenses being requested.  Ms. 
Hackl noted the amounts outlined in the proposed amendment did not include moving expenses.    
Ericka Smith, attorney for  Ms. Ramirez, advised state statutes pertaining to eminent domain 
scenarios provide for payment of attorney fees, and explained how the amount being requested in 
the substitute was computed.  Motion to amend failed (due to a tie vote).  Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, 
Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna, Mr. Roybal and Mayor Orr.  Voting “no” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Dr. 
Rinne, Mr. Shanor and Mr. White.  Mr. Shanor, seconded by Mr. Case, moved to amend Section 
2 (b) by including a sentence indicating the seller may retain and remove kitchen appliances 
including the refrigerator, stove, washer and dryer, and adding a new Subsection (e) which states 
the City also agrees to compensate seller for the following costs incurred in resolvin g this matter 
as supported by proper documentation: (i) reasonable relocation costs as may have been required 
by law; and (ii) reasonable attorney’s fees as may have been required by law.  During verification 
of wording of the motion, Dr. Rinne raised a point of information inquiring whether the phrase “as 
supported by proper documentation” was to be included in the motion with Mr. Shanor confirming 
that wording is intended to be part of the motion.  For clarification, Ms. Hackl inquired whether a 
defined dollar amount of compensation was intended to be included in the amendment  with Mr. 
Shanor explaining if the amendment passed an additional amendment could be made to include a 
“not to excee d amount”.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Hackl advised, outside of a court case, the closest 
legal application regarding attorney’s fees may be the eminent domain state statute s, and that 
nothing in law pertains to determining the amount of relocation expenses but th at legal research 
on the topic could be conducted.  Comment w as made on the governing body’s responsibility 
involving relocation cost s.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Smith provided information on attorney’s fees 
relative to whether a lawsuit were involved through inverse condemnation, which is  part of the 
eminent domain statutes, and stated that would not be the cause of action they would bring forward.  
Ms. Smith expressed there was nothing that would allow for r elocation costs as may be required 
by law, therefore, the amendment would “strip” Ms. Ramirez of those costs.   Motion to amend 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson and 
Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  Mr. Cook move to amend the amendment by adding language that the 
costs (i.e. relocation costs and attorney’s fees) shall not exceed the amount of $15,000.00, 
seconded by Mr. Case.  Pursuant to Dr. Rinne raising a point of order, Mr. Cook restated his motion 
as a motion to amend instead of a motion to amend the amendment.  Comment was made on the 
intent of the amendment, which provides for dollar amount flexibility for costs which are currently 
unknown.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the 
exception of Mr. Johnson and Mayor Orr voting “no”.  As a clerical housekeeping measure, it was 
noted that references in Section 16 (h) indicating “Western Standard or Western Daylight Time” 
should be corrected to “Mountain Standard or Mountain Daylight Time” .  Following comments 
on handling this issue through the courts (judicial process) vs. the governing body, main motion, 
as twice amended, carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception 
of Mr. Johnson voting “no”. (#6799) 
 
[CA] Juvenile Housing Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County for the 
housing of juvenile detainees in the Laramie County Juvenile Services Center located at the Archer 
Complex – referred to Finance Committee. 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenn e, Laramie County Sheriff’s Department and

[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne, and Laramie County

[CA] Consideration of Bid #S -6-18 for a co ntract between the City of Cheyenne, Community

Pavillion Project (Land and Water Conservation grant and Parks Projects funds) .  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $277,600.00). (#6800) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S -4-18 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Community 
Recreation and Events Department, and Borderline Fencing, Inc., Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the 
Backstop Fence Removal and Repl acement at Converse Athletic Complex Project (Cheyenne

(amount not to exceed $149,580.00). (#6801) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -14-18 for furnishing one new 2017 -2018 low ground 
pressure/waste handling dozer for the City of Cheyenne Landfill (Solid Waste management)  –

[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -19-18 for furnishing 95 -gallon and 65 -gallon automated refuse

Committee. 
 
 Renewal applications for existing City liquor licenses and permits (retail, restaurant, 
limited retail, resort, bar and grill licenses and microbrewery and satellite distillery permits) filed 
with the City Clerk’s office.   Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr.  Roybal.  Mr. Luna 
moved to divide the question to provide for a separate vote on the renewal liquor license 
application filed by Keg, Inc., d/b/a D.T.’s Liq uor Mart and Tusker Bar , 3310 Ridge Road , 
seconded by Mr.  Case.  Mayor Orr advised a motion to divide the question was not debatable.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Mayor Orr stated as the 
motion carried, the governing body was now back on the main motion as divided to act upon the 
renewal liquor license application filed by D.T.’s Liquor Mart and Tusker Bar.  Upon inquiry, 
Office Josh Hedum, Police Department, provided information on annual compliance checks and 
the number of compliance failures involving D.T.’s.  Upon inquiry, Kevin Georges, Keg, Inc., 
provided information in response to non -compliance, employee involvement in the “failures”, 
employee TIPS certification training, and steps they have taken to address fu ture compliance 
issues.  He expressed concern with the compliance check process utilized by police officers, use 
of fake ID’s by underage individuals attempting to purchase alcohol, and unreliability of ID 
electronic scanners.  Mr. Georges verified local l iquor licensees utilize a communication “phone 
tree” and advised of training and topics of discussion, including deterrence of underage acquisition 
of alcohol, through the Laramie County Beverage Association.  Mayor Orr relinquished duties of 
the chair to Council President Dr. Rinne to allow her the opportunity to discuss the motion on the 
floor.  Discussion included:  Observation of youth on D.T.’s outdoor premises; TIPS training 
requirements and licensee responsibility, and objection to comments Mr. Georges had made during 
earlier discussion regarding police department compliance check practices.  Motion to approve the 
renewal liquor license application for Keg, Inc., d/b/a D.T.’s Liquor Mart and Tusker Bar carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr voting “no”.  
Following announcement of the vote, Dr. Rinne returned duties of the chair to Mayor Orr.  Dr. 
Rinne declared a conflict of interest (family member  interest in a license ) and vacated Council 
Chambers.  Mayor Orr stated the governing body was now back on the main motion to approve 
the remaining liquor license and permit renewal applications.  Discussion include: Availability of 
high quality fake ID’s through the internet and electronic ID readers/scanners.  Upon inquiry, Chief 
Kozak and Officer Hedum provided information on fake ID’s, effectiveness of ID scanners, holder 
of the fake ID being held responsible for use violations, Real ID Act and use of fake ID’s compared 
to other states, and benefit of licensees’ sharing of confiscat ed fake ID’s with the department.   
Main motion to approve the remaining liquor license and permit renewal applications carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following 
announcement of the vote, Dr. Rinne returned to Chambers. 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase Motorola digital portable radios for the Cheyenne Fire and 
Rescue Department from Wireless Advanced Communications, Evans, Colorado (Homeland 
Security funds) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase a replacement engine for a loader for Street and Alley Division 
from Tri-State Truck & Equipment, Casper, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held March 26 , 2018 at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a retail liquor license 
transfer of location application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Steamboat’s, LLC d/b/a 
Steamboat’s Wyoming Steak and Smoke House, from 1947 Dell Range Blvd. to 115 E. 17 th St., 
Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
 Under other business: 
 
 Mr. Case stated at the February 26, 2018 City Council meeting, the motion to accept the 
proposal for the new Municipal Court site from Chuck West, #1 Properties, Inc. of Cheyenne in 
an amount not to exceed $275,000.00 failed, and, having voted on the prevailing side and pursuant 
to Chapter 2.04 of City Code, moved to reconsider the vote on the main motion, seconded by Mr. 
Shanor.  For clarification of the February 26 th agenda item proposed to be reconsidered, Carol 
Intlekofer, City Clerk, read the agenda entitlement into the record as follows: “Consideration of 
RFP-14-18 for the new site of the City of Cheyenne Municipal Court (1% Specific Purpose Sales 
and Use Tax).”  Motion to reconsider carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Luna, 
Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor, Mr. White and Mayor Orr.  Voting “no” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Laybourn and 
Dr. Rinne.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend the main motion to read “that we accept the proposal from 
Chuck West, #1 Properties, Inc., of Cheyenne in an amount not to exceed $275,000.00 for t he 
purchase of the property located at the corner of 17 th St. and Carey Avenue, known as the Carey 
Building.  The closing on the property shall not occur until and unless an analysis of the site, along 
with estimates to demolish the building, remediate the  site and construct a one story building is 
completed, and such analysis concludes this can be completed within the $9,000,000.00 allowed 
by the ballot proposition.  If not, then that site will be rejected and a new RFP shall be issued 
seeking new proposal s.”  The motion was seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. Shanor explained his 
reasons for proposing the amendment.  Upon inquiry regarding a letter received by governing body 
members on this date, Chuck West, #1 Properties, Inc., advised a previous contract involvin g the 
Affinity Group’s potentially purchasing the Carey Building property had been rescinded, and that 
the owner is reaffirming his RFP response to sell the property to the City in the amount of 
$275,000.00.  He noted a date in which the owner would receive an answer from the City on its 
decision to purchase the property was contained within the owner’s letter so the matter would not 
be “floating forever”.  Mr. West expressed his belief that the property owner would be open to a 
reasonable amount of time needed by the City to conduct its analysis.  Upon inquiry, Amy Allen, 
City Engineer, stated, based upon her previous experience, the time frame proposed by the property 
owner was “a bit tight”.  Upon inquiry concerning which RFP proposals received completely met 
all of the RFP minimum requirements, Craig La Voy, Public Works Department, advised he was 
not familiar with  specific proposal contents, and Steve Gaer, Assistant Director, Facilities 
Maintenance, stated only one proposal (i.e. Carey Building a/k/a former Fowlers Building) closest 
met the minimum requirements.  Discussion included: RFP  criteria and minimum requirements 
generally; proposed closing date; City Council procedures; property being vacant and dormant for 
several years, and no specific date for closing on the property included in the motion to amend.  
Upon request, Mr. Shanor re iterated the wording of his motion to amend.   Upon inquiry, Sylvia 
Hackl, City Attorney, expressed, based upon wording of the proposed amendment, the due date 
would be the closing date, however, with respect to the owner’s letter that the b uilding be under 
contract by March 26th with a closing date of April 30 th, it may or may not be possible to get a 
closing done by that date due to City Council’s governmental process in acting upon a purchase 
contract, and legal staff time to review and prepar e a contract document .  Ms. Hackl further  
expressed her belief that the City may need a minimum of another month to review conditions and 
prepare an agreement. 
 
  
 
Mr. West explained the property owner’s position relative to agreeing to take the property off the 
market to the City’s benefit, and owner’s willingness to work on a reasonable time table as long 
as the City is acting in good faith , and stated another mo nth may be considered reasonable.  
Discussion followed on constructability review pursuant to the proposed amendment and whose 
responsibility it would be to conduct and fund; services by Noel Griffith Jr. and Associates; use of 
1% Specific Purpose funds; past use of 1% funds relative to conducting project due diligence, and 
single story vs. two story facility and parking availability at the site.  During discussion, Mayor 
Orr provided information in response to comments and inquiry, and Ron Downey, City Treasurer, 
advised of potential funding sources that may be available for due diligence costs.  Joe Dougherty 
spoke in support of conducting due diligence if funding was available and the need to acquire cost 
information, historical aspects of the Carey Build ing, and stated an artist’s rendition should be 
provided to show the public what the new building would look like.  Motion to amend carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook and Mr. 
Laybourn voting “no”.  Pursuant to query, Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, explained governing body 
meeting procedures involving a motion to reconsider in which the public previously had the 
opportunity to provide input on the main motion.  Discussion included: Rationale for remediation 
of the site; short term vs. long term “view” ; minimal impact of the Municipal Court office in the 
downtown area, and private/public partnerships involving downtown rehabilitation projects.  In 
response to a point of order called by Mr. Roybal, Ms. Intlekofer advised procedurally discussion 
on the main motion, as reconsidered and amended, by City Council members was in order.  Mr. 
Johnson requested consideration to hear comments from those in the audience who wished to speak 
on the motion with Mayor Orr a greeing to the request .  Offering public comments were Ma jor 
Dorr, who spoke against the proposed Carey Building location and in support of building the new 
Municipal Court on the north side of the Municipal Building property, or upon a vacant lot, and 
Pat Lane, St. Mary’s School, who spoke in support of the Carey Building site, and against building 
on Municipal Building property.   Main motion, as amended, carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, 
Mr. Johnson, Mr. Luna, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor, Mr. White and Mayor Orr.  Voting “no” – Mr. 
Cook, Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne. 
 
There being no further business to come before the  governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 8:35 p.m. 
 
        Submitted by, 
 
             
        Carol Intlekofer, MMC 
        City Clerk 
   
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  March 23, 2018

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2018-03-26 | CLIP_ID: 646 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 646

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
March 26, 2018 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  COUNCIL PRESIDENT – Dr. Mark Rinne ; 
COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, 
Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White. Absent: Mayor Marian J. Orr .  Also present: Carol 
Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney. In the absence of Mayor Orr, Council 
President Rinne chaired the meeting.  [City Clerk’s note:  As chair, President Rinne exercised his 
option, as entitled by state statute, to not cast a vote on agenda items].  The pledge of allegiance 
was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  present with the exception of Mr.  
Johnson voting “no”, and President Rinne who did not vote.  For informational purposes, President 
Rinne advised those present that the Reed Avenue Rail Corridor Master Plan resolution, included 
on the Consent Agenda, was being referred to Public Services Committee which would meet on 
April 3, 2018 at Noon in Room 104. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on March 12, 2018.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Roybal moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Luna.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of 
President Rinne who did not vote. 
 
President Rinne opened a p ublic hearing for a retail liquor license transfer of location 
application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Steamboat’s, LLC d/b/a Steamboat’s Wyoming 
Steak and Smoke House, from 1947 Dell Range Blvd. to 115 E. 17 th St., Cheyenne, Wyoming.   
Scott Rupe, Steamboat’s Wyoming Steak and Smoke House , explained they are in negotiations 
with the property owner relative to the lease agreement for the restaurant’s current location , and 
indicated, at this time, they would like to move forward with the process to transfer the license 
location.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Rupe advised notification would be provided prior to the April 9 th 
City Council meeting if the transfer of location application would be withdrawn.  There being no 
further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, President Rinne closed the 
hearing and referred the application to Finance Committee. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Section 1.16.050, Off -Site improvements – 
Requirements, of Chapter 1.16, Annexation and Infrastructure Improvements, of Title 1, General 
Provisions, of the code o f the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Mr. Johnson moved to approve on 
third and final reading, seconded by Mr . Case.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. 
Roybal stated the recommendation of the committee is to approve on third and final reading.  Upon 
inquiry, Ben Rowland, Assistant C ity Attorney, explained the process that would be utilized if a 
property were sold after a deposit had been made by the initial property owner, and noted the 
parties involved may negotiate between themselves relative to the deposit amount.  Comment was 
made regarding the refund process; infrastructure improvements and lack of implementation, and 
annexation of county pockets.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Rowland verified the ordinance change applies 
only to properties which have been annexed into the City involving instances where deposits have 
been made and infrastructure has not been put in.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” all members of 
the governing body present with the exception of President Rinne who did not vote. (#4221) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Chapter 8.56, Noise Control, Section 
8.56.060, Permit for relief from designated levels, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Code of the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat for 
Mountain View Homes Subdivision, 2nd Filing, a replat of Tracts 3 and 4 of Mountain View Homes 
Subdivision being situated in a portion of the SW¼ Section 12, Township 13 North, Range 67 
West, of the Sixth Principal Meridian, Laramie County, Wyoming (located west of and adjacent 
to Southwest Drive, north of West College Drive).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5920) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Creating the Municipal Court Building Process Review Committee.  As

stated the item was withdrawn from the agenda. 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Amending Resolution No. 5808 by appointing the Mayor of the City of

according to the provisions of W.S. §§ 16-4-112 & 16 -4-113 – referred to Finance Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA]  RESOLUTION – Acknowledging receipt of the Reed A venue Rail Corridor Master Plan 
– referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsors – Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White)  
 
[CA] Juvenile Housing Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County for the

[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Laramie County Sheriff’s Department and

Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6807) 
 
[CA] 2018–2019 Collective Labor Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne 
Firefighters Local Union No. 279, International Association of Firefighters.  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. (#6808) 
 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Josh Cossitt to provide tennis services and 
instruction at City-owned tennis facilities (revenue to City).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried 
(revenue to the City $600.00). (#6809) 
 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and HollyFrontier Cheyenne Refining LLC for 
designation of the title of sponsorship of SuperDay and the Tour de Prairie events .  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried (revenue to the City $25,000.00). (#6810) 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understa nding between the City of Cheyenne, and Laramie County 
School District No. 1 to provide transportation service for the Recreat ion Division “REC Camp 
Program”.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6811) 
 
[CA] Agreement by and between the City of Cheyenne , Cheyenne Civic Center, and the 
International Alliance of Theatrical Stage Employees, Moving Picture Technicians, Artists and 
Allied Crafts of the United States, Its Territories and Canada (IATSE),  Local 229, for stagehand 
labor – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 [CA] Consideration of Bid #E -14-18 for furnishing one new 2017 -2018 low ground 
pressure/waste handling dozer for the City of Cheyenne Landfill (Solid Waste management) .  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid submitted by Power Equipment, Casper, WY, 
in an amount not to exceed $353,117.00).  
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -19-18 for furnishing 95 -gallon and 65 -gallon automated refuse 
carts for the City of Cheyenne Sanitation Div ision (Solid Waste management ).  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid submitted by IPL Inc., Forsyth, GA, in an amount n ot to 
exceed $72,172.00).  
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-22-18 for the City’s annual purchase of “Aggregates” (2015-2018 
1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-23-18 for the City’s annual purchase of “Hot Mix, Tack Oil and 
Cold Mix” (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase Motorola digital portable rad ios for the Cheyenne Fire and 
Rescue Department from Wireless Advanced Communications, Evans, Colorado (Homeland 
Security funds).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $38,703.68). 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase a replacement engine for a loader for Street and Alley Division 
from Tri-State Truck & Equipment, Casper, Wyoming.   Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried 
(amount not to exceed $47,129.31). 
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held April 9, 2018, at 6:00 p.m. in City 
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the City of Cheyenne’s Annual 
Action Plan Fiscal Year 2018 -2019 prepared by the Cheyenne Housing and Community 
Development Office. 
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held April 9, 2018, at 6:00 p.m. in City 
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to determine whether compliance 
of annexation conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exists for Lot 14, Block 5, 
Homestead Addition, First Filing Replat, Laramie County, Wyoming (located south of Homestead 
Avenue, north of the Cheyenne Greenway). 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held April 9, 2018, at 6:00 p.m. in City 
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to determine whether compliance 
of annexation conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exists for a portion of Lot 1, 
Block 1, Cheyenne Hills Church Subdivision and portions of the east half of Section 25, T.14N, 
R.66W., of the 6 th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (locate d north of and including Pershing 
Boulevard, east of Farthing Road; also located south of Sage Road, east of Gunsmoke Road). 
 
 Under other business: 
 
 Commenting on the Municipal Court building project and the selected Carey Building site 
were:  Ed Melko, Astrid, Lois Phillips and Jim Byrd, State Representative (legislature).  Comments 
included: Building design to include a second floor “shell-out” space; utilizing additional funds, if 
needed, for the project from other projects; designing building to be similar in physical appearance 
as the historic block, and proposed location for the project being an i nappropriate choice and not 
contributing to revitalizing downtown.  
 
 Mr. Laybourn commented on the resolution withdr awn from the agenda  earlier in the 
meeting and his reasons for proposing the resolution to provide for a review committee for the 
Municipal Court project .  He expressed concerns with  revitalizing downtown; use of 6th Penny 
funds for demolition, remediation of environmental issues and design costs , and lack of current 
property owner financial contribution toward property improvements. 
 
 Mr. Johnson requested a memo of clarification from City Attorney staff regarding a  
Wyoming Supreme Court ruling related to firefighter union contract negotiations.  Sylvia Hackl, 
City Attorney, explained the ruling require s there be a quorum of the governing body present 
during negotiations, however, a Labor Management Committee was created last year as part of the 
approved current contract which provides for the committee to meet throughout the year and bring 
items before the governing body .  She stated negotiations were in complete compliance with the 
law and that she would prepare and provide a memo. 
 
 There being no further business to come before the  governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:43 p.m.  
 
        Submitted by, 
 
             
        Carol Intlekofer, MMC 
        City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2018-04-09 | CLIP_ID: 648 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 648

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
April 9, 2018 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn (arriving at 6:10 p.m.), 
Mike Luna, Scott Roybal, Dr. Mark Rinne and Dicky Shanor. Absent: Jeff White.  Also present: 
Carol Intlekofer , City Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was 
recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Johnson, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Roybal and Mr. Cook, Agenda Item #18 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. 
Roybal moved to approve  remaining Consent Agenda i tems, seconded by Mr . Cook.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on March 26, 2018.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Roybal declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Cowboy 
Dodge, Inc.) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Johnson moved to approve payment of vouchers 
as presented, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Roybal returned to 
Council Chambers. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing for the City of Cheyenne’s Annual Action Plan Fiscal 
Year 2018 -2019 prepared by the Cheyenne Housing and Community Development Office.   
Deanne Widauf, Housing and Community Development, provided a brief overview of the history 
and purpose of the Community Development Block Grant program , noting the proposed plan is 
consistent with the 5-year Comprehensive Consolidated Plan, and stated the anticipated allocation 
funding amount is $398,442.00 with $1,000.00 in program income and $56,000 .00 in recaptured 
funding. Ms. Widauf explained the T ier system being utilized and reviewed information on the 
grant application process; public notifications and opportunities for input, and role of the Advisory 
Council in reviewing and recommending projects to be funded.  She stated nine projects are being 
proposed and discussed the category type, funding amount and purpose for each proje ct, and 
commented on program administration funding . Upon inquiry, Ms. Widauf advised the  
neighborhood involved in one of the projects (a street paving project) was located north of Dell 
Range, between Frontier Mall and Yellowstone Road.  [City Clerk’s note: Councilman Laybourn 
joined the meeting] Patrice Gap en, Housing and Community Development Advisory Council  
member, stated the number of applications submitted were more than what could be funded, and 
that the Advisory Council unanimously agreed on the projects to recommend.  Ms. Gap en also 
noted the area that will receive paved roads had previously been a county pocket.  Brief comment 
was made on county pockets.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing 
having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a p ublic hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exists for Lot 14, Block 5, Homestead 
Addition, First Filing Replat, Laramie County, Wyoming (located south of Homestead Avenue, 
north of the Cheyenne Greenway).  Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development Department,  provided 
information on the property requested by the landowner to be annexed and reviewed requirements 
of and compliance with annexation conditions contained in state statutes.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Lloyd 
explained it was his understanding that a portion of the property utilized for vehicle storage is not 
used by the land owner, but by an adjacent property owner.  He stated if the proposed zone change 
in conjunction with the annexation is approved, it  would prohibit the use of the land for vehicle 
storage.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, 
Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a p ublic hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exists for a portion of Lot 1, Block 1, 
Cheyenne Hills Church Subdivision and portions of the ea st half of Section 25, T.14N., R.66W., 
of the 6 th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located north of and including Pershing Boulevard, 
east of Farthing Road; also located south of Sage Road, east of Gunsmoke Road).   Seth Lloyd, 
Planning and Development Department, provided information on the property requested by the 
landowner to be annexed and reviewed requirements of and compliance with annexation 
conditions contained in state statutes.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of 
the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapter 8.56, Noise Control, Section 
8.56.060, Permit for relief from designated levels, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Code of the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Astrid, The Plains Hotel,  expressed concern with vehicle noise 
levels and speeding in the area of The Plains Hotel, and questioned whether the proposed ordinance 
would negatively impact the situ ation.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Luna moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr . Cook.  Upon inquiry, Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, 
explained the proposed ordinance is affiliated with an ordinance change last year which 
streamlined bus iness licensing processes, and that a change to this City Code section was 
inadvertently omitted from the previous ordinance, noting the proposed change is essentially a 
housekeeping measure.  She further explained the ordinance’s effect will result in the Police Chief 
being authorized to determine  conditions affiliated with  a noise permit instead of the Mayor.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Annexing to the City of Che yenne, Wyoming, Lot 14, 
Block 5, Homestead Addition, First Filing Replat, situated in the N½ NE¼ Section 4, T.13N., 
R.66W., 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located south of Homestead Avenue, north of the

ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments,

ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending, creating, or deleting various chapters and 
sections within Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the Cheyenne City Code relating to

(Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Amending Resolution No. 5808 by appointing the Mayor of the City of 
Cheyenne to serve on the Advisory Board of the participating municipalities of the Laramie State 
Small Business Credit Initiative (“SS BCI”) Consortium.  Motion ( per Consent Agenda) carried. 
(#5921) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Adopting the revised City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, Investment Policy 
and Portfolio Guidelines, February 26, 2018.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5922) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Re-appropriating the Fiscal Year 2018 budget of the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W.S. §§ 16-4-112 & 16 -4-113.  Motion (per Consent Agend a) 
carried. (#5923) 
  
 RESOLUTION – Acknowledging receipt of the Reed Avenue Rail Corridor Master Plan. 
Speaking in support of the Master Plan were: Vicki Dugger, Downtown Development Authority; 
Cari Cuffney (who also voiced support on behalf of Danielle and Mark Cullins who could not be 
present); Dave Teubner, Warehouse Twenty One; Andrew Rose, property co-owner in the West 
Edge vicinity; Amy Spieker; Affie Ellis, State Senator, District 8, and Astrid, owner, The Plains 
Hotel.  Comments included the following topics:  Master Plan’s importance to development of the 
West Edge area; financial investments by business property owners; types of businesses that may 
be interested in locating in the West Edge; investing in revitalization of the downtown as well as 
the West Edge, and difference between acknowledging receipt vs. adoption of the plan.  Tony 
Lucero, A & C Feed, voiced his concerns regarding the Master Plan and future access to property 
he owns along the rail corridor.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to 
acknowledge receipt , seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Discussion followed on the 6 th Penny ballot 
question that was approved for  the Reed Avenue Corridor project; acknowledging receipt vs. 
adoption of the report; railroad easement that must be acquired, and moving forward with the 
Master Plan.  During discussion and upon inquiry, Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney, provided her 
interpretation of the difference between “acknowledging receipt” vs. “adoption” of the plan.  
William Powell Haley, property owner in the area, questioned the effect of a railroad easement 
relative to his property boundaries.  Providing information in response to in quiries were:  Tom 
Mason, Director, Metropolitan Planning Organization, noting the plan reflects a 35% preliminary 
design, and Troy Russ, Kimley-Horn and Associates, planning and design engineering consultants, 
who provided information on working with the railroad regarding an easement, and determining 
any private property access issues .  Mr. Laybourn moved to amend the pending motion from 
“acknowledging receipt” to “adopting”.  Motion died due to lack of a second.  Comment was made 
that funding for the project was in place through voter approval of the 6 th Penny ballot question.  
Motion to acknowledge receipt carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
(#5924) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Adopting the City of Cheyenne Annual Action Plan 2018-2019 prepared 
by the Cheyenne Housing and Community Development office – referred to Finance Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION –  Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat 
for Hawk’s Landing Subdivision, a replat of a portion of Tract 8, Capitol Tracts, being situated in 
a portion of the NE¼ NW¼, Section 18, T13N, R66W, of the 6th Principal Meridian, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located west of and adjacent to York Avenue, south of West College Drive) – 
referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Precluding the rental or use of the City’s athletic fields by Steve Avila 
or SAA Enterprises until those parties have repaid the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Mustangs 
Baseball for all losse s sustained from the 20 17 CABA World Series Tournament – referred to 
Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Laybourn) 
 
[CA] Agreement by and between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Civic Center, and the 
International Alliance of Theatrical Stage Employees, Moving Picture Technicians, Artists and 
Allied Crafts of the United States, Its Territories and Canada (IATSE),  Local 229, for stagehand 
labor.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6816) 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne  and Rebecca Velez for 
concessionaire services at the Converse Softball Complex, the Brimmer Softball Complex, the 
Cheyenne Junior League Baseball Complex and the Powers Field Baseball Complex  (revenue to 
City) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Youth 
Baseball for concessionaire services at the Dutcher Baseball Complex (revenue to City) – referred 
to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Chicago Dog House 
for concessionaire services at the Pioneer Park Baseball Complex (revenue to City)  – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County 
School District Number One to provide drainage improvements on Sunflower Road adjacent to 
Buffalo Ridge Elementary School (Ward II Contingency and Engineering Drainage Funds)  – 
referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification No. 3 to City Contract No. 6498 between the City of Cheyenne and 
DHM Design Corporation, Denver, CO, for professional services associated with the Civic Center 
Commons Project (FEMA Pre-Disaster Mitigation Grant and Wyoming Business Council Park 
Grant) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 [CA] Consideration of Bid #E-22-18 for the City’s annual purchase of “Aggregates” (2015-2018 
1% Sales Tax) .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accept ed bid from Simon C ontractors, 
Cheyenne, WY). 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-23-18 for the City’s annual purchase of “Hot Mix, Tack Oil and 
Cold Mix” (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from 
JTL Group, Inc. d/b/a Knife River, Cheyenne, WY).  
 
[CA] Retail liquor license transfer of location application filed with the City Clerk’s O ffice for 
Steamboat’s, LLC d/b/a Steamboat’s Wyoming Steak and Smoke House, from 1947 Dell Range 
Blvd. to 115 E. 17th St., Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase six Self Contained Breathing Apparatuses (SCBA) and affiliated 
equipment from L.N. Curtis & Sons, Salt Lake City, UT, for the Chey enne Fire and Rescue 
Department (Homeland Security funds) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase twenty 20 premium ice bumper cars from IBC I nternational, 
Steamboat Springs, CO, for the Community Recreation and Events Department, Ice and Events 
Center (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Under other business: 
 
 Major Dorr commented in support of Civic Center operational changes relative to a recent 
newspaper article, and expressed concern with loss of parking spaces in the west parking lot across 
from the Municipal Building and Civic Center as part of the Civic Center Commons Project. 
 
 There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:05 p.m.

        City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2018-04-23 | CLIP_ID: 650 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 650

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
April 23, 2018 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White. Absent: Scott Roybal.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City 
Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on April 9 , 2018 .  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Johnson moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Case.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Chapter 8.56, Noise Control, Section 
8.56.060, Permit for relief from designated levels, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Code of the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on 
third and final reading, seconded by Mr . Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body present. (#4222) 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, Lot 14, 
Block 5, Homestead Addition, First Filing Replat, sit uated in the N½ NE¼ Section 4, T.13N., 
R.66W., 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located south of Homestead Avenue, north of the 
Cheyenne Greenway).  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Shanor moved to approve 
on second reading with staff conditions 1 and 2, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from County MR Medium -Density 
Residential to NR-2 Neighborhood Residential - Medium-Density for Lot 14, Block 5, Replat of 
Homestead Addition, First Filing, Laramie C ounty, Wyoming (located south of Homestead 
Avenue, north of the Cheyenne Greenway ).  Mr. Shanor moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr.  Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present.  
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion 
of Lot 1, Block 1, Cheyenne Hills Church Subdivision and portions of the east half of Section 25, 
T.14N., R.66W., of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located north of and including 
Pershing Boulevard, east of Farthing Road; also located south of Sa ge Road, east of Gunsmoke 
Road).  John Edwards provided a handout and reviewed development history of Saddle Ridge 
Subdivision and development plans proposed for the subdivision’s 13th Filing involving 
annexation and re-zoning of the property.  Mr. Edwards noted the types of residential housing that 
would be provided in addition to an area reserved for a new school and open space .  Mr. Shanor 
moved to approve on second reading with revised annexation map, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from County A -1, Agricultural and Rural 
Residential to MR, Medium -density Residential and P -Public for a portion of Lot 1, Block 1, 
Cheyenne Hills Church Subdivision and port ions of the east half of Section 25, T.14N., R.66W., 
of the 6 th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located north of and including Pershing Boulevard, 
east of Farthing Road; also located south of Sage Road, east of Gunsmoke Road ).  Mr.  Shanor 
moved to approve on second reading with revised map, seconded by Mr. Case.  Mr. Shanor moved 
to amend by substitute dated April 11, 2018, seconded by Mr.  Cook.  Upon inquiry, Susana 
Montana, Planning and Development  Department, explained the revised map  includes a title 
clarification, and the substitute adds the zoning type “P-Public”, both being clerical adjustments. 
Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Main motion 
as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the 
Cheyenne Unified Development Code, changing the zoning classification from Conditional MUR, 
Mixed Use Residential Emphasis to NB, Neighborhood Business for a parcel of land consisting of 
all of Block 16, Hellmans Addition Subdivision, Cheyenne, Wyoming to be known as Peak 
Wellness Center (located at 510 West 29 th Street, between O’Neil and Thomes Avenues ).  Mr.  
Shanor moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr . Case.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending, creating, or deleting various chapters and 
sections within Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the Cheyenne City Code relating to 
adoption of the National Electrical Code, 2017 Edition.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr.  Johnson.  Upon inquiry regarding  stakeholder input , Robert Briggs, 
Planning and Development Director, explained Building department staff is compiling an email 
list of electrical contractors in the community to be contacted for their input. Mr. Briggs explained 
the electrical code is a standard adopted at the State level , which the City is  required to adopt .  
Comment was made on initiating communication with local electrical contractors to receive 
feedback on any concerns.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fee 
Schedule – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Adopting the City of Cheyenne Annual Action Plan 2018-2019 prepared 
by the Cheyenne Housing a nd Community Development office.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#5925) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION –  Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat 
for Hawk’s Landing Subdivision, a replat of a portion of Tract 8, Capitol Tracts, being situated in 
a portion of the NE¼ NW¼, Section 18, T13N, R66W, of the 6th Principal Meridian, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located west of and adjacent to York Avenu e, south of West Co llege Drive). 
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5926) 
 
RESOLUTION – Precluding the rental or use of the City’s athletic fields by Steve Avila 
or SAA Enterprises until those parties have repaid the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Mustangs 
Baseball for all losses sustained from the 20 17 CABA World Series Tournament .  Jerry Innis, 2 
Doors Down, commented on the financial impact benefitting local businesses in allowing the 
tournament to be held in Cheyenne.   Carissa Pollard read a statement in support of Cheyenne 
Mustangs baseball; commented on financial concerns and responsibilities related to last year’s 
tournament, and scheduling of ball fields, and encouraged the governing body to “do the right 
thing” fo r Cheyenne and local baseball players.  Major Dorr spoke in support of Mr. Avila 
reimbursing the City and paying his bills incurred from last year’s tournament prior to allowing 
Mr. Avila use of the ball fields for the 2018 tournament.  During public comme nts, Mayor Orr 
clarified last year’s financial arrangement was between the City and Cheyenne Mustangs, not 
directly with Mr. Avila.  Mr. White reported there was no recommendation from Finance 
Committee on the item.  Mr. Johnson moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Mr. Shanor moved 
to amend by substitute, seconded by Mr. Case.  It was explained the amendment will place certain 
conditions that must be fulfilled  by Mr. Avila in order to hold the 2018 tournament.  Discussion 
included the following:  Changes contained within the proposed substitute; time frame required to 
submit revenue documentation and payment to the City of 30% of gross revenues from the 
tournament; payment for field use; use of home dugout for participating Cheyenne teams; whether 
the 7th Whereas clause contained within the resolution was needed, and the proposed substitute 
not being available to Council members or the public prior to the current meeting.  Upon inquiry, 
Mr. Shanor advised the proposed amendment had been shared with Cheyen ne Mustangs but had 
not been reviewed or approved by the City Attorney.  Jay Folds, Assistant Coach, Cheyenne 
Mustangs, spoke in support of the proposed substitute and the 2018 tournament.  Rob Jarosh, Hirst 
and Applegate law firm and supporter of Cheyenne  Mustangs, stated he had received inquiries 
from City Council members relative to the City renting the fields to Mr. Avila, and explained his 
position that the City could rent the fields for purposes in the best interests of the City and for 
economic interests of the community, and spoke in support of the proposed substitute.  Motion to 
amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor 
and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn and Mayor Orr.  Mr. Shanor moved to 
amend as follows, seconded by Dr. Rinne: Under the Now Therefore, Be It Resolved clause, in 
Section 1 (d) and (e) change the phrase “within five days” to instead read “within thirty days” .  
Discussion included payment of certain team registration fees by a private donor, and whether the 
resolution should reference Mr. Avila only instead of Mr. Avila and SAA Enterprises, LLC, 
collectively.  Upon inquiry during discussion, Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney, advised that SAA 
Enterprises, LLC, had been administratively dissolved and suggested the resolution be changed to 
include Steve Avila’s name only.  Dr. Rinne raised a point of order noting the amendment on the 
floor should be addressed prior to any additional proposed amendments.  Motion to amend carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Johnson and 
Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  Discussion followed on reasons for naming Mr. Avila and SAA 
Enterprises, LLC, collectively in the 1st W hereas clause, and timing of field reservations for the 
2018 tournament through City staff.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend by striking the 7th Whereas 
clause, seconded by Mr. Case.  Upon inquiry, Jason Sanchez, Assistant Director, Community 
Recreation and Events Department, provided information on when the ball fields had been “put on 
hold” vs. Mr. Avila’s application to reserve the fields ; inquiry for field reservation by the 
Mustangs, and standard staff policy on holding fields for tournaments.   Motion to amend carried.  
Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor, Mr. White and Mayor Orr.  Voting “no” – Mr. 
Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne.  Comments of concern were made regarding the 
message to the community the substitute resolution relayed, and constructing amendments from 
the dais during meetings without advance notice.  Main motion, as amended three times, carried.  
Voting “yes” -- Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor and Mr. White.  Voting 
“no” – Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn and Mayor Orr. (#5927) 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Rebecca Velez for 
concessionaire services at the Converse Softball Complex, the Brimmer Softball Complex, the 
Cheyenne Junior League Baseball Complex and the Powers  Field Baseball Complex (revenue to 
City).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6822) 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Youth

[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Chicago Dog House

Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $60,000). (#6825)

[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Amazing Amusements for an ATM machine

[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Amazing Amusements for game machines

[CA] Purchase and Sale Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Linda M. Moran, Gail L. 
Anderson and Vici Rae Foster, for real property to be used for the Christensen Road Project (1% 
Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Purchase and Sale Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Sung Thik Lim, for r eal

construction of a municipal courthouse (1% Spec ific Purpose Sales and Use Tax) – referred to

[CA] Contract for group health insurance for City of Cheyenne employees – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Delta Dental for group dental benefits for City

[CA] Contract Modification No. 3 to City Contract No. 6498 between the City of Cheyenne and 
DHM Design Corporation, Denver, CO, for professional services associated with the Civic Center 
Commons Project (FEMA Pre-Disaster Mitigation Grant and Wyoming Business Council Park 
Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $150,544.50). (#6498) 
 
[CA] Amendment No. 4 to Contract No. 5930 between the City of Cheyenne and KONE, Inc., 
Englewood, Colorado, to standardize pricing for elevator maintenance services and to add services 
for an elevator at the Botanic Gardens and Transfer Station – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Amendment No. 1 to Contract No. 5939 between the City of Cheyenne and Lamar 
Companies, Baton Rouge, Louisiana, to extend the term of lease for placement of billboards on 
the Belvoir Ranch (revenue to City) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 6743, Professional Services Agreement 
between the City of Cheyenne and River Oaks Communications Corporation, Centennial, 
Colorado, for drafting a nd negotiation of Charter Communications Cable Franchise renewal and 
CenturyLink Cable Television franchise agreements – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-18-18 for furnishing one new 2017 -2018 front end wheel loader 
for the Street and Alley Division (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-20-18 for furnishing front end load containers for the Sanitation 
Division (Solid Waste Management funds) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase six Self Contained Breathing Apparatuses (SCBA) and affiliated 
equipment from L.N. Curtis & Sons, Salt Lake City, UT, for the Chey enne Fire and Rescue 
Department (Homeland Security funds) .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amo unt not to 
exceed $62,571.30). 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase twenty 20 premium ice bumper cars from IBC International, 
Steamboat Springs, CO, for the Community Recreation and Events Department, Ice and Events 
Center (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed 
$148,700.00). 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase Motorola complete vehicle 2 -way radio systems and chargers 
from Wireless Advanced Communications, Evans, Colorado, for the Cheyenne Police Department 
(Homeland Security funds, 2015-2018 1% Sales Tax and 1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax) 
-- referred to Finance Committee. 
 
Appointment of Kyle Wall and Lane Pilbin  to the International Fire Code Board of 
Appeals, and Steven Bates and David Halley to the Mayor’s Council for People with Disabilities. 
Dr. Rinne moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body pr esent with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (Mayoral 
appointees).   
 
Re-appointment of Kevin Williams to the Internati onal Fire Code Board of Appeals.   Dr. 
Rinne moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body present with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (Mayoral 
appointee). 
 
 Under other business:  
 
 Major Dorr spoke in opposition to people owing money to the City and not paying their  
debts. 
 
 Mr. White commented on the success, attendance-wise, of the most recent Fridays in the 
Asher building concert series, and volunteers who are helping with the concerts. 
 
Mr. Johnson commented on the success of a fundraiser benefitting the Rooted in Cheyenne 
tree program which involved partnership with the Accomplice Beer Company and a special craft 
beer brewed for the fundraiser.  He noted another fundraiser, again in partnership with Accomplice, 
is being held in conjunction with Arbor Day. 
 
 Mayor Orr advised the Bloomberg team will be in Cheyenne on April 24th, and the City 
will be introducing its Buildings with a Purpose Project during an event to solicit stakeholders and 
public input to be held April 24th at 1601 Central Ave. at 7:00 p.m. 
 
 There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:17 p.m. 
 
   
 
        Submitted by, 
 
             
        Carol Intlekofer, MMC 
        City Clerk

Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the 
Cheyenne Unified Development Code, changing the zoning classification from Conditional MUR, 
Mixed Use Residential Emphasis to NB, Neighborhood Business for a parcel of land consisting of 
all of Block 16, Hellmans Addition Subdivision, Cheyenne, Wyoming to be known as Peak 
Wellness Center (located at 510 West 29 th Street, between O’Neil and Thomes Avenues ).  Mr.  
Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4227) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending, creating, or deleting various chapters and 
sections within Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the Cheyenne City Code relating to 
adoption of the National Electrical Code, 2017 Edition .  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third 
and final reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”. (#4228) 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fee 
Schedule.  Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works, reviewed reasons for the recommendation 
of a 5% rate increase, and advised of the 2014 rate study recommendation , noting an updated 
integrated solid waste management study will be conducted next year .  Upon inquiry, Ms. 
Nemecek provided information on the following topics: Vertical landfill e xpansion and DEQ 
permit that has been obtained ; savings realized from not hauling solid waste to Ault, CO ; solid 
waste program, including compost and yard waste programs; elements of the solid waste, landfill, 
compost, Transfer Station  and yard waste program operational costs and upgrades ; proposed 
additional route for the yard waste  program; anticipated revenue  to be received from the rate 
increase; 2014 rate study findings and components included in the study ; employee salaries, and 
discontinuance of equipment leasing as a mechanism to acquire trucks and equipment.   Upon 
further inquiry, Ms. Nemecek and Ron Downey, City Treasurer,  explained the $ 748,000.00 
transfer from the solid waste enterprise fund to the general fund (subsidy) is essentially a franchise 
  
fee and will be allocated for elsewhere in the budget.  Upon inquiry by Jeff Dierksen, Mayor Orr 
and Ms. Nemecek advised the solid waste fund is an enterprise fund; that the yard waste program 
is optional and, due to the number of residents on a waiting list who want the service, an additional 
route is being proposed.  Kevin Kovak commented on blight concerns near property he owns, 
submitting a photo of the blighted property (on file with City Clerk’s office),  and requested 
information on the Mayor’s Fight the Blight program .  Mr. Shanor raised a point of order  that 
comments were off subject from the agenda topic .  Mayor Orr stated that  blight issues can be 
considered during upcoming budget discussions, and that the information provided by Mr. Kovak 
will be reviewed.   Mr. White reported there was no recommendation on the item from Finance 
Committee.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Case.  Discussion 
followed on the 2014 rate study and components that have changed since the study was conducted.  
Mr. Shanor, seconded by Mr. Case, moved to amend by adding an additional note, Note 16 (i.e. at 
the end of the fee schedule attached with the ordinance), as follows:  All revenues raised as a result 
of this 5% fee increase shall be dedicated to the landfill and equipment expenditures identified in 
the HDR 2014 study before being used for payroll or other expenses.  Upon inquiry on proposed 
amendment impact on the subsidy amount to be transferred to the general fund, Mr. Downey stated 
the franchise fees are 5% of the total City budget and the amendment’s impact would depend on 
any changes to t he proposed FY2019 budget .  Upon inquiry, Mr. Shanor stated his proposed 
amendment would only apply for Fiscal Year 2019 and could be evaluated next year.  In response 
to inquiry, Ms. Nemecek advised all public w orks department employees are currently at the 
minimum salary amount as recommended in the EIS salary study.  Comments followed in support 
as well as non-support for the amendment; DEQ permit that has been obtained; department savings 
that have been realized and program improvements that have been made over the past few years; 
salaries of public works employees meeting a living standard, and allowing staff time to study 
potential impact of the proposed amendment, if approved, prior to third and final reading of the 
ordinance.  Motion to amend failed (due to a tie vote).  Voting “yes” –  Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Dr. 
Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr. Shanor. Voting “no” - Mr. Johnson, Mr. L aybourn, Mr. L una, Mr. 
White and Mayor Orr.  Main motion carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Case, Dr. Rinne, 
Mayor Orr, Mr. Cook, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. Johnson, Mr. 
Luna and Mr. Shanor. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Approving and establishing revised water and sewer

[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Goat Green, LLC, for 
vegetation management along Dry Creek and Crow Creek (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) .  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $100,000.00). (#6830) 
 
 Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Metropolitan Planning 
Organization, and AVI p.c., for the Parsley Boulevard Corridor Plan (MPO Planning funds) . 
Nancy Olson, Metropolitan Planning Organization , explained purpose of the agreement is to 
acquire a 35% design corridor plan on Parsley from Ames Drive to West College Drive and a 10% 
design from West College Drive to West Wallick Rd.  Ms. Olson reviewed corridor elements that 
will be addressed, noting WYDOT will be replacing the bridge  on Parsley Blvd. over I-80 in the 
future, and advised the consultant will also be providing cost estimates for improvements and input 
from citizens will be obtained  during the study process.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Olson explained t he 
project involves a corridor plan for expansion of Parsley Blvd. and that intersections along the 
corridor will be designed per identified needs, but the study  will not go beyond the intersections 
and a study of Pinto Lane will not be conducted as part of the project but input from residents may 
be made on related corridor concerns.  Jeff Dierksen inquired about resident notification methods 
that will be utilized and encouraged consideration that the intersection with Pinto Lane be included 
in the study.  Ms. Olson indicated that social media , newspaper, radio and television media  
advertisements and announcements , email mailing lists, mailings, and a variable message sign 
board along the corridor are notification mechanisms that may be used to advise citizens of public 
meetings that will be held.  Mr. White moved to approve in the amount of $78,419.00, seconded 
by Mr. Johnson.  Mr. White moved to amend by substitu te dated May 7, 2018, seconded by Mr. 
  
Johnson.  Ms. Olson explained  the amendment is for an address clerical correction contained in  
the scope of work area.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with 
the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. (#6831) 
 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Amazing Amusements for an ATM machine 
at the Cheyenne Ice and Events Center  (revenue to City).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  
(#6832) 
 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Amazing Amusements for game machines 
at the Cheyenne Ice and Events Center (revenue to City).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  
(#6833) 
 
[CA] Purchase and Sale Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Linda M. Moran, Gail L. 
Anderson and Vici Rae Foster, for real property to be used for the Christensen Road Project (1% 
Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed 
$13,000.00). (#6834) 
 
 Purchase and Sale Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Sung Thik  Lim, for real 
property located at 308 West 17th Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to be used as the site for the 
construction of a municipal courthouse (1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax).  Joe Dougherty 
requested information on due diligence that has been conducted and any estimated costs that have 
been acquired since the governing body approved the property as the site of the new Municipal 
Court building, and commented in support of utilizing the building’s original facade if possible 
due to the building’s historic value.  In response to his request, Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public 
Works, reviewed evaluations and site analysis that have been done to date, including the status of 
environmental and asbestos assessments.  She advised the estimated cost for si ngle level 
construction is $6,650,000.00 for a 10,500 square foot building, and noted total remediation costs 
involving the environmental issues are yet to be received.  Mr. White moved to approve the 
Purchase and Sale Agreement in an amount not to exceed $275,000.00, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  
Discussion followed and included the following topics:  Environmental studies; unknown costs 
affiliated with demolition and construction, and conditions contained in Section 6 of the 
agreement.  Upon inquiry, Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney, confirmed the purchase price to be paid to 
the property owner, and that, upon the City taking ownership of the property, the City assumes 
remediation costs.  Mr. Case, seconded by Mr. Shanor, moved to amend Section 6.2 of the 
Agreement by inserting the phrase “upon Council approval” so the section would read as follows:  
In the event any of the foregoing conditions precedent are not fulfilled as of Closing (or earlier 
date where specified otherwise), then the City shall have the option, upon Council approval, of 
either: (i) waiving the condition and closing “as is” without reduction in the Purchase Price or 
claim against Seller therefore provided that Seller assigns to the City all of Seller’s right, title and 
interest to the title insurance policy it obtained when it acquired the Property, or (ii) cancelling this 
Agreement by written notice to Seller.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Hackl advised legal staff had not 
reviewed the proposed amendment wording but that she did not see a problem with it if the Seller, 
or Seller’s representative, had no objection.  Chuck West, agent for the Seller, expressed his belief 
that the Seller would be acceptable to the proposed amendment.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting 
  
“yes” – Mr. Case, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Ro ybal, Mr. Shanor, Mr. White and Mayor Orr.  
Voting “no” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson and Mr. Laybourn.  Main motion as amended carried.  
Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, Mr. Luna, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor, Mr. White and Mayor Orr.  Voting 
“no” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne. (#6835) 
 
[CA] Contract for group health insurance for City of Cheyenne employees.  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried (approve proposal from Blue Cross/Blue Shield). (#6836) 
 
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Delta Dental for group dental benefits for City 
employees.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6837)

(#5930)

Companies, Baton Rouge, Louisiana, to e xtend the term of lease for placement of billboards on
