[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 6743, Professional Services Agr eement 
between the City of Cheyenne and River Oaks Communications Corporation, Centennial, 
Colorado, for drafting and negotiation of Charter Communications Cable Franchise renewal and 
CenturyLink Cable Television franchise agreements .  Motion (per Consent A genda) carried. 
(#6743) 
 
 Consideration of Bid #E-18-18 for furnishing one new 2017 -2018 front end wheel loader 
for the Street and Alley Division (2015 -2018 1% Sales Tax). Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public 
Works, provided information on the content of bid specifications and subsequent addendums that 
had been issued; pre-bid meeting and bid opening date and those in attendance; response by bidders 
relative to each bidder’s proposed equipment and the bid process that was followed.  Ms. Nemecek 
reviewed specific bid specifications, including the requirement of a manual locking differential, 
compared with bid responses received, and noted acknowledgment was received by all bidders 
that they had received the bid addendums.  She discussed key issues that were revi ewed during 
review of the bids received and stated the bidder meeting all bid specifications was Titan 
Machinery, Inc., the third lowest bidder.  Keith Cashman, Wyoming Machinery, commented on 
and explained reasons for the equipment his company had bid involving a limited slip differential, 
not a manual locking differential as contained in one of the addendums, and noted attendance at 
the pre-bid meeting was not mandatory.  Mr. White reported the motion to accept the bid from 
Wyoming Machinery of Cheyenne i n an amount not to exceed $227,156.48 failed at Finance 
Committee, therefore, there was no recommendation on the item from the Committee.  Mr. Luna 
moved to accept the bid submitted by Wyoming Machinery of Cheyenne in the amount of 
$227,156.48, seconded by  Mr. Case.  Discussion followed and included the following topics:  
Difference between a manual locking differential and a limited slip differential and staff’s 
recommendation of a manual locking differential; operational needs involving acquisition of the  
equipment; compilation of bid specification information; Wyoming Machinery vs. Titan 
Machinery equipment differences, and pre -bid meetings and bid process generally.  During 
discussion and upon inquiry, Ms. Nemecek provided additional information regardin g the bids 
received.  Motion failed.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Luna.  Voting 
“no” – Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor, Mr. White and Mayor Orr.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to accept the bid submitted by Titan Machinery, In c., in the amount of $257,621.67, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Discussion followed on the option of rejecting all bids and the timeframe 
involved to re -bid the equipment; bid process consistency; making pre -bid meetings mandatory 
for attendance by interested bi dders, and the average number of bids that are issued by Public 
Works for heavy machinery per year.  Motion failed (due to a tie vote).  Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, 
Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal, Mr. White and Mayor Orr.  Voting “no” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. 
Laybourn, Mr. Luna and Mr. Shanor.  Mr. Shanor moved to reject all bids and to re -bid the 
equipment, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Pursuant to comment that perhaps the motion wasn’t 
necessary, Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, suggested that officially rejecting all bids  would be the 
procedurally correct way to proceed as there were other bids received not just the bids involved in 
the motions that failed.  Following comment on the need to fine -tune the bid process, motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne 
and Mr. Shanor.  Voting “no” – Mr. Roybal, Mr. White and Mayor Orr. 
 
  
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-20-18 for furnishing front end load containers for the Sanitation 
Division (Solid Waste Management funds) .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accept ed bid 
submitted by Wastequip Manufacturing Co., Inc., Statesville, North Carolina, in an amount not to 
exceed $89,274.35). 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-10-18 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Public Works 
Department, and Modern Electric Co., Casper, Wyoming, for the 2018 Traffic Signal Upgrades 
Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-11-18 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Engineering 
Department, and Simon Contractors, for the 2018 Chip Seal #1 Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) 
– referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-14-18 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Engineering 
Department, and Reiman Corp, for the Civic Center Commons Park Project (1% Specific Purpose 
Sales Tax; FEMA; SLIB; Wyoming Business Council grants) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase Motorola complete vehicle 2 -way radio systems and chargers 
from Wireless Advanced Communications, Evans, Colorado, for the Cheyenne Police Department 
(Homeland Security funds, 2015-2018 1% Sales Tax and 1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax).  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $87,714.60) 
 
 Designation of Official Voting Delegate and Alternate Delegate for the City of Cheyenne 
at the June 14, 2018 Wyoming Association of Municipalities Membership Business Meeting to be 
held in Pinedale, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
Appointment of Astrid to the Cheye nne Downtown Development Authority Board.   Mr. 
Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr . Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no” and Mayor Orr who did not 
vote (Mayoral appointee). 
 
Announcement that due to the Memorial Day holiday on Monday, May 28, 2018, the 
regularly scheduled meeting of the governing body will instead be held on Tuesday, May 29, 2018 
beginning at 6:00 p.m. in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Following a brief recess, Mayor Orr reconvened the meeting at 8:15 p.m. 
 
 Under other business:  
 
Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk,  advised due to timeliness involving a Federal transit grant 
application the following item was being introduced under other business:  Resolution authorizing 
submission of an application to the Wyoming Department of Transportation for a Wyoming Rural 
Public Transportation Program grant to provide the local match for operating funds for the City of 
Cheyenne for its Transit Program, as authorized by 49 U.S.C § 5311.  Mayor Orr referred the item 
to Finance Committee. 
  
 
Major Dorr expressed concern s regarding loss of available nearby parking for persons 
attending events at the Civic Center due to the Civic Center Commons Project, which will 
eliminate the parking lot west of the Municipal Building. 
 
Mr. Johnson expressed appreciation to volunteers who recently helped clean up trash in the 
Saddle Ridge neighborhood, and announced a fundraiser supporting the Depot Plaza splash pad 
feature will be held on Wednesday, May 16, 2018 at the Luxury Diner  with food sale proceeds 
being donated toward the splash pad.  
 
 Stating there was no other business, Mayor Orr adjourned the m eeting but immediately 
reconvened the session, noting she had inadvertently overlooked the need for her to present the 
following to City Council members:  Mayor Orr referenced a written Mayoral veto message (on 
file with City Clerk’s office) she had previo usly provided to City Council members on May 7, 
2018 detailing her objections to Resolution No. 5927, as amended, which was adopted by a 
majority vote of the governing body at the April 23, 2018 City Council meeting. [City Clerk note:  
Resolution No. 5927 is titled as follows:  Resolution – requiring certain conditions associated with 
the rental of the City’s athletic fields by Steve Avila or SAA Enterprises so as to serve and protect 
the interests of the Cheyenne Community.]  Mayor Orr read her veto messag e in its entirety into 
the record and stated, for the record, that she has had many communications or meetings with 
representatives of the Cheyenne Mustangs, Post 6 Baseball and Mr. Avila throughout the past year.  
Mr. Shanor moved to override the Mayor’s veto of Resolution No. 5927, seconded by Mr. Cook.  
Mr. Shanor requested that if any member of the public in attendance at the meeting wished to speak 
on the motion that they be allowed to do so.  Mayor Orr denied the request stating the topic of the 
motion is procedural to be addressed by City Council and does not involve the merits of the 
resolution itself.  Mr. Shanor raised a point of order, appealing the decision of the chair, and moved 
to override the decision of the chair, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Following brief procedural comments, 
motion to override the decision of the chair and allow public comment on the motion to override 
the Mayor’s veto carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. 
Luna, Mr. Shanor and Mr. White.  V oting “no” – Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mayor Orr.  Rick 
Thompson, speaking on behalf of Cheyenne Mustangs, commented in support of the resolution as 
amended and adopted at the April 23, 2018 Council meeting.  Discussion followed and included 
the following topics:  Substance of the Mayor’s veto message; conditions placed on Mr. Avila 
pursuant to the amended resolution that was adopted; enforceability of resolutions; whether Mr. 
Avila will agree to the conditions; authority to impose conditions affiliated wit h athletic field 
rentals; legal entity status of SAA Enterprises; City’s prior monetary sponsorship agreement with 
Cheyenne Mustangs, not Mr. Avila; Cheyenne Mustangs agreement with Mr. Avila, and 
supporting local youth baseball and tournament events.  Dur ing discussion and upon inquiry, 
Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney, advised she had been in communication with Mr. Avila following 
adoption of the resolution, and had emailed him a copy of the resolution, with a cover memo and 
a form she had prepared titled “Ack nowledgment of Receipt of Resolution and Acceptance of 
Conditions Set Forth Therein”, which listed the conditions contained in the resolution for his 
initials and signature.  Ms. Hackl stated in her cover memo she requested a response by May 3 rd 
from Mr. Avila as to whether he would comply with the resolution conditions.  Ms. Hackl noted 
she and the Mayor’s Chief of Staff participated in a telephone call received from M r. Avila on 
  
May 3rd in which he stated he would not sign the form or accept the conditions.  Ms. Hackl shared 
additional information on the content of the telephone conversation, and expressed her 
interpretation that, pursuant to Wyo. Stat. §15 -1-103 (a)(xxii), the City Coun cil does have the 
authority to place terms and conditions on use of the City’s athletic fields through administrative 
Departmental rental guidelines and rental agreements. Motion to override the veto carried.  Voting 
“yes” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Shanor.  Voting “no” – 
Mr. Johnson, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White.  [City Clerk note:  State statute and City Code prohibits 
a Mayor from voting on their own veto message.  Passage of a motion to override the Mayor’s 
veto requires a minimum 2/3rds affirmative vote of the qualified members of City Council (i.e. 9 
members).] 
 
 There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 9:04 p.m. 
  
 
        Submitted by, 
 
             
        Carol Intlekofer, MMC 
        City Clerk 
   
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

MEETING: Special Meeting of the Governing Body | DATE: 2018-05-16 | CLIP_ID: 656 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 656

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
 
Office of City Clerk 
 
May 16, 2018 
 
 A special meeting of t he Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne was held on this  date 
beginning at 6: 35 p.m. (immediately following a special City Council Finance Committee 
meeting) in City Council Chambers for the purpose of acting upon the following:  Consideration 
of Bid #S -14-18 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Engineering Department, and 
Reiman C orp, for the Civic Center Commons Park Project (1% Specific Purpos e Sales Tax; 
FEMA, SLIB; Wyoming Business Council grants).  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; 
COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Richard Johnson , Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Absent:  Bryan Cook.  Also present:  Carol 
Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve the bid submitted by 
Reiman Corp, including Alternates A and C, in an amount not to exceed $6,833,524.21, seconded 
by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with 
the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”. (#6839) 
 
Mayor Orr stated the purpose of the special meeting had been met, and there being no 
business previously noted for the special meeting, adjourned the meeting at 6:38 p.m. 
 
      Submitted by, 
 
 
       Carol Intlekofer, MMC 
       City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2018-05-29 | CLIP_ID: 657 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 657

  
 
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
May 29, 2018 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  COUNCIL PRESIDENT – Dr. Mark Rinne ; 
COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, 
Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White . Absent: Mayor Marian J. Orr .  Also present: Carol 
Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney. In the absence of Mayor Orr, Council 
President Rinne chaired the meeting.  [City Clerk’s note:  As chair  and with the exception of the 
vote taken on the main motion for Agenda Item #9, an ordinance on third and final reading , 
President Rinne exercised his option, as entitled by state statute, to not cast a vote on agenda items].  
The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Expression of Appreciation to the 2017 -2018 Mayor’s Youth Council and State of the 
Youth Address.  Jan Spires, Youth Alternatives and staff advisor to the Youth Council, expressed 
appreciation to Youth Council members, and introduced Julia Horst, Mayor’s Youth Council chair 
for 2017-2018.  Ms. Horst presented the State of the Youth address, and provided information on 
various accomplishments, projects and programs the group participated in throughout the year . 
Ms. Spires and Eric Fountain, Mayor’s Chief of Staff, presented Certificates of Appreciation to 
the following Youth Council members: Alexis Arp, Maggie Black, McCoy Bush, Ashen Doty, 
Raegan Downham, Dylan Fertig, Julia Horst, Keiran Loughran, Emily Lucero, Nickolas Metheny, 
Kaylee Sanchez, Ken itra Shanks, Dominick Singleton and Ga rrett Vickery. President Rinne 
expressed appreciation to the group and their parents for their participation  and support 
respectively. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on t he agenda.)  Mr. Luna moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded 
by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  present with 
the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no” and President Rinne who did not vote. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on May 14, 2018 .  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on May 16, 2018 .  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Roybal declared a conflict (business interest in voucher payable to Cowboy D odge, 
Inc.) and vacated Council Chambers .  Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouche rs as 
  
presented, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present in Council Chambers with the exception of President Rinne who did not vote.   
Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Roybal returned to Council Chambers. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fee 
Schedule.  Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works, provided information about the proposed 
expansion (adding an additional route) of the yard waste program, and advised she had no objection 
to not expanding the program if that was the desire of the g overning body.  Ms. Nemecek, upon 
inquiry, discussed the 2014 HDR rate study and information on the yard waste program, which 
has about 375 future customers on a waiting list, and elements involved with adding one additional 
yard waste pick-up route.  Jeffrey Dierksen expressed concerns about the yard waste program and 
his belief that existing customers shouldn’t subsidize a new route. Upon inquiry by Mr. Dierksen, 
President Rinne advised the sanitation fee schedule is reviewed annually.  Reporting for Finance 
Committee, Mr. White reported there was no recommendation on the item from the Committee.  
Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Laybourn.  Mr. Shanor, 
seconded by Mr. Roybal,  moved to amend by adding  a Note 16 at the end of the schedule as 
follows: No revenues realized from the 5% sanitation fee increase may be used on payroll until 
such time as the construction and equipment objectives identified in the 2014 HDR st udy have 
been completed in full.  Discussion included: Construction and equipment objectives; solid waste 
enterprise fund; past fee increases; landfill expansion and closure costs; vehicle replacements and 
sanitation employee salary increases.   During discussion, Ms. Nemecek responded to questions 
involving sanitation and landfill operations, expenses and budget; anticipated future fee increases, 
and the landfill plan, and commented on the 3% pay for performance employee funds included in 
the FY2019 solid waste budget and the need to retain employees.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr.  Laybourn voting 
“no” and President Rinne who did not vote.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present (including President Rinne) with the exception of Mr.  
Johnson and Mr. Luna voting “no”. (#4229) 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Approving and establishing revised water and sewer 
rates, tap and system development fees, administrative fees, service charges and penalties, and 
septic and commercial waste dump rates, as recommended by the Board of Public Utilities.  Randy 
Hays, Board of Public Utilities (BOPU), submitted a hand -out (on file with City Clerk’s office) 
which he reviewed regarding the Board’s recommendation for a 3% rate increase for both water 
and sewer funds, and explained factors involved in proposing the rate  adjustments.  Mr. Hays 
provided information on how rates are assessed for multi-family vs single family residential units 
and, upon inquiry, noted the reasons for decreases in funding amounts for operational and 
maintenance, and capital expenditures affiliated with BOPU’s proposed FY2019 budget.  Jeff rey 
Dierksen questioned location identification of lead pipes relative to replacement of water mains, 
and the different rate tiers for multiple vs. single family residences with Mr. Hays providing 
information in response.  Astrid commented on fair allocation rate structures , suggesting rates be 
based upon the size of a residence, and reviewing older portions of the City to determine the 
location and replacement of lead pipes.  Mr. White moved to approve on second reading, seconded 
by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes’ – all members of the governing body present with the 
exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no” and President Rinne who did not vote. 
  
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, for the various purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing 
the amount of general and special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said 
appropriation, all for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2018 and ending June 30, 2019. Stephanie 
Lowe, President, Cheyenne Public Employees Association, spoke in support of the proposed 3% 
employee pay for performance funding contained in the budget, noting a performance based plan 
will assist with retaining employees.  Officer Manny Fardell a, Cheyenne Police Department, 
commented in support of competitive wages; consideration of needs vs. wants; 3% performance 
pay, and the value of employees.  Reporting for Committee of the Whole, Mr. Case moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. Case moved to amend by removing 
$100,000.00 from reserves and placing it into the Abandoned Building line item, seconded by Mr. 
Cook.  Discussion included use of taxpayer’s funds vs. neighborhoods taking care of blighted 
properties through suc h mechanisms as property covenants; utilizing funding from recurring 
revenue instead of reserves; enforcing existing codes relative to blighted properties, and limited 
staffing levels currently existing for addressing nuisance violations and abatements thr ough the 
Code Enforcement office.  During discussion Ron Downey, City Treasurer, provided information 
on possible additional funding sources for the Fight the Blight program (i.e. Abandoned Building 
line item).  Motion failed.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Laybourn and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Case, 
Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Luna, Mr. Roybal and Mr. Shanor.  (President  Rinne did not vote).  
Mr. Case moved to amend by removing $21,000.00 from reserves and placing it into the Animal 
Shelter line item, seconded by  Mr. Cook.  Discussion included utilizing funding from recurring 
revenue instead of reserves, and Animal Shelter expenses and reduced hours in which it is open to 
the public.  During discussion and upon inquiry, Bob Fecht, Director, Cheyenne Animal Shelter , 
provided information on methods that had been reviewed to meet available budgetary funding vs. 
Shelter needs; decision to reduce hours, and plans to hire a second veterinarian.  Motion failed.  
Voting “no” – all members of  the governing body present except President Rinne who did not 
vote.  Discussion followed on departmental supplemental budget requests noted in the working 
budget book, specifically Fire and Rescue Department requests; amount of funding that would be 
needed to include supplemental reques ts in the budget; inclusion of revenue in the budget from 
proposed sale of city property that has not yet been approved for sale; funding from reserves vs. 
recurring revenue, and employee raises and benefits.  Upon inquiry during discussion, Chief Greg 
Hoggatt, Fire and Rescue Department, provided information on supplemental requests for his 
department involving equipment replacements; plans to create a life cycle value for equipment; 
lease/option to purchase equipment scenario; department overtime and trai ning; and response to 
calls through dispatch involving Fire and Rescue staff as well as ambulance response.  Mr. Shanor 
moved to amend by removing the approximately $771,818.00 proposed to be expended on merit 
(i.e. pay for performance) raises in the stand ard budget and removing the entire $6,534,851.00 
proposed in supplemental requests, seconded by Mr. Case.  Detective Aaron Willmarth, Police 
Protective Association, and Ms. Lowe commented in support of the value of City employees and 
pay for performance be nefits.  Discussion included the pay for performance plan, which is still 
being developed by the administration, and implementation timeframe.  Mr. Downey and Eric 
Fountain, Mayor’s Chief of Staff, provided information in response to inquiries regarding th e 
performance plan and status regarding implementation, with Mr. Downey noting that, if revenues 
are appropriated to do so, the budget could be supplemented at a later date to implement the plan.  
  
Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook , Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna, Mr. 
Shanor and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Johnson and Mr. Roybal (President Rinne did not vote).  
Mr. Shanor moved to amend by adding the following expenditures into the standard budget 
request, seconded by Mr. Luna:  Add $16,2 68.00 in Facilities and Maintenance for what was 
identified as a FY2018 budgetary insufficiency; add in Police Admin proposed supplemental items 
of $65,156.00; add in Police Patrol proposed supplemental items of $292,471.00; Fire Admin add 
$45,126.00; Fire Training add $50,000.00; Fire Suppression add $125,000.00 for contractual wage 
adjustment and overtime; City Treasurer professional services add $30,000.00; to transfer an 
engineer position currently in the 5th penny budget to the City Engineer budget and add $33,000.00 
(other half in 5th penny funds); Planning and Development historic preservation grant matches add 
$6,000.00; economic support to Animal Shelter add $21,000.00, and add $50,000.00 for Fight the 
Blight efforts, for a total of approximately $741,021.00, and that the City Treasurer may make any 
adjustments needed due to any inaccuracies in the numbers enumerated in the amendment as well 
as if the expenditures do not add up to the proposed revenues the treasurer will reduce his sales tax 
estimate accordingly to match revenues to expenditures.  Discussion on the amendment included 
the following:  Verification of funding amount of $40,000.00 needed for the City Engineer 
position; areas included in funding for Police Admin and Police Patrol line item s, including two 
additional police officer positions, and clarification of Patrol funding amount of $292,473.00 not 
$292,471.00; Fight the Blight program funding sources, and resources for nuisance enforcement.  
Upon inquiry, Mr. Downey provided informatio n on potential Community Development Block 
Grant funds that may be available for the Fight the Blight program.  Upon inquiry during 
discussion, Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works, stated her concurrence with the proposed 
amendment regarding the Facili ties and Maintenance budget but indicated an additional 
$10,000.00 is needed in the Public Works Admin budget for the City’s fleet vehicle pool costs .  
Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department, Chief Hoggatt, and Mr. Downey also concurred with the 
proposed amendments relative to their respective budgets.  Mr. Downey clarified the general fund 
expenditure amount budgeted for merit based increases was $525,000.00 and the total of 
$771,818.00 for merit increases included other funds (i.e. 5th penny, 6th penny, sanitation and fleet, 
etc.).  Mr. Shanor moved to amend the amendment by adding $10,000.00 to the Public Works 
Admin budget and increasing the amount of $33,000.00 to $40,000.00 for the City Engineer 
position line item, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Downey advised adjustment would 
need to be made to the budget (due to previously approved amendment and the proposed 
amendments, if approved) to add an additional $525,000.00 in expenditures to the general fund.  
Motion to amend the amendment carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no” and President Rinne who did not vote.  
Motion to approve the amendment, as amended, carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no” and President Rinne who 
did not vote.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Downey advised with the amendments the general fund budget 
was short by $246,525.00.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend as follows, seconded by Mr. Case:  Create 
a one-time revenue line item with an amount of $1,000,000.00 from res erves; create a one -time 
expenditure line item with $1,000,000.00 from reserves to be spent as follows:  $629,000.00 to 
Fire Suppression for self-contained breathing apparatus and other necessary equipment as deemed 
necessary by the Fire Chief; the remainder, approximately $371,000.00, to be spent at the Mayor’s 
discretion only on items identified in the proposed 2019 supplemental budget and of those items, 
only ones that are not payroll related; that the City’s total full -time employee count be limited to 
  
570 for FY2019, and for the Mayor to find the $246,525.00 in recurring revenues and recurring 
expenditure decreases to balance the general fund budget as well as to account for the other funds 
that were removed for merit raises to find another expenditure in those line items other than the 
merit raises.  Discussion included Fire Chief discretion and authorization to utilize the $629,000.00 
as he deemed appropriate.  Friendly amendment was offered by Mr. Roybal and accepted by Mr. 
Shanor and Mr. Case, as the maker and second to the motion to amend, to add “at his discretion” 
at the end of the phrase “as deemed necessary by the Fire Chief”.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no” 
and President Rinne who did not vote. 
 
Following a 10 -minute recess, President Rinne reconvened the meeting at 8:41 p.m.  
Motion to approve the budget ordinance as amended four times carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body prese nt with the exception  of Mr. Johnson voting “no” and 
President Rinne who did not vote. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments,

 RESOLUTION – Expressing the City of Cheyenne’s desire and commitment to facilitating 
commercial passenger air service at the Cheyenne Regional  Airport both now and in the future . 
Mr. Johnson declared a conflict of interest (business interest) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. 
White moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. White moved to amend the “Now therefore 
be it resolved” clause by adding the words “safe and reliable” in the 3 rd line following the word 
“support”, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Wendy Volk, Cheyenne Regional Air Focus Team (CRAFT), 
provided information on CRAFT ; spoke in support of the resolution and commented on 
community benefits involving local passenger air service.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers with the exception of President  
Rinne who did not vote .  Discussion included:  P urpose of the resolution ; new voter approved 
airport terminal currently under construction; local partnerships/stakeholders; facilitating air 
passenger service for economic development and community benefits, and widespread community 
collaborative efforts to provide commercial passenger air service.  It was noted that if the resolution 
was adopted, payment would not be allocated until discussions between all partnership and 
stakeholders were completed.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body present in Council Chambers with the exception of President Rinne who did 
not vote .  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Johnson returned to Council Chambers. 
(#5930)

 Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Asbestos and 
Technical Services, Inc., for services for the demolition and disposal of the residential structure 
located at 815 Capitol Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. White moved to approve in an amount 
not to exceed $43,000.00, seconded by Mr. Case.  Upon inquiry, Eric Fountain, Mayor’s Chief of 
Staff, explained potential long-term community and neighborhood benefits of re -developing the 
property may off-set demolition costs.  Ron Downey, City Treasurer, concurred that the chance of 
recouping demolition costs would be minimal . Discussion included:  Public health and safety 
issues involving blighted properties within the community, and status of any liens upon the 
property.  Mr. Fountain stated the only known lien on the property was a tax lien.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Johnson 
voting “no” and President Rinne who did not vote. (#6827) 
 
 Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Noel Griffith Jr. & 
Associates Architects for analytical and design  engineering for the construction of a new 
Municipal Court at 308 West 17 th Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming (1% Specific Purpose Sale s Tax).  
Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works, explained the importance of acquiring a professional 
consultant for the project and reviewed the project’s timeline of events resulting in the agreement 
being submitted for the agenda.  She provided information on the Phase I environmental site 
assessment; recommendation to have a structural engineer conduct an assessment,  and the st ate 
  
asbestos program, and responded to inquiries regarding the project, condition of the building and 
contract services provided by TriHydro.  Discussion included:  Acquiring additional information 
prior to making a determination on the agreement; a nalysis and assessment of abatement costs; 
estimated overall project cost; services from a structural engineer and developing a scope of work 
relative to a structural assessment ; asbestos abatement , and terms of the property purchase 
agreement relative to the closing timeframe.  During discussion, Ms. Nemecek and Amy Allen, 
City Engineer, provided information on structural engineer services vs. environmental engineer 
services, and coordination with the state asbestos program staff.  Noel Griffith, Noel Griffith Jr. & 
Associates Architects,  explained the fees and services involved with the proposed agreement; 
stated there is a structural engineer affiliated with his firm  who c ould conduct the structural 
assessment and help determine the asbestos remediation issue but they need a contract first to move 
forward.  He expressed his belief the entire project should be able t o be completed with in the 
amount of funding available  and, upon inquiry, advised he would be able to provide an estimate 
for the financial cost of acquiring a structural engineer assessment and anticipated asbestos 
removal cost prior to the next City Council meeting.  Upon inquiry, Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney, 
advised the property purchase and sale agreement wasn’t fully executed by both parties until today, 
so timeframe requirements  contained in the purchase agreement haven’t started yet, and she is 
working with the property owner’s agent on a closing schedule.  Mr. White reported there was no 
recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.   Mr. Roybal moved to approve  (in the 
amount of $657,500.00), seconded by Mr. Case. Mr. Shanor moved to postpone to June 11, 2018, 
seconded by Mr. Roybal. Comment followed in support of the postponement to allow time for Mr. 
Griffith to assist with a potential amendment to the agreement.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of President Rinne who did 
not vote.

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2018-06-11 | CLIP_ID: 659 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 659

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
June 11, 2018 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White .  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, 
and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Roybal, and pursuant to supp ort received 
by Mr. Cook  and Mr. White , Agenda Item #18 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon 
request by Mr. Johnson, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Cook and Dr. Rinne, Agenda 
Item #30(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Luna moved to approve remaining 
Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body.

 Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Johnson.

ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Approving and establishing revised water and sewer 
rates, tap and system development fees, administrative fees, service charges and penalties, and 
septic and commercial waste dump rates, as recommended b y the Board of Public Utilities.  
Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on third and final  reading, 
seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes’ – all members of the governing body with 
the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”. (#4230) 
  
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, for the various purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing 
the amount of general and special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said 
appropriation, all for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2018 and ending June 30, 2019. Stephanie 
Lowe, President, Cheyenne Public Employees Association  (CPEA), spoke in support of the 
proposed 3% allocation affiliated with implementation of  a merit based pay plan, citing the need 
for competitive wages, and commented in support of services provided by City employees.   She 
noted CPEA  will be working with the administration on implementing the pay plan.  Susan a 
Montana also spoke in favor of being able to reward employees through a merit increase program 
and encouraged support for the 3% evaluation plan.  Reporting for Committee of the Whole, Dr. 
Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Dr. Rinne moved to 
amend by initiating a 3% merit-based pay increase for all non-uniformed employees allocated only 
after adoption and implementation of a merit-based pay increase plan to be taken from individual 
funds as appropriate from the general fund, seconded by Mr. Luna.  D r. Rinne moved to amend 
the amendment by adding the following language after the word “plan” in the 3rd line: “as approved 
by the Governing Body”, seconded by Mr. Case.  Upon inquiry, Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney, 
confirmed no merit pay raises will be given until the pay for performance plan is approved by the 
governing body.  Discussion included: Time frame in which the Mayor anticipates submitting the 
plan to the governing body; patterning the plan upon the recent plan implemented by the Board of 
Public Utilities (BOPU) and sharing of resources on the performance plan between BOPU and the 
City; concern with approving funding for the plan prior to approval of the plan by the governing 
body; purpose of the plan to provide an evaluation system not just a monet ary increase system, 
and implementation of the plan by December 31, 2018.  During discussion, Dr. Rinne raised a 
point of order that comments should be limited to the motion to amend the amendment on the floor 
with Mayor Orr ruling comments could continue.   During discussion, Darrin Hass, Deputy 
Director, Human Resources, stated compilation and implementation of the plan is on an aggressive 
schedule with an anticipated implementation date of December 31, 2018.  Motion to amend the 
amendment carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Comment of clarification 
was made that not all employees may receive a 3% increase under the new plan.  Motion to amend, 
as amended, carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Dr. Rinne moved t o 
amend by initiating the on-going maintenance of the downtown trees in the amount of $110,000.00 
which includes one full-time arborist and a portion of a 45 -foot aerial lift truck to be taken from 
5th penny sales tax fund overage collections, seconded by Mr. Laybourn.  Discussion topics 
included:  Concern with paying a salary position from 5 th penny funds; new arborist position and 
affiliation with the ordinance amending the tree chapter in City Code also on the current agenda 
(Item #13), and removal of th e position in the past budget.  Upon inquiry, Ron Downey, City 
Treasurer, stated 5 th penny funds are an addition to the general fund and provided a brief 
explanation of future funding.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, Mr. Johnson, 
Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor and Mayor Orr.  Voting “no” – Mr. Cook, Dr. 
Rinne and Mr. Shanor.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by increasing the full -time employee cap 
number from 568 to 570 to include an additional arborist for the Forestry Division  and a senior 
heavy equipment operator for the Compost Division, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion to amend 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Discussion followed and included 
the following topics:  S upplemental department budget re quests; deferred maintenance needs ; 
  
funding amount of $250,000.00 contained in the proposed Public Works budget for on -going 
maintenance needs, and keeping the former “model” of a budget line item for capital improvements 
and deferred maintenance in the ge neral fund .  During discussion and upon inquiry, Vicki 
Nemecek, Director of Public Works, confirmed solid waste department employees were included 
in the 570 employee count; Mr. Downey advised any monies left in the current capital 
improvements fund budget  would be transferred to reserves at the end of the current fiscal year 
but could be reallocated through re-appropriation, and Mayor Orr provided information generally 
on the budget and proposed funding of $250,000.00 as a start in addressing maintenance needs.  
Mr. Shanor moved to amend by moving $250,000.00 from the facilities maintenance line item 
contained in the propose d Public Works budget; r e-create the capital improvement line item for 
the FY2019 budget and carry over any remaining balance in that line item from this fiscal year 
into next year’s budget, and include the $250,000.00 in the FY2019 capital improvement line item 
for use toward deferred maintenance and identified needs affiliated with departmental FY2019 
supplemental budget requests, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Mayor Orr advised the funding was 
placed in the Public Works budget as that department oversees faci lities, which has the greatest 
maintenance needs.  Discussion included:  Average amount of funding in the capital improvement 
line item; retaining $78,000 in the Public Works facilities budget for payment of annual 
maintenance requirements and other necess ities for facilities; remaining funds in the capital 
improvement line item for the current budget; need for the state legislature to reform the tax system 
to allow for more stable funding sources for municipalities to address infrastructure needs; 
prioritizing maintenance needs for use of the funding; current Capital Improvement Plan (CIP), 
and providing the Mayor and department directors flexibility in requesting real -time 
recommendations for use of the money as opposed to “locking into” the CIP.   During discussion 
and upon inquiry, Ms. Nemecek advised the $250,000.00 was an additional request to the budget 
and that $78,000.00 should remain in the facilities maintenance line item to pay for annual 
maintenance responsibilities and other facilities maintenanc e necessities.  Mr. Downey provided 
information on the amount of funding in the current budget for capital improvements 
($820,000.00) that would go into reserves but could be re -appropriated and the amount of 
$328,765.00 for capital improvements in the proposed FY2019 budget.  Mayor Orr commented on 
the lack of adoption of the CIP by the governing body.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  
The following is a summary of the proposed budget , including the aforementioned amendments : 
General Fund, $ 53,750,797; Weed and Pest, $58 3,147; Youth Alternatives Grants, $ 635,008; 
Development Impact Fees, $ 151,200; Recreation Programs, $1,1 59,555; Community 
Development Block Grant, $ 398,422; Law Enforcement Grants, $ 221,584; Transportation 
Planning, $870,097; FTA Transit Grant, $2, 447,162; Juvenile Justice, $ 73,037; Special Friends, 
$77,394; Speci fic Purpose Option Tax, One Percent Sales Tax 2015 -18, $11,185,325; Youth 
Activities/Ropes, $18,000; Golf Facilities, $201,000; Fleet Maintenance, $3,949,084; Solid Waste 
Management, $16,573,784; Civic Center, $ 2,177,792; Ice & Events Center, $644,760; Perpetual 
Care, $2,000; Total City, $9 5,119,148; Water Enterprise, $2 4,070,900; Sewer Enterprise, 
$13,081,450; Total Appropriation, $132,271,498. (#4231)

ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Repealing in its entirety Chapter 12.16, Trees and 
Shrubs, and creating a new Chapter 12.16, Trees, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, 
of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. White moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Discussion topics included:  Past maintenance responsibility for care 
of trees in the downtown area; tree replacement costs; expertise that is needed involving 
maintenance knowledge and diseased and dangerous trees; funding of a new staff position in Urban 
Forestry affiliated with the ordinance; Urban Forestry guidelines , purpose and promulgation (i.e. 
Wyoming Administrative Procedure Act) of same; City forestry staff services on public and private 
property; use of revenue from fees that are generated; improved notification process to property 
owners; local arborists’ knowledge of the proposed ordinance, and simplified provisions and 
additional flexibility provided through the proposed ordinance.  During discuss ion, Alessandra 
McCoy Fakelman, Assistant City Attorney, and Mark Ellison, City Forester, provided information 
in response to inquiries.  Motion to approve carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body with the exception of Mr. Shanor voting “no”.

 Consideration of RFP-16-18 for a contract for  furnishing and installing a parking access, 
permit and revenue control system.   Captain Nathan Buseck, Police Department, reviewed the 
project’s history, including reorganization of the parking patrol staff, parking structure permits and 
parking enforcement which are now a part of Police Department operations.  Captain Buseck 
provided information on proposed improvements and processes through implementation of the 
proposed new system, citing new technologies that will be incorporated; Parking Transition Team; 
public communications on the proposed system; RFP responses and elements of the recommended 
proposal to be accepted that will provide better enforcement and revenue management and more 
user convenience to customers; anticipated contract costs;  revenue assistance for paying parking 
structure revenue bonds, and use of license plate reader cameras and affiliated software.  Vicki 
Nemecek spoke in support of the new system; Astrid, owner of the Plains Hotel, expressed 
concerns about parking in the d owntown area, and Thomas Reddington expressed his belief that 
free parking should be considered.  Upon inquiries by governing body members, Captain Buseck 
responded with additional information on current parking enforcement and parking permit 
procedures, clarifying that the fee structure was not changing, and components of the proposed 
new updated system involving technology.  Mr. White moved to approve (proposal from Passport 
Labs, Inc., Charlotte, North Carolina) in an amount not to exceed $574,002.58, se conded by Mr. 
Case.  Discussion included system costs vs . unknown revenue amounts that may be generated 
through use of the new system; current manual “chalking” that is conducted for enforcing overtime 
parking violators; purpose of the new system , and post poning the item to receive additional 
information.  Mr. Case moved to postpone to June 25, 2018, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. 
Reddington inquired about public comment opportunities if the item were postponed with Mayor 
Orr advising of the dates and times of the next Finance Committee and City Council meeting.  
Upon inquiry, Logan Sharpe, City Attorney’s office, advised a postponement would not affect 
provisions of the RFP and affiliated contract.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. 
Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna and Dr. Rinne.  Voting “no” – Mr. Roybal, 
Mr. Shanor, Mr. White and Mayor Orr. 
 
  
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -28-18 for furnishing new computers and operating systems for 
the City of Cheyenne through the Information Technology Division (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) -- 
referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-29-18 for furnishing 18 police vehicles with equipment installed 
for the Cheyenne Police Department (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) -- referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -26-18 for furnishing one new 2019 cab and chassis, 4x4 with 
utility body installed for the City of Cheyenne, Traffic Di vision (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) -- 
referred to Finance Committee. 
 
Appointment of Frank Gerstenkorn to the City of Cheyenne Personnel Commission.   Dr. 
Rinne moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Roybal. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (Mayoral appointee). 
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, June 25, 2018 at 6:00 
p.m. in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a transfer of retail 
liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s office for Stults Management LLC d/b/a The 
Office Bar and Eatery, 1600 E. Pershing Blvd., Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently held by 
Robert Moberly d/b/a Bob’s Place, 1600 E. Pershing Blvd.). 
 
 Announcement was made that two Calcuttas, benefitting Cheyenne area charities, will  be 
held as follows:  Thursday, July 19, 2018 beginning at 7:00 p.m. at the Redwood Lounge, 2105 
East Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming, in conjunction with the 2018 Cheyenne Frontier Days 
Wild Horse Races, and Friday, July 27, 2018 beginning at 5:30 p.m. in t he Headquarters tent 
located in front of the Old West Museum, 4601 North Carey Avenue, Frontier Park, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming (10% of proceeds to bona fide charities). 
 
 Under other business:  
 
 Mr. Laybourn expressed appreciation for efforts made for budget pla nning sessions with 
staff in preparation of the FY2019 budget .  Mayor Orr thanked City Council members for their 
participation in the additional budget work sessions.   
 
Mr. Johnson expressed appreciation to those who attended the fundraiser at Danielsmark  
for the Depot Splash Pad Project, and Mr. Case noted approximately $2,000 has been raised so far 
in private funding toward the project. 
 
Mr. Johnson commented on issues current , as well as past, governing bodies and 
administrations face regarding the econ omy, budgets and municipal funding; community issues, 
and decision making, and thanked co-governing body members and City staff for their efforts. 
 
 There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 8:58 p.m. 
   
  
        Submitted by, 
             
        Carol Intlekofer, MMC 
        City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2018-06-25 | CLIP_ID: 661 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 661

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
June 25, 2018 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott 
Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White. Absent: Rocky Case.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City 
Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, announced agenda item #26(d) was 
inadvertently placed on the Consent Agenda and, therefore, sh ould not be included as a Consent 
Agenda item.  Mr. Johnson declared a conflict of interest (personal interest in Consent Agenda 
item #19) and vacated Council Chambers.  Upon request by Mr. Roybal, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Roybal and Mr. White, Agenda Item #20 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  
Upon request by Mr. White, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Cook and Dr. Rinne, Agenda 
Item #18 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Luna moved to approve remaining Consent 
Agenda items, seconded by Mr.  Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body  present in Council Chamber s with the exception of Dr.  Rinne voting “no”.  
Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Johnson returned to Council Chambers. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on June 11 , 2018.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Roybal declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Cowboy 
Dodge, Inc.) and vacated Council Chambers.   Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouche rs 
as presented, seconded by  Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Roybal 
returned to Council Chambers. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for a transfer of retail 
liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s office for Stults Management LLC d/b/a The 
Office Bar & Eatery, 1600 E. Pershing Boulevard, Cheyenne, Wyoming (License currently held 
by Robert Moberly d/b/a Bob’s Place, 1600 E. Pershing Boulevard, Cheyenne, Wyoming).  There 
being no comments or input and  purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the 
hearing and referred the application to Finance Committee. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Defining the terms and conditions of employment for 
non-uniformed employees of the City of Che yenne for Fisc al Year 2018 -2019.  Reporting for 
  
Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on third and final  reading, seconded by Mr.  
Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4233) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Defining the terms and conditions of employment for 
sworn officers of the Cheyenne Police Department for Fiscal Year 2018-2019.  Mr. White moved 
to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr . Cook.  Mr. White moved to  amend by 
substitute dated June 18, 2018, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department, 
explained the substitute is due to the recently approved FY2019 budget which changed Exhibit B 
affiliated with Sergeant salary increases and the Department’s pay for performance plan.  Motion 
to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.   Main motion as 
amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4234) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING repealing in its entirety Chapter 12.16, Trees and Shrubs, 
and creating a new Chapter 12.16, Trees, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of the 
Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. White moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  
present with the exception of Mr. Cook voting “no”. (#4235) 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending various chapters and sections contained 
within Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
pertaining to notarization of applicant signatures.  Mr. White moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr.  Luna. Motion carried.   Upon inquiry, Carol Intlekofer , City Clerk,  explained 
removing the notarization requirement will not affect the ability to conduct  required applicant 
background checks.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Section 5.12.140, Persons under the age of 
twenty-one (21) years – Restrictions – Provisions, of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic Liquor and Malt 
Beverages, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Mr. White moved to approve on 
second reading, seconded by Mr.  Johnson. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments,  
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from MUB Mixed-Use Business Emphasis 
to CB Community Business for Lots 1 and 2, Block 1, Simpson Estates , Cheyenne, Wyoming 
(located at the southwest corner of the intersection of College Drive and Cheyenne Street). Ginger 
Brown, Coffey Engineering and Surveying, speaking on behalf of the developer, advised the zone 
change request is for a new express carwash business; stated measures will be taken to avoid 
vehicle congestion , and indicated the business will be utilizing  a water reclamation system.   
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Luna.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Orr noted that construction for the business has not 
yet started.  Comment was made concerning drainage and traffic.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body present. 
 
  
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Sect ion 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from MR Medium -Density Residential to 
MUB Mixed -Use Business Emphasis fo r Lots 1 -5, Block 19, North Cheyenne, 2nd Filing, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the northwest corner of the intersection of Yellowstone Road and

Upon inquiry, Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development Department, advised phone calls had been 
received inquiring about the proposed project (residential and commercial development) , but no 
one from the public made comment at the last Public Services Committee meeting.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Johnson 
voting “no”. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Section 8.40.070, Food Stands or Pushcarts-
Mobile Food Trucks-Mobile Food Units-Temporary Parade Vendors-Business License Required-
Application, of Chapter 8.40, Food and Food Service Establishments, of Title 8, Health and Safety, 
of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, by creating a new Section 8.40.070(D) exempting 
lemonade stands operated by minor s under the age of eighteen (18) years from the provisions of 
this section – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Creating Section 15.08.031, Building Permit Fees – 
Exceptions for Incentivization Program, of Chapter 15.08, International Building Code Adopted, 
of Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred 
to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
 RESOLUTION – To establish uniform procedures, fees, costs, and charges for inspecting, 
copying, and producing public reco rds.  Mr. White moved to adopt, seconded by Mr.  Johnson.  
Mr. White moved to amend the “Be  It Further Resolved” clause by changing the language of the 
last item, last line under Section 1 , Research or compilation services-Fee, to read “Actual staff 
time if above $150” , seconded by Mr.  Luna.  Upon inquiry, Mr. White explained the intent of 
calculating staff time is to utilize an employee’s hourly salary wage.  Motion to amend carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Main motion as amended carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Johnson and 
Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. (#5936)  
 
 RESOLUTION – Approving the budget of the Cheyenne Downtown Development 
Authority for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1 , 2018 and endin g June 30, 2019.  Vicki Dugger, 
Executive Director, Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority, explained an adjustment to the 
proposed budget has been submitted due to changes in the downtown tree maintenance and parking 
subsidy programs, and pursuant to discussion at the Finance Committee meeting.  Ms. Dugger 
provided information on development incentives; proposed DDA re-branding; DDA 
administrative st affing level, and budget items funded through mill levy  monies.  Discussion 
included the following topics:  Changes to budget items included in a proposed substitute budget; 
development incentives and re -branding.  Upon inquiry by S ylvia Hackl, City Attorn ey, Ms. 
Dugger confirmed the parking subsidy line item had been removed from the revenue and 
expenditure area.  Mr. White moved to adopt, seconded by M r. Roybal.  Mr. White moved to 
  
amend by substitute , seconded by Mr. Roybal.   Motion to amend  carried. Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present  with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  
Discussion continued on the budgeted amount for re-branding; downtown infrastructure needs; 
governing body authority to approve the DDA’s annual budget; current status of any Memorandum 
of Understanding (MOU) between the City and DDA , and DDA projects involving City 
departments.  Mr. Laybourn moved to postpone the item to July 9, 2018, seconded by Dr. Rinne .  
Upon inquiry, Ms.  Dugger, Alessandra McCoy, Assistant City Attorney, and Robert Briggs, 
Director of Planning and Development, provided information on past MOU’s and a draft MOU 
that has been prepared as a work plan.  Discussion included DDA funds being spent equitably ; 
comment in support as well as non-support of  postponement; DDA strategic plan information 
provided during FY2019 budget workshops; DDA board members, as well as other boards 
affiliated with the City, being appointed by the governing body; DDA budget year based on a fiscal 
year, beginning July 1st, and, if the item is postponed, funds not being distributed from the City 
Treasurer on that date until the DDA budget is approved.  Motion to postpone failed.  Voting “yes” 
– Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Shanor.  Voting “no” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Luna, 
Mr. Roybal, Mr. White and Mayor Orr.  Mr. Laybourn moved to amend by striking $30,000 for 
re-branding from the DDA budget, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Laybourn advised, 
in conjunction with his amendment, it was his intent to instead place the amount of $30,000 in the 
Depot Plaza summer events budget.  Following brief comment, Dr. Rinne withdrew his second to 
the motion to amend.  Comments continued on DDA mill levy funding for certain budget items; 
re-branding; downtown impr ovements and infrastructure, and whether the governing body has 
oversight of DDA funds following adoption of the DDA budget.  During discussion, Ms. Dugger 
provided further information on re -branding plans and Ron Downey, City Treasurer, confirmed 
the City’s FY2019 includes $110,00.00 for the downtown tree program to be provided through the 
City.  Mr. White called for the question (to limit debate), seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing present with the except ion of Mr. Laybourn voting 
“no”.  Main motion as amended by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne voting “no”. (#5937) 
 
[City Clerk note:  Due to a prior commitment, M r. Shanor vacated Council Chambers and was 
absent for the remainder of the meeting.] 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Approving the budget of the Cheyenne Regional Airport Board for the 
Fiscal Year beginning July 1 , 2018 and ending June 30, 2019 .  Motion ( per Consent Ag enda) 
carried. (#5938) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Supporting the expedient redevelopment of the building located at 1602 
Capitol Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, commonly referred to as the historic Hynds Building, and 
the lot adjacent to and west of the Hynds Building known as The Hole.  Mr. White moved to adopt, 
seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. Roybal moved to postpone to July 9, 2018, seconded by Mr. Luna.  
It was noted postponement would allow time for a development plan on the project to be reviewed 
by the Planning Commission prior to action on the resolution.  Discussion followed and included:  
Probable changes to the resolution; suggestion that a City Council work session be held on the 
topic; public/private partnering and re-development of the property.  Motion to postpone carried. 
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.

Solutions, LLC, Fort Collins, Colorado, for the Pavement Condition Survey Project (2015 -2018

Community College and for an area of Laramie County immediately adjacent to the City (Revenue

[CA] Contract Modification No. 2 to Contract No. 6305 between the City of Cheyenne and RBC

 Consideration of RFP-16-18 for a contract for furnishing and installing a parking access, 
permit and revenue control system.   Captain Nathan Buseck, Police Department, reviewed the 
purpose for implement ing a new system  which will assist with management of the City’s two 
parking structures and East surface lot, and provided information on a validated parking option for 
businesses; positive community business feedback for the new system; parking fees; system cost, 
including annual maintenance fees; accountability for users and uniform assessment, and types of 
permit users and revenue generation.   Astrid, owner, Plains Hotel, expressed concerns with 
expenditure cost for the system vs. benefits; supporting a “fragile downtown”, and cost of parking 
fines and parking rates.  Alane West reiterated comments she had made on the topic at the Finance 
Committee meeting, and suggested employees/property owners be allowed to park on upper levels 
in the parking structures and re -striping one-way streets to assist with on -street parking.  John 
Dinneen commented in support of the new system, citing recommendations and observations made 
in the Kimley-Horn downtown parking study, and stated the new system will provide better data 
and reports for future decisions related to downtown parking strategies.  Mr. White moved to 
approve the proposal submitted by Passport Labs, Inc., Charlotte, North Carolina , in an amount 
not to exceed $574,002.58, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Discussion followed in support of the new 
system and various affiliated features, and the different types of data that will be provided through 
the system.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#6861) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -28-18 for furnishing new computers and operating systems for 
the City of Cheyenne through the Information Technology Di vision (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  
  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accept ed bid from Dell Marketing L.P., Round Rock, 
Texas, in an amount not to exceed $37,016.24).  
 
 Consideration of Bid #E-29-18 for furnishing 18 police vehicles with equipment installed 
for the Cheyenne Police Depa rtment (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Lieutenant Rob Dafoe, Police 
Department, provided information on the annual bid to replace vehicles, noting the bid includes 
three new vehicles, and advised it is a “turn key” bid , meaning the bid includes acquisition of 
vehicles with equipment also installed for better warranty provisions.  Upon inquiry, Lt. Dafoe 
explained the depar tment is working toward a fleet comprised of trucks instead of passenger 
vehicles, which should have a longer use life.  Mr. White advised there was no recommendation 
on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Roybal moved to accept the bid submitted by Spradley 
Barr Motors, Cheyenne, in an amount not to exceed $797,400.00, seconded by Mr . Johnson .  
Comments were made that Community Service Officers ’ utilization of trucks will assist with 
response to vehicle collisions (hauling of equipment needed to assi st with debris clean -up) and 
transporting of bicycles, etc. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present. 
 
 Consideration of Bid #E -26-18 for furnishing one new 2019 cab and chassis, 4x4 with 
utility body installed for the City of Cheyenne, Traffic Division (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax). Vicki 
Nemecek, Director of Public Works, explained the bid is for a replacement of a sign installation 
truck and that three vehicles will be submitted as trade-ins, and discussed vehicle problems with 
the current truck resulting in costly repairs.  Discussion followed on utilizing one of the trucks to 
be traded -in for Forestry in association with the downtown tree maintenance program.  Upon 
inquiry, Ms. Nemecek explained she had visited with Jason Sanchez, Deputy Director, Community 
Recreation and Events, who advised the current tower truck is not compatible for Forestry use due 
to its size and lack of maneuverability in certain downtown areas.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Sanchez 
confirmed the truck only has a single boom and is too large for working in downtown traffic areas.  
Mr. White moved to accept the bid submitted by Spradley Barr Motors, Cheyenne, in an amount 
not to exceed $103,627.00, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Ms. Nemecek offered that purc hase of a 
new truck could be approved without trading-in the 2003 GMC truck.  Mr. Roybal moved to 
remove the 2003 GMC truck as a trade-in, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Comment was made that if the 
2003 GMC could not be utilized by Forestry it could be used as a trade-in for the new Forestry 
truck for the downtown tree maintenance program.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne voting 
“no”.  Main motion as amended carried .  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present.  
 
Following a brief recess, Mayor Orr reconvened the meeting at 8:24 p.m. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-25-18 for furnishing new refuse vehicles for the City of Cheyenne, 
Sanitation Division (Solid Waste Management fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-30-18 for furnishing one new 2019 77-passenger bus for the City 
of Cheyenne, Latchkey Division (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
  
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-15-18 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Public Works 
Department, and Containment Systems, LLC, Birmingham, Alabama, for the City of Cheyenne 
Landfill Permanent Litter Fence Project (Solid Waste Management f und) – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] Restaurant liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s office for Mancuso’s LLC 
d/b/a Esther’s, 2114 Warren Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.   
 
Appointment of Daniel Holbrook to the Downto wn Development Authority Board.   Mr. 
Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White. During inquiry on Board member composition 
(per City Code) , Chuck West advised Mr. Holbrook is a commercial property appraiser, who 
doesn’t own property in the DDA District, and that the majority balance of Board members leasing 
or owning property in the downtown district will be maintained.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote 
(Mayoral appointee). 
 
Reappointments of Scott Cowley and Dema Jo Gilbert to the Cheyenne/Laramie County 
Economic Development Joint Powers Board, and Allison Anderson, Richard Berry and Ronn 
Jeffrey to the Laramie County Juvenile Services Joint Powers Board.  Mr. Luna moved to approve, 
seconded by Mr . White.   Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (Mayoral appointees). 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, July 9, 2018 at 6:00 p.m. 
in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a restaurant liquor 
license application filed with the City Clerk’s office for The Ridge Restaurant LLC d/b/a The 
Ridge Restaurant, 1820 Ridge Road, Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, July 9, 2018 at 6:00 p.m. 
in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to receive public 
comments on a proposed trade of a portion of real property owned by the City of Cheyenne at 2303 
Westland Road, for a portion of real property owned by Roger E. Gill and Alec Yurgilevich at 
2305 Westland Road, Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, July 9, 2018 at 6:00 p.m. 
in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to receive public comment 
and input on the “Development Plan for the Hynds/Hole (H2) Project” as proposed by the 
Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority. 
 
 Under other business:  
 
 Jeff Dierksen questioned stormwater run -off drainage issues for some county residents 
allegedly due to a private residential development project within the City and whether the City 
would be addressing the situation.  Mayor Orr advised communication is planned with the County 
Engineer, and a City -County Joint Stormwater Drainage Committee may be formed  to discuss 
issues generally involving stormwater drainage.  Mr. Dierksen also commented on lead water pipes 
  
still in use by the Boar d of Public Utilities, and trash and debris along the Greenway path, 
suggesting a Keep Cheyenne Beautiful campaign be implemented.  Information in response was 
provided on BOPU water testing requirements, Greenway clean -up future plans, and trash 
containment requirements and pick -up services provided by Sanitation staff.  During comments 
on lead water pipes, Mark Court provided information regarding ph water levels. 
 
 Mark Court expressed concerns with recreational vehicles parked on residential streets and 
affiliated safety issues.  Frank Cole stated that certain neighborhoods have property covenants in 
place that prohibit RV parking in certain places; commented on oversized vehicles, trailers , etc. 
obstructing traffic signs, and suggested these types o f vehicles and equipment not be allowed to 
be parked on streets. 
 
 Mr. White congratulated City staff and volunteers for the great SuperDay event held during 
the past weekend. 
 
 Mr. Johnson commented on various topics, including the recent U.S. Supreme Cou rt 
opinion involving non-discrimination; advised of an upcoming Depot Plaza splash pad fundraising 
event, and expressed concern with a hedge blocking an intersection line-of-sight. 
 
 There being no further business to come before the governing body, the me eting was 
officially adjourned at 8:59 p.m. 
   
        Submitted by, 
  
  
 
        Carol Intlekofer, MMC 
        City Clerk
