ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Vacating the 9 th Street right -of-way lying west of

[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Miller 
Addition, 4th Filing, a replat of all of Block 2, Miller Addition, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located east of Townsend Place, North of Carlson Street).  M otion (per Consent

[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat for

 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for the 
Nishigawa Annex Subdivision, a replat of all of Lots 3 and 4 and the West 44 feet of Lot 2, Block 
393, Original City, situated in the SE1/4 SW1/4 of Section 31, Township 14N, Range 66W, of the 
6th P.M., Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located at the southeast corner of W. 17 th Street 
and O’Neil Avenue).  Casey Palma, Steil Surveying Services, agent for the applicant, explained 
an updated revised map had been submitted; that site plans have been submitted with waiver 
requests, which were under review and would be determined later, and spoke in favor of moving 
forward with the revised map.  Dr. Rinne moved to adopt with revised plat map dated September 
12, 2018, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion to adopt with revised plat map carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body. (#5950) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing a street name change from “Prairie Avenue” to “Back Way” 
for the portion of the street located between Dell Range Boulevard and the undeveloped section of 
Prairie Avenue, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming.   Jordan Evans, GIS Cooperative, 
provided information on the purpose of the street name change, which is a request from the 
Combined Communications (dispatch) Center, to alleviate conflict with other street areas named 
Prairie Avenue off Dell Range  and Powderhouse Road relative to emergency responders.  Dr. 
Rinne moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Case.  Concern was expressed regarding the proposed 
name of “Back Way”, and current east /west portions of Prairie Avenue.  Dr. Rinne moved to 
amend by naming the section to the east of Powerhouse Road as East Prairie Avenue, and the area 
west of Powerhouse Road as West Prairie Avenue, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Chief Greg Hoggatt, 
Fire and Rescue Department, expressed concern that geographically the City  has an east/west 
“point” based on Central Avenue and changing the dividing point to Powderhouse for just this 
street section may affect block numbers and emergency response.  Mr. Evans stated the amendment 
would affect some resident addresses and suggested postponement of the item could be considered 
to review suggestions  for a street name other than “Back Way”.  Amy Allen, City Engineer, 
provided information on the Prairie Avenue intersections involved, and expressed she was open to 
other street name suggestions other than “Back Way”.  Discussion included naming of street s 
pursuant to City Code provisions.  Upon inquiry, Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney, advised she would 
like time to review applicable City Code provisions.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting “no” – all 
members of the governing body.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Evans revie wed information elements the 
Combined Communications Center receives relative to calls for service.  Dr. Rinne moved to 
postpone to October 8, 2018, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body. 
 
 RESOLUTION – Acknowledging the receipt of the Preliminary Cost Models for a second 
level on the Cheyenne Municipal Court Building to be located at 308 West 17 th Street and 
supporting the dedication of funds to design and build a finished second level on the Ch eyenne 
Municipal Court Building .  Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works, stated a commitment by 
  
the governing body is necessary to move forward with the second story to facilitate appropriate 
engineering, structural and design decisions , and noted adoption of the resolution involves 
acknowledging receipt of the preliminary cost models for a second level and states the g overning 
body’s commitment to dedicate necessary funds to design and finish the second level.  She advised, 
pursuant to inquiry at the Finance Committee meeting, the City Treasurer had submitted a memo 
to governing body members on funding availability for the project relative to use of 6 th penny 
overage sales tax funds, and that a question was posed relative to legal interpretation of the phrase 
“acknowledging receipt”.  Ron Downey, City Treasurer, advised 6 th penny sales tax overage 
cannot be spent on general government (ie: salaries or general maintenance), or anything that was 
not approved on the ballot by voters.  In response to inquiry, Mayor Orr provided information on 
possible sale of a portion of City -owned Swan  Ranch (which may assist with project costs) 
pursuant to an interest expressed by Dyno Nobel to expand their footprint, noting the City would 
be required to surplus the property and offer it through the bidding process, and that the parcel has 
been surveyed and appraised and there were no short term plans for use of the property by the City.  
Noel Griffith, Noel Griffith Jr. and Associates Architects, stated a study for space utilization within 
the new Municipal Court building still needs to be conducted  and provided information on site 
parking; aesthetic elements involving the building’s façade; timing of UDC (Unified Development 
Code) and Development review , and removal of the building from the Historic Preservation 
Board’s contributing structures list.  Mr. Luna advised there was no recommendation on the item 
from Finance Committee.  Mr. Cook moved to adopt, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Comment followed 
on financial concerns involving a commitment to finish the second level.  Dr. Rinne moved to 
amend by removing the word “finished” from the second section of the Resolution’s Now 
Therefore Be It Resolved clause, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Discussion topics included:  Unknown 
use of the second level; designing the second level as a “shell” space only at this time; potential 
future finishing costs; revenue streams identified for the project, and $1.1 million dollar difference 
between shell-out and a finished product.  During discussion, Mr. Griffith advised finishing the 
second level at a later date may be more expensive but could be done at any time and provided 
information on mechanical and electrical needs, including elevator and stairways, relating to 
designing a “shell” (empty) space, and elements considered to be included, or not included, in an 
unfinished “shell”.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Nemecek stated use of the second floor could be determined 
later and that the main issue is whether to contract services to design a “shell” or “finish” space, 
noting the Con struction Manager At Risk (CMAR) will be recommended for selection on 
September 28th with a contract then being presented.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – 
Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr. Shanor.  Voting 
“no” – Mr. Johnson, Mr. White and Mayor Orr.  [City Clerk note:  As a clerical correc tion in 
conjunction with the approved amendment, the word “finished” w ill also be removed from the 
Resolution entitlement.]  Further discussion included cost concerns and other municipal capital 
facilities and maintenance needs and responsibilities; “shell” design based upon budget proposals 
received, and potential future sale of the Swan Ranch parcel.  Main motion as amended carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#5951)  
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Terminating the agreement between the City of  Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
and the Cheyenne Animal Shelter/Services for Animal Contr ol and Animal Shelter Services – 
referred to Committee of the Whole. (Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
 
  
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department, and the Wyoming 
Department of Transportation for D.U.I. Mobile Command Task Force enforcement and 
educational activities (Highway Safety Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6909) 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police 
Department, and the City of Casper, Casper Police Department, for law enforcement 
extraterritorial assistance – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Industrial and Economic Development Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne 
and the Cheyenne/Laramie County Corporation for Economic Development  (LEADS) – referred 
to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Amendment No. 1 to Contract No. 6709, Human Services Agreement, between the City of 
Cheyenne and United Way of Laramie County to extend the termination date for the Community 
School Pilot Program (2011-2014 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6709) 
 
[CA] Contract Renewal for Contract No. 6794 between the City of Cheyenne, Planning and 
Development Department, and American Contracting of Wyoming, LLC, Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
for the Warren Rest House Restoration, Phase 2 Project, to extend the comple tion date.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $37,687.00). (#6794) 
 
 Consideration to purchase Motorola portable radios for the Cheyenne Fire and Rescue 
Department from Wireless Advanced Communications, Evans, Colorado (2015 -2018 1% Sales 
Tax).  Chief Greg Hoggatt, Fire and Rescue Department, advised the purchase amount approved 
by the Finance Committee did not include financing interest as the radios will be leased.  Mr. Luna 
stated it was brought to his attention that the Finance Committee inadvertently recommended to 
approve in an amount not to exceed $446,889.52, however, that amount  did not include interest 
charges.  Mr. Luna moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $517,310.95 which includes the 
interest charges, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried .  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-4-19 for furnishing ice control product for the City of Cheyenne, 
Public Works Department (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Report of Community Facility Fees for Fiscal Year 2017 -2018.  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Roybal moved to approve the reappointment of Dr. Earl B. Kincheloe to the Cheyenne 
Housing Authority, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (Mayoral appointment). 
  
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held October 8, 2018 at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to determine whether 
compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for a portion 
  
of the west half, of the west half of Section 17, T14N, R66W, of the 6th Principal Meridian, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located west of and adjacent to Powderhouse Road, north of Spirit Lane). 
 
 Under other business: 
 
 Jeff Dierksen  discussed how the governing body addresses pet itions (which are not 
otherwise addressed by law or City requirements), suggesting the governing body implement a 
procedure to openly discuss and act upon petitions that are received, and questioned whether the 
location of lead pipes in the City’s water sy stem has been identified.  Response comments were 
made by members of the governing body regarding the handling of citizen requests and complaints 
and responses to same by individual members of the governing body ; uniformity and standards 
concerns involving a formal policy, and concern with developing a threshold as it may pertain to 
one resident or numerous residents.   Mr. Laybourn, City Council liaison to the Board of Public 
Utilities (BOPU), advised he would pass on the inquiry concerning lead pipes to the BOPU. 
 
 Mr. Johnson expressed appreciation to the organizers, donors and volunteers involved in 
the Rooted in Cheyenne tree planting event  over the weekend , and also to the coordinators, 
organizers, volunteers and participants who assisted with or partic ipated in the community’s first 
marathon event on September 23. 
 
 Mr. Case expressed appreciation to the Fire and Rescue Department relative to involvement 
with Bob Quick’s Journey, a bicyclist riding across the country who was recently traveling through 
Cheyenne. 
 
 Mr. Luna commented on a concern he had received regarding unauthorized “residents” at 
the vacated Hitching Post property and requested follow-up on the issue. 
 
 There being no  further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:54 p.m. 
 
        Submitted by, 
 
             
        Carol Intlekofer, MMC 
        City Clerk 
   
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  October 5, 2018

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2018-10-08 | CLIP_ID: 679 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 679

  
 
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
October 8, 2018 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor  and Jeff White.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, 
and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Presentation of Main Street Leadership award to Councilman Richard Johnson.  Bruce 
Heimbuck, Cheyenne DDA and Main  Street Advisory Board member, commented  on Mr. 
Johnson’s volunteerism on  projects, and several local community agencies that have benefitt ed 
from fundraising efforts in which he has been involved .  Mr. Heimbuck, Councilman White and 
Vicki Dugger, DDA Executive Director, presented a plaque to Mr. Johnson with Mr. Johnson 
expressing appreciation to the commu nity, colleagues, friends and family for supporting him on 
the various projects he has participated in for the community.  
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.) Upon request by Mr. White, and pursuant to support received by 
Mr. Case and Mr. Cook, Agenda Item #19(b) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr.  Cook.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on September 24, 2018.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried.   
 
 Mr. Roybal moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Luna.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
 
 Mayor Orr opened a public hearin g to  determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for a portion of the west half, of the west 
half of Section 17, T14N, R66W, of the 6 th Principal Meridian, Laramie County, Wyoming 
(located west of and adjacent to Powderhouse Road, north of Spirit Lane).   Seth Lloyd, Planning 
and Development Department,  provided information on the property requested by the landowner 
to be annexed and reviewed requirements of and compliance with annexation conditions contained 
in state statutes .  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the public hearing 
having been met, Mayor Orr closed the public hearing. 
 
  
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Vacating the 9th Street right-of-way lying west of Snyder 
Avenue between fractional Block 523, all of Blocks 524 and 573, and fractional Block 574, 
Original City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, 
Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4246) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Chapter 12.24, Art in Public Places, of Title 
12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
pertaining to the Art in Public Places Committee  – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsors – 
Mr. Johnson and Mr. White) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READI NG – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion 
of the west half, of the west half of Section 17, T14N, R66W, of the 6th Principal Meridian, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located west of and adjacent to Powderhouse Road, north of Spirit Lane)  – 
referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing a street name change from “Prairie Avenue” to “Back Way” 
for the portion of the street located between Dell Range Boulevard and the undeveloped section of 
Prairie Avenue, City  of Che yenne, Laramie County, Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, 
seconded by Mr.  Case.  Mr. Roybal moved to amend by substituting “Short Road” for “Back 
Way”, seconded by Mr.  Case. Upon inquiry, Jordan Evans, GIS Cooperative, advised it is his 
understanding that local emergency management agencies don’t have any concerns with the name 
“Short Road”.  Chief Greg Hoggatt, Cheyenne Fire and Rescue, confirmed that “Short Road” is 
acceptable for emergency management purposes . He explained this change is part of an ongoing 
process to make updates to street names that are problematic.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”. (#5952)  
 
 RESOLUTION – Terminating the agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
and the Cheyenne Animal Shelter/Services for Animal Contr ol and Animal Shelter Services .  
Tammy Maas, Board President, Cheyenne Animal Shelter , advised they were available for any 
questions.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Case.  
Mr. White moved to amend by substitute submitted on 10 -3-18, seconded by Mr.  Case.  David 
Pope, Board Treasurer, Cheyenne Ani mal Shelter , advised there were no concerns with the 
proposed substitute.  Appreciation was expressed to the Board for their support in addressing 
issues.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion 
as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#5953) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Designating Arts Cheyenne as the Local Arts Agency fo r the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsors – Mr. Johnson and Mr. White) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Providing for the review and approval of the South Cheyenne Water & 
Sewer District’s Federal SLIB Chapter 3 Mineral Royalty grant application befor e the Wyoming 
State Loan and Investment Board for the emergency repair of the existing sanitary sewer siphon  
crossing of Crow Creek Project – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
  
[CA] RESOLUTION – Declaring Cheyenne a City of Kindness and Generosity – referred to 
Public Services Committee. (Sponsors – Mr. Johnson, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White) 
 
 [CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police 
Department, and the City of Casper, Casper Police Department, for law enforcement 
extraterritorial assistance.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6911) 
 
[CA] Industrial and Economic Development Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne 
and the Cheyenne/Laramie County Corporation for Economic Development  (LEADS).  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $49,825.00). (#6912) 
 
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Youth Alternatives Division, and State of 
Wyoming, Department of Family Services, for kinship advocacy services for displaced, at -risk 
youth – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Minimum Revenue Guaranteed Payment agreement between the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, and the Cheyenne Regional Air Service Focus Team, c/o Cheyenne LEADS – referred 
to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Grant Award between the City of Cheyenne, Police Department, and Wyoming Office of 
Homeland Security to purchase protective gear for SWAT team – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and FCI Constructors of Wyoming, LLC, for 
the pre-construction and construction phase services for Cheyenne Municipal Courts Building (1% 
Specific Purpose Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 [CA] Consideration of Bid #E-4-19 for furnishing ice control product for the City of Cheyenne, 
Public Works Department  (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried 
(accepted bid from Desert Mountain Corp., Riverton, Wyoming, in the amount of $128,540.00 for 
the 1 st year and $132,960.00 for the second year for a total of $261,500.00, with the option to 
purchase additional tons). 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -7-19 for furnishing two new 2018 -2019 rough mowers for the  
City of Cheyenne Golf Division – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase 76 Self Contained Breathing Apparatuses (SCBA’s) along with 
accessory equipment from L.N. Curtis & Sons, Salt Lake City, Utah, for the Chey enne Fire and 
Rescue Department – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Restaurant liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s office for The Golden Egg 
of Cheyenne, Inc. d/b/a Epic Egg, 2300 Carey Ave., Cheyenne, Wyoming.   Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. 
 
 Transfer of retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s office for Cheyenne 
Frontier Days, Inc. d/b/a Cheyenne F rontier Days, 1230 West 8 th Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 
(license currently held by Steamboat’s, LLC d/b/a Steamboat’s Wyoming Steak House, 115 E. 
  
17th St.). Tom Hirsig, CEO, Cheyenne Frontier Days, provided information related to their retail 
liquor license request, new h eadquarters and conference center  project and involvement with 
contributing to the community .  During discussion and in response to inquiries, Mr. Hirsig 
provided information on CFD’s mission statement;  the new building assisting with economic 
development for the City; retail liquor license cost savings due to not having to partner with an 
alcohol caterer;  access and parking changes related to the new building ; “parking” the license 
until the building is completed; Cheyenne’s need for a conference center; tourism efforts on a year 
round basis and their business plan for operating under the license.  Sam Galeotos commented in 
opposition of the application, and stated his belief that operation of a retail liquor establishment is 
contradictory to CFD’s mission statement.  He discussed CFD’s non-profit status and how license 
approval may affect private businesses. Discussion followed on the mission and image of CFD.  
Kevin Morris, representative for Steamboat’s Wyoming Steak House , spoke in favor of the 
application and explained if the transfer is approved  Steamboat’s investors will not receive any 
proceeds from the transfer . He stated CFD is  a responsible and economical candidate for the 
license, and that requirements set forth in Wyoming §12-4-104(b) have been met.  Upon inquiry, 
he advised Steamboat’s operated under the license for  approximately five years.  Randy Reed, 
attorney representing CFD, expressed his opinion  that it isn’t permissible to contemplate 
competition as a reason to deny approval of a license transfer, and stated the governing body’s role 
is to determine that the five statutorial requirements are satisfied.  Bryan Grzegorczyk, owner, 
Alf’s Pub, spoke in support of the transfer and provided information on Alf’s temporary alcohol 
catering permits during CFD and liquor liability insurance cost.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Grzegorczyk 
provided information regarding alcohol related violations which occurred at Frontier Park during 
CFD, stating none were associated with his catering permits.  Captain Nathan Buseck, Cheyenne 
Police Department, further explained violations involving underage service and over consumption, 
and other non-CFD alcohol related citations during the event.  Evan Asher commented on the lack 
of event venues in the community, and spoke in favor of the transfer.  In res ponse to a prior 
comment, Mr. Hirsig advised non-profit agencies are eligible to hold liquor licenses with revenue 
subject to taxes, and there are no special exemptions currently available for financing their project.  
Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  
Discussion continued and included: Statutory and city code requirements for transferring licenses; 
other non-profit entities in the state that hold liquor licenses; special club license not sufficient for 
CFD’s particular scenario; city retail license fee ; City’s involvement in a transfer between two 
private entities; focus being applied towards the statutory requirements; origin of CFD, and archaic 
state liquor laws.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn, 
Mr. Luna and Mr. Roybal. Voting “no” -  Dr. Rinne, Mr. White, Mr. Shanor and Mayor Orr. 
 
Under other business:  
 
 Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, requested, due to timeliness involving a contract renewal, the 
following item be introduced: Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract #6427 between the City of 
Cheyenne, Fire and Rescue Department, and Physio -Control, Inc., Redmo nd, Washington, for 
renewal maintenance services for factory -authorized inspection and repair services of LIFEPAK 
devices.  Mayor Orr referred the item to Finance Committee. 
 
  
 Mr. Laybourn expressed , for the record, his concern regarding possible removal of 
“Cottonwood Joe”, the Cottonwood tree located near the west entrance of the Municipal Building. 
He submitted and discussed a past risk assessment which rated the tree as low risk and in general 
good health, and Resolution #4513 relating to preserving cultural heritage and historical assets (on 
file with the City Clerk’s office).  Mr. Laybourn suggested the decision for removal of the tree go 
before the governing body, and landscaping for areas adjacent to the Municipal Buildin g be 
reviewed. 
 
 There being no  further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:43 p.m. 
   
 
        Submitted by, 
             
        Carol Intlekofer, MMC 
        City Clerk 
   
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  October 23, 2018

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2018-10-22 | CLIP_ID: 681 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 681

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
October 22, 2018 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Scott 
Roybal and Dicky Shanor.  Absent:  Dr. Mark Rinne and Jeff White.  Also present: Carol 
Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Cook and Mr. Johnson, Agenda Item # 10 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr.  
Luna moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr.  Johnson.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on October 8, 2018.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried.   
 
 Mr. Roybal declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Cowboy 
Dodge, Inc.) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr.  Johnson moved to approve payment of vouchers 
as presented, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Roybal returned to 
Council Chambers. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapter 12.24, Art in Public Places, of Title 
12, Stre ets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
pertaining to the Art in Public Places Committee .  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Case  
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Votin g “yes” – 
all members of governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READI NG – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion 
of the west half, of the west half of Section 17, T14N, R66W, of the 6th Principal Meridian, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located west of and adjacent to Powderhouse Road, north of Spirit Lane) .  
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Votin g “yes” – all members of the governing body  
present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
  
Development Code, changing th e zoning classification from County A -1 Agricultural and Rural 
Residential to MUR Mixed -Use Residential Emphasis for a portion of the west half, of the west 
half of Section 17, T14N, R66W, of the 6th Principal Meridian Cheyenne, Wyoming (located west 
of an d adjacent to Powderhouse Road, north of Spirit Lane ) – referred to Public Services 
Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Designating Arts Cheyenne as the Local Arts Agency fo r the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Case moved to adopt, seconded by  Mr. Johnson.  Upon inquiry, Bill 
Lindstrom, Executive Director, Arts Cheyenne, reviewed the history and purpose of the association 
and described public arts services envisioned to be provided by Arts Cheyenne through the 
resolution and an affiliated Memorandum of Understanding (MOU).  Upon further inquiry, Mr. 
Lindstrom provided information on :  Proposed Arts Cheyenne services related to an ordinan ce 
currently before the governing body  (Agenda Item #7) , the resolution and MOU,  which would 
authorize Arts Cheyenne to serve as the City’s Art in Public Places Committee; the resolution and 
MOU; communication with City staff on community development proje cts; public/private 
partnering and engaging the community to define and enhance Cheyenne’s public arts 
environment.  Discussion included the following topics:  Developing guidelines as set forth in the 
MOU; past Art in Public Places projects; funding consideration for the next fiscal year budget, and 
public/private partnering.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present. (#5954) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Providing for the review and approval of the South Cheyenne Water & 
Sewer District’s Federal SLIB Chapter 3 Mineral Royalty grant application before the Wyoming 
State Loan and Investment Board for the emergency repair of the existing sanitary sewer siphon  
crossing of Crow Creek Project.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5955) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Declaring Cheyenne a City of Kindness and Generosity .  Geri Maria 
Johnson commented in support of the resolution.  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. 
Johnson.  Discussion included the following topics: Following the “golden rule”  philosophy; 
encouraging a kind and generous community; concern with legislating the topic  and setting 
community morality decisions through a resolution; Compassionate Cheyenne organization efforts 
and past discussions; resolution serving only as a stateme nt of opinion; remarks contained in a 
recent local newspaper article and offense to the remarks taken by some governing body members; 
expressions of both support and opposition to the resolution, and intimidating comments that had 
been made involving support for the resolution.  Motion failed (due to a tie vote).  Voting “yes” – 
Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Roybal.  Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. Luna, Mr. 
Shanor and Mayor Orr. (#5956) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for South 
Park Estates, 10th Filing, a replat of Lots 1, 2, 5, and a portion of Lot 3, Block 1, South Park Estates, 
6th Filing, Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located between Partoyan Drive and 
Walterscheid Boulevard, north of I-80) – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. 
Roybal) 
 
  
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat for 
Hope Subdivision, 4 th Filing, a replat of Lot 2, Block 1, Hope Subdivision, 2 nd Filing, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located at the southeast corner of the intersection of E. Nation Road and S. 
Avenue B-6) – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Proclaiming ‘Cheyenne Anthem’ as the official song  for the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Youth Alternatives Division, and State of 
Wyoming, Department of Family Services, for kinship advocacy services for displaced, at -risk 
youth.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6915) 
 
[CA] Minimum Revenue Guaranteed Payment agreement between the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, and the Cheyenne Regional Air Service Focus Team, c/o Cheyenne LEADS .  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $600,000.00). (#6916) 
 
[CA] Grant Award between the City of Cheyenne, Police Department, and Wyoming Office of 
Homeland Security to purchase protective gear for the SWAT team.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#6917) 
 
 Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and FCI Constructors of Wyoming, LLC, for 
the pre-construction and construction phase services for Cheyenne Municipal Courts Building (1% 
Specific Purpose Sales Tax) .  Joe Dougherty questioned the scope of services and the City’s 
financial commitment affiliated with the agreement .  In response, Vicki Nemecek, Director of 
Public Works, advised the purpose of the agreement is to hire FCI as the Construction Manager 
At Risk (CMAR) for the project with compensat ion in the amount of $21,485.00 for pre -
construction services and a stipulated maximum fee of $1,246,043.00 for construction phase  
services.  Brandon Munz, FCI Constructors, stated the contract at this time is only authorizing pre-
construction services involving budgeting front work to establish the GMP (Guaranteed Maximum 
Price).  Noel Griffith, Noel Griffith Jr. & Associates Architects, architect for the project, advised 
FCI will be very involved in the project design and at about 50% completion of the design and 
working drawings, the governin g body will be presented with the GMP information .  Comment 
followed on finding funding related to the second floor plans for the building and process and 
topics to be discussed during the pre -construction services phase.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Munz 
provided information on the process to arrive at the GMP amount, which will result in a proposed 
amendment for governing body approval; stated typically, if there isn’t any major change in the 
scope of services, the final dol lar amount comes in under the estimated GMP, and value 
engineering will be one of the elements discussed during the pre -construction process.  Mr. Case 
reported there was no recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Roybal moved 
to approve, seconded by Mr. Case.  Mr. Case moved to  amend by substitut e dated October 22, 
2018, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Ms. Nemecek noted the substitute includes the construction costs 
for the first ($6.4 million ) and second ($4,138,750.00) floors of the building, f or a total 
construction budget of  $10,538,750.00, and also includes Exhibit A (CMAR fee proposal ) and 
Exhibit B (CMAR fee structure).  Upon inquiry, Ms. Nemecek clarified the number ing of pages 
contained in the agreement vs. the exhibit pages.  Comments followed in support of the agreement 
  
and previous work by FCI on past City projects, and timeliness in moving forward with the project.  
Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Main motion 
as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#6918) 
 
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract #6427 between the City of Cheyenne, Fire and 
Rescue Department, and Physio -Control, Inc., Redmond, Washington, for renewal maintenanc e 
services for factory-authorized inspection and repair services of LIFEPAK devices.   Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $51,949.80). (#6427) 
 
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract #6892 between the City of Cheyenne, Public 
Works Department, and Noel Griffith Jr. & Associates Architects, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for design 
engineering of a second level shell and core to the Cheyenne Municipal Court Project (1% Specific 
Purpose Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Amendment No. 1 to Contract #6854, Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the 
Cheyenne Animal Shelter/Services for Animal Control and Animal Shelter Services – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-7-19 for furnishing two new 2018 -2019 rough mowers for the  
City of Cheyenne Golf Division.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accept bid from Stotz 
Equipment, Evansville, Wyoming, in an amount not to exceed $109,500.00). 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase 76 Self Contained Breathing Apparatuses (SCBA’s) along with 
accessory equipment from L.N. Curtis & Sons, Salt Lake City, Utah, for the Chey enne Fire and 
Rescue Department.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accept bid in an amount not to exceed 
$620,665.00) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-6-19 for furnishing one new 2018-2019 motor grader for the Street 
and Alley Division (2015 – 2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Appointment of Alexis Arp, Maggie Black, Ashen Doty, Darby Downham,  Raegan 
Downham, Dylan Fertig, Julia Horst, Keiran Loughran, Marie Lucas, Emily Lucero, Lars 
Quinlivan, Kenitra Shanks, Dominick Singleton and Marty Zhang to the 2018 -2019 Mayor’s 
Youth Council.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion c arried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not 
vote (Mayoral appointments). 
 
 Report from the Cheyenne Animal Shelter Board pursuant to Resolution No. 5953.  Randy 
Reed, attorney for the Animal Shelter Board, provided information pertaining to directives 
contained within the resolution, noting discussions between himself and Sylvia Hackl, City 
Attorney, and referencing an affiliated memorandum that had been s ubmitted to governing body 
members relating to a proposed amendment to the City’s contract with the Animal Shelter, which 
had been referred to Finance Committee earlier in the meeting (Agenda Item #17 (c)).  Upon 
inquiry, Mr. Reed  stated his understanding that additional Animal Shelter costs pursuant to the 
directives would be in addition to the monies the City is paying the Shelter under the current 
  
agreement, and the cost of implementing the directives may be spread between t he governmental 
entities receiving animal shelter services.  Comments included concern with additional costs to the 
City pursuant to information contained in the aforementioned memorandum and probable further 
discussion on the topic at the next Finance Committee meeting. 
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held November 13, 2018 at 6:00 p.m. 
in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a special malt beverage 
annual permit application filed with the City Clerk’s office for The City of Cheyenne d/b/a  City 
of Cheyenne – Civic Center, 510 W. 20th St., Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
 Announcement was made that due to the Veterans Day holiday and City offices being 
closed on Monday, November 12, 2018, the regularly scheduled City Council meeting will instead 
be held on Tuesday, November 13, 2018 at 6:00 p.m. in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil 
Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Under other business: 
 
Tom Mason, Director, Cheyenne Metropolitan Planning Organization, provided an update 
on the MPO’s planning projects for the Dell Range and US Highway 30 corridor, and the Whitney 
Road plan.  Mr. Mason reviewed the physical areas involved and the status of  each plan ; 
anticipated completion dates for the plans, which will then come before the gover ning body, and 
funding availability.  He  advised traffic data will be reviewed  to assess the need to make any 
intermediate, short-term traffic solution recommendations for US 30 and Whitney Road, and Dell 
Range and Whitney Road, due to the projects’ propos ed actual construction starting time frame.  
During his comments, Mr. Mason submitted a handout containing information about the projects 
(on file with City Clerk’s office).  Mayor Orr advised she requested Mr. Mason provide the report 
due to the number of  serious accidents in the area over the weekend and shared that 
communications have been made with state and county project partners in pledging commitment 
to review data from the accidents to discuss any stop-gap traffic measures that may be taken since 
construction on the projects isn’t slated until 2021 to 2024.  Mayor Orr also stated communication 
with the school district had also been made regarding Dakota Crossing and the Safe Route to 
Schools (school crosswalk area) .  Comments followed on the need fo r moving the projects 
forward; concern with traffic volume impact and incidents, and length of time it has taken to 
develop the plans.  Mr. Mason commented on crash and traffic data and increased traffic volumes 
in these areas over the years.  Mr. Case offered to be a contact point for interested residents on the 
topic.  Donn Edmunds expressed concern with “poor drivers” who do not safely operate vehicles; 
increased traffic volume on small lane roads due to residential subdivision growth, and traffic 
patterns.  Wendy Volk stated moving forward with the City’s Christensen Road Overpass project 
in a more timely manner would perhaps help with traffic issues in the vicinity.  Upon inquiry, Amy 
Allen, City Engineer, advised the bid issuance date for the Christensen Road project is tentatively 
set for January 8, 2019.  Mayor Orr expressed financing, designing and planning for big projects 
involve a lot of time and she is in discussions regarding utilizing  a project manager to assist in 
overseeing large projects.  [City Clerk note:  During comments, Mr. Roybal, due to an unforeseen 
circumstance, excused himself from the meeting at 7:14 p.m.] 
 
  
Mr. Johnson commented on the pending retirement of City Clerk Carol Intlekofer, noting 
her encouragement, assistance and sharing of knowledge during his term of office, and expressed 
appreciation for her many years of service to the City.  Governing body members recognized Ms. 
Intlekofer with a round of applause. 
 
        Submitted by, 
 
             
        Carol Intlekofer, MMC 
        City Clerk 
   
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  November 1, 2018

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2018-11-13 | CLIP_ID: 683 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 683

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
November 13, 2018 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Scott 
Roybal and Jeff White.  Absent:  Dr. Mark Rinne and Dicky Shanor.  Also present: Kylie Soden, 
Deputy City Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Case, and pursuant to support received by 
Mr. Cook and Mr. Luna, Agenda Item #26(b) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr.  Luna 
moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr.  Cook.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on October 22, 2018.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried.   
 
 Mr. Roybal declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Cowboy 
Dodge, Inc.) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouche rs 
as presented, seconded by  Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Roybal 
returned to Council Chambers. 
 
 Public Hearing for a special malt beverage permit application filed with the City Clerk’s 
Office for The City of Cheyenne d/b/a City of Cheyenne – Civic Center, 510 W. 20th St., Cheyenne, 
Wyoming.  Jason Sanchez, Deputy Director, Community Recreation and Events, explained  
governing body directives and goals involving the Civic Center, and stated due to recent state 
statute and city code changes, it has been determined the City is eligible to hold this type of permit.  
Mr. Sanchez provided information on the proposed  use of the permit  including:  dispensing 
occurring from 1-3 locations depending on the performance; eligible liquor licensees providing 
wine an d liquor sales at certain events; anticipated r evenue from malt beverage sales;  TIPS 
certified staff; positive customer feedback related to  offering malt beverage sales and enhancing 
the Civic Center experience overall. Upon inquiry, Mr. Sanchez confirmed malt beverage orders 
must be paid for at delivery which will require staff to pre-determine order needs to request a check 
payment through the accounts payable system, and that any remaining malt beverage products will 
be locked in a secure area.  Upon inquiry, Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department, provided 
information on alcohol compliance checks, stating the Civic Center will be eligible for compliance 
checks under the same protoco l as all annual permit/license holders.  There being no further 
  
comments or input and purpose of the public hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the public 
hearing and referred the application to Finance Committee. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Chapter 12.24, Art in Public Places, of Title 
12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
pertaining to the Art in Public Places Committee .  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr.  White 
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of governing body present. (#4247) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion 
of the west half, of the west half of Section 17, T14N, R66W, of the 6th Principal Meridian, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located west of and adjacent to Powderhouse Road, north of Spirit Lane) .  
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. Case .  Upon inquiry, Amy Alle n, City Engineer, explained due to current and 
future residential and commercial growth in the area of Powderhouse and Gardenia, consideration 
of widening/expanding existing road infrastructure  in the future is warranted.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4248) 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from County A -1 Agricultural and Rural 
Residential to MUR Mixed -Use Residential Emphasis for a portion of the west half, of the west 
half of Section 17, T14N, R66W, of the 6th Principal Meridian Cheyenne, Wyoming (located west 
of and adjacent to Powderhouse Road, north of Spirit Lane ).  Mr. Roybal mo ved to approve on 
second reading, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, 
Section 5.1.2, Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the 
Cheyenne Unified Development Code, changing the zoning classification from HR, High Density 
Residential to CB, Community Business for a parcel of land consisting of Lots 9 through 12, Block 
9, Meadowbrooke Park Subdivision, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the south east corner of 
Edgewater Avenue and Bluegrass Circle) – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. 
Roybal) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, 
Section 5.1.2, Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the 
Cheyenne Unified Development Code, changing the zoning class ification from CB, Community 
Business to HR, High Density Residential for a parcel of land consisting of a portion of Lot 1, 
Block 1, Plaza Del Range, Second Filing Subdivision, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at 3110 Dell 
Range Blvd., between Hilltop Avenue and Ridge Road) – referred to Public Services Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending, creating, deleting and re -creating various 
chapters and sections within Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the Cheyenne City Code 
  
relating to general provisions, and including adoption of the International Building Code and other 
related Codes, 2018 edition – referred to Public Service Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending, deleting and re -creating sections  within 
Chapter 8.12 International Fire Code adopted, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Cheyenne City 
Code relating to general provisions pertaining to fire protection and regulations, and adoption of 
the International Fire Code, 2018 edition, and cert ain Codes and practices promulgated by the 
National Fire Protection Association – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. 
Roybal) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Chapter 2.32, Fire Department, Section 
2.32.020, Department Personnel, and C hapter 2.84, Miscellaneous Boards and Commissions, 
Section 2.84.050, International Fire Code Board of Appeals, of Title 2, Administration and 
Personnel, of the Cheyenne City Code pertaining to the Fire and Rescue Department – referred to 
Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Creating Section 1.08.030, Official Song -Recognition 
and Use, of Chapter 1.08, Official Seal and Emblem, of Title 1, General Provisions, of the Code 
of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, pertaining to designation of ‘Cheyenne Anthem’ (©2018) as 
the official song of the City of Cheyenne – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsors – 
Mr. Cook, Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Roybal) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat f or South 
Park Estates, 10th Filing, a replat of Lots 1, 2, 5, and a portion of Lot 3, Block 1, South Park Estates, 
6th Filing, Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located between Partoyan Drive and 
Walterscheid Boulevard, north of I-80). Kelly Hafner,  CivilWorx, LLC, agent for the applicant, 
explained they would like to request a 60 ft.  right-of-way opposed to  the 70 ft.  right-of-way as 
proposed in the staff report  that will include a pedestrian pathway easement, and stated  during a 
meeting with City Engineering staff, it had been determined the request for the 60 ft. right-of-way 
would be a fair compromise .  Amy Allen, City Engineer, further explained most right -of-way 
widths in the area are 60 ft.  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt with revised map and the following staff 
conditions #1 – provide 70 ft.  right-of-way for 3 rd Street and #2 – correct the name of Thomes 
Avenue, seconded by Mr. Case.  Mr. Roybal moved to amend by removing staff condition #1 , 
seconded by Mr. Case. Motion to amend failed.  Voting “no” -  all members of the governing body 
present.  Mr. Roybal moved to amend staff condition #1  by changing the 70  ft. right-of-way to 
“Provide 60 ft.  right-of way for 3 rd Street”, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Upon staff request and for 
clarification, Mr. Roybal moved  to amend by  correcting the name of Thomes Avenue in staff 
condition #2, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Upon inquiry, Susana Montana, Planning and Development 
Department, explained the property is zoned LI, Light Industrial, and according to the UDC , the 
right-of-way width for an industrial street is 70 ft.  She explained this portion of the UDC is under 
the jurisdiction of the City Engineer, who has  the authority to approve a subdivision standard 
waiver such as a street width  requirement.  Both m otions to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present.   Main motion as twice amended carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body present. (#5956) 
 
  
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat for 
Hope Subdivision, 4 th Filing, a replat of Lot 2, Block 1, Hope Subdivision, 2 nd Filing, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located at the southeast corner of the intersection of E. Nation Road and S. 
Avenue B-6).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5957) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Proclaiming ‘ Cheyenne Anthem’ as the official song for the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal reported there was no recommendation from Public Ser vices 
Committee on the item and explained it was decided to introduce the item as an ordinance instead 
of a resolution, which is included on the current agenda.  The resolution died due to lack of a 
motion. 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for the 
Clear Creek Industrial Park Subdivision, a replat of a portion of Block 10, Lots 1 and 12 of Block 
11 and Lots 1 and 17 of Block 12, Interior Heights Addition, and portions of adjacent streets and 
alleys thereto, situated in the SW1/4 of Section 6, Township 13N, Range 66W, of the 6 th P.M., 
Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located at the northeast corner of Arp Avenue and Van 
Tassell Court, east of S. Parsl ey Blvd.) – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. 
Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Aspen 
Creek, a plat of a portion of the west half, of the west half of Section 17, T14N, R66W, of the 6 th 
Principal Meridian, Laramie County, Wyoming (located west of and adjacent to Powderhouse 
Road, north of Spirit Lane) – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Swan 
Ranch Rail Park, Fifteenth Filing, a plat of a portion of the northwest one -quarter of Section 27, 
Township 13 North, Range 67 West of the Sixth Principal Meridian, Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located east of Berwick Drive, north of Tundra Drive)  – referred to Public Services 
Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Declaring as surplus a C ity-owned property legally described as Lot 1, 
Block 1, Clear Creek Subdivision, 1 st Filing; Lot 2, Block 1, Clear Creek Subdivision, 1 st Filing; 
and Lot 1, Block 1, Clear Creek Subdivision, 2 nd Filing together with improvements thereon, 
Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, and authorizing the City’s Purchasing Manager to proceed 
with the sale of said parcel pursuant to Wyo. Stat. §15 -1-112 – referred to Finance Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] CFD Park N Ride Planned Unit Development Preliminary Plan for a portion of the SE1/4, 
Section 11 and the NE1/4, Section 14, T13N, R67W, of the 6 th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming 
(located east of and adjacent t o I -25, north of College Drive) – referred to Public Services 
Committee. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agree ment between the City of Cheyenne and Russell + Mills 
Studios, Fort Collins, Colorado, for the North Cheyenne Community Park Masterplan  – referred 
to Finance Committee. 
  
 
[CA] Grant Award Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the State of Wyoming Office 
of Homeland Security for a grant from the U.S. Department of Homeland Security, FEMA, Grant 
Programs Directorate, State Homeland Security Program Grant Fiscal Year 2018 for the Cheyenne 
Regional Emergency Response Team #7 (DHS Grant) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Burns & McDonnell 
Engineering Company, Bloomington, Minnesota, for the City of Cheyenne Integrated Solid Waste 
Plan – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Grant Agreem ent between the City of Cheyenne, Transit Division, and the Wyoming 
Department of Health, Aging Division, to provide support services to persons 60 years or older 
under Title III-B Program Grant for FY19 (grant funds) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract #6892 between the City of Cheyenne, Public 
Works Department, and Noel Griffith Jr. & Associates Architects, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for design 
engineering of a second level shell and core to the Cheyenne Municipal Court Project (1% Specific 
Purpose Sales Tax).  Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works, provided informat ion on the 
project stating the modification is for design of a second level shell and core. Ms. Nemecek 
referenced a memo issued by  the City Treasurer c ontaining project funding source information.   
Upon inquiry by Joe Dougherty, Noel Griffith provided information on the project cost to date , 
which includes purchase of the building, asbestos removal, demolition, soft costs and second level 
shell and core totaling approximately $13.5 million.  He advised, upon inquiry, the second level 
would not be used for the Municipal Court and provided first and second floor measurements. Mr. 
White moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $31,100.00, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Luna 
voting “no”. (#6892) 
 
 Amendment No. 1 to Contract #6854, Agreement between the City of Cheyenne an d the 
Cheyenne Animal Shelter/Services for Animal Cont rol and Animal Shelter Services.  Mr. White 
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Case.  Upon inquiry, Phil Kiner, Interim CEO, Cheyenn e 
Animal Shelter, explained a third -party audit of policies and procedures will begin in early 
December.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing present. (#6854) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-6-19 for furnishing one new 2018-2019 motor grader for the Street 
and Alley Division (2015 – 2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted 
bid from Honnen Equipment, Mills, Wyoming, in an amount not to exceed $279,190.00). 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -9-19 for furnishing three new mowers for the Parks Divi sion 
(2015 – 2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S -7-19 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Community 
Recreation and Events Department, and A To  Z Recreation, Littleton, Colorado, for the Fitne ss 
Course at Lions Park Project (Rec Mill Levy Grant) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
  
 Appointment of Charles W. Bloom as Director of the Planning and Development 
Department for the City of Cheyenne.  Mayor Orr introduced and welcomed Mr. Bloom.  Mr. Luna 
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (Mayoral appointment). 
 
 Announcement was made that due to the Thanksgiving holiday schedule, the regularly 
scheduled City Council Public Services Committee meeting at Noon on Tuesday, November 20, 
2018 in the Mu nicipal Building, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Room 104, Cheyenne, Wyoming, will 
instead be held on Monday, November 19, 2018 beginning at Noon. 
 
Under other business: 
 
Mr. Johnson commented and requested clarification on  governing body participation 
related to site plan  review and miscellaneous study procedures  required during an annexation 
request.  Mayor Orr stated City Council  participation on related items occurs during Council 
Committee meetings and encouraged determining long term maintenance needs. Susana Montana, 
Planning and Development Department, explained the annexation application and review process.  
Discussion included agencies’ involvement during the review process and tryi ng to address 
concerns early in the process. 
 
Mr. Johnson requested clarification on the project dollar amount to date for the Cheyenne 
Municipal Court Project.  Mayor Orr  and City Treasurer Ron Downey reviewe d current and 
projected dollar amounts and discussed utilizing funds out of reserves that are needed in excess of 
the approved 6th Penny project fund dollar amount. Discussion included reserve account balance 
and utilization with Mayor Orr explaining re quired reserve fund am ounts (90 day reserve) and 
excess funds.   Upon in quiry, Mayor Orr advised estimates received to salv age historical 
components of the existing building at the proposed Municipal Court project site  were high, and 
confirmed the building will be demolished .  Public Works Director Vicki Nemecek stated she 
would review the project contract and provide the building demolition date, as requested. 
 
 Mr. Case commented on the possibility of a live performance of the proposed ‘Cheyenne 
Anthem’ at a future Council meeting.  Mayor Orr stated it is her understanding that there is a 
possibility the Cheyenne Children’s Choir will record the anthem and it will be presented during 
a Council meeting. 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:54 p.m. 
         
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kylie Soden 
        Deputy City Clerk 
   
 
  
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  November 24, 2018

 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning

Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.

 Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouche rs as presented, seconded by  Mr. Case.

ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from County A -1 Agricultural and Rural 
Residential to MUR Mixed -Use Residential Emphasis for a portion of the west half, of the west 
half of Section 17, T14N, R66W, of the 6th Principal Meridian Cheyenne, Wyoming (located west 
of and adjacent to Powderhouse Road, north of Spirit Lane ). Reporting for Public Services 
Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4249) 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2. 2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, 
Section 5.1.2, Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the 
Cheyenne Unified Development Code, changing the zoning classification from HR, High Density 
Residential to CB, Community Business for a parcel of land consisting of Lots 9 through 12, Block 
9, Meadowbrooke Park Subdivision, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the south east corner of 
Edgewater Avenue and Bluegrass Circle) .  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr.  Johnson.  Comment was made  regarding support of local restaurants. Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
  
Mayor Orr recognized Ward 3 Councilman -elect Ken Esquibel who was present at the 
meeting. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, 
Section 5.1.2, Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the 
Cheyenne Unified Development Code, changing the zoning classification from CB, Community 
Business to HR,  High Density Residential for a parcel of land consisting of a portion of Lot 1, 
Block 1, Plaza Del Range, Second Filing Subdivision, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at 3110 Dell 
Range Blvd., between Hilltop Avenue and Ridge Road).  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending, creating, deleting and re -creating various 
chapters and sections within Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the Cheyenne City Code 
relating to general provisions, and including adoption of the International Building Code and other 
related Codes, 2018 edition. Joe Patterson, Guardian Development Companies and representative 
for the Southeast Wyoming Homebuilders Association  (SEWBA), expressed concern with 
proposed removal of the fire sprinkler alternative compliance option for multi-family dwellings, 
and proposed changes to the energy code removing (exempting) the commissioning of HVAC 
equipment for new or existing buildings .  During his comments, Mr. Patterson requested those 
SEWBA members in attendance to stand to e xpress their support for retaining the current fire 
sprinkler compliance option.   Upon inquiry, Mr. Patterson advised in the past Chapter 11, Energy 
Efficiency Code, of the IRC (International Residen tial Code) has been removed and substituted 
with the 2009 IEEC (International Energy Efficiency Code), noting the proposed ordinance keeps 
everything the same but strikes out the HVAC commissioning, and provided information on state 
adoption of the 2018 International Building Code, local jurisdictional authority to adopt the IRC, 
and code adoptions by other jurisdictions related to fire sprinkler options vs. mandating sprinkler 
systems.  Tom Jacobson, local real estate agent, provided information on fire statistics involving 
fire systems; costs to builders invol ving mandate of requiring a sprinkler system, which will be 
passed on to buyers; local housing shortage, and fire wall requirements.   During discussion, Mr. 
Patterson and Mr. Jacobson provided information in response to inquiries regarding costs to 
property owners; adoption of codes by the state fire marshal vs. local jurisdiction; National 
Association of Homebuilders stance on a fire sprinkler system, and unknown homeowner 
insurance discount benefit relative to installation of a fire sprinkler system.  Dur ing comments, it 
was clarified the proposed fire sprinkler compliance option change would apply only to multi -
family dwellings.  Upon inquiry, Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney, explained Wyoming Statute Section 
35-9-121 which outlines the requirement by municip alities that have local authority to enforce 
codes to adopt, within a set time frame, minimum standards that are equivalent to or more stringent 
than those standards adopted by the state fire marshal, and advised local authority has been granted 
to the City to enforce the codes but that may revert to the state if compliance with Section 35 -9-
121 is not met.  She noted interpretation of this section requires technical expertise from Building 
and Fire personnel.   She further noted that both City Building a nd Fire Department  staff are in 
congruence that the proposed ordinance  meets minimum standards , which agreement has not 
always been the case in the past.   Alane West, Westline  Designs, Inc., spoke on the impact of 
requiring a fire sprinkler system in multi -family dwellings; current housing shortage , and 
  
maintenance costs.  Blas Hernandez, Chief Building Official, clarified mechanical commissioning 
of HVAC equipment means testing of the equipment , and explained the reason to remove the 
requirement was due to contractors advising him that it was an unnecessary cost.  Mr. Hernandez 
explained the change to fire sprinkler systems would affect multi -family dwellings, not small 
residential houses, and reviewed the difference between the IRC and IBC (International B uilding 
Code) relative to what would be required to be sprinkled, and the difference between fire walls 
and a fire sprinkler system , which provides time for occupants to vacate a building in case of a 
fire.  He expressed his support for the change, noting cost is important but the end concern is 
keeping people safe, and his belief that requiring a system follows the intent of the code.  He 
commented on the possibility of looking at modified sprinkler systems as an alternative to reduce 
costs.  Mr. Hernandez  provided information in response to inquiries concerning 13R sprinkler 
system requirements; other communities who require a fire sprinkler system , and water supply 
needs depending upon the system, and confirmed that adoption of new codes is not retroactiv e 
relative to enforcement.  Chief Greg Hoggatt, Fire and Rescue Department, also commented in 
support of requiring fire sprinkler systems in the interest of saving lives, and provided information 
on residential vs. commercial settings, and the effect of fi re walls vs. a sprinkler system, which 
results in less water damage.  He spoke in favor of providing a safer environment for life and 
protection of property, and, upon inquiry, expressed he also supports requiring residential sprinkler 
systems in the futur e.  Discussion included past alternative sprinkler system option; cost to 
builders; potential additional costs for water taps through the Board of Public Utilities, and public 
education campaign to gauge interest if requiring a sprinkler system in single residential homes is 
contemplated in the future.  Mr. Roybal reported there was no recommendation on the item from 
Public Services Committee.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr.  
Johnson.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend by substitute, seconded by Mr. Case.  Mr. Shanor reviewed 
changes proposed in the substitute ordinance, which would still adopt the 2018 codes but would 
leave the current alternative fire sprinkler compliance option in place ; reduce fees in certain 
sections of the ordinance relative to a Temporary Certificate of Occupancy (TOC) (i.e. sliding fee 
incentive) and keep the current investigation fees, relating to work commencing prior to a permit 
being obtained, with no fee increase.  Upon inquiry Logan Sharpe, City Staff Attorney, stated staff 
agrees with the substitute ordinance content with the exception of keeping investigation fees at the 
current level.  Evan Asher spoke in favor of a fire sprinkler system requirement citing the topic as 
a safety issue and not a cost issue.  Edward Ernste suggested alternative sprinkler system options 
that could perhaps be considered and spoke in favor of looking at 13D options vs. 13R.  Discussion 
topics included:  Alternative fire sprinkler or fire wall options; compliance with state statute; TCO 
sliding scale fee incentive; IRC vs. IBC; timing of receipt of the substitute document; further legal 
review regarding compliance with codes adopted by the state ; state code adoption d ate; fire 
inspection/enforcement authority; City Council’s amendment process, and distribution of  
information to the public relative to the substitute document.  Upon inquiry, Byron Mathews, Fire 
Division Chief, advised pursuant to adoption of the state codes by the state fire marshal, the City 
has until the end of 2018 to approve.   Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Case, Mr. 
Luna, Mr. Roybal, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Johnson, Mr. 
Laybourn and Mayor Orr.  Main moti on as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing present with the exception of Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn and Mayor Orr voting “no”. 
 
  
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending, deleting and re -creating sections within 
Chapter 8.12, International Fire Code adopted, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Cheyenne City 
Code relating to general provisions state pertaining to fire protection and regulations, and adoption 
of the International Fire Code, 2018 edition, and certain Codes and practices promulgated by the 
National Fire Protection Association .  Joe Patterson, S outheast Wyoming Homebuilders 
Association, requested consideration to keep the current City Code wording for Section 8.12.030 
(F) in place, which pertains to alternative fire sprinkl er compliance options, and referenced his 
comments on the topic under the previous agenda item.  Mr. Patterson provided a brief history of 
the sprinkler compliance option as approved by the governing body in the past; discussed utilizing 
domestic water taps vs. Board of Public Utilities’ reasons for requiring additional water taps for 
sprinkler systems in multi -plex family dwellings, and suggested, in the future, consideration be 
given to addressing IRC and IBC related codes by separate ordinances.  Chief Greg Hoggatt, Fire 
and Rescue Department, provided information on sprinkler systems, noting several elements are 
involved relative to waterflow determinations; stated he would be amenable to further discussion 
on sprinkler options, and indicated he would in itiate a discussion with the state fire marshal with 
regard to 13D sprinkler options.  Mr. Roybal reported there was no recommendation from Public 
Services Committee on the item.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by 
Mr. Laybourn.  Mr . Shanor moved to amend by  substitute, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. Shanor 
explained the amendment updates the code to the 2018 edition but allows for the alternative fire 
sprinkler compliance option currently existing in City Code to remain the same.  Discussion topics 
included:  Need for local affordable housing and regional front range competition, past state fire 
marshal acceptance of the compliance option, and past code adoptions.  Motion to amend carried.  
Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, Mr. Luna, Mr. Roy bal, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor and Mr. White.  Voting 
“no” – Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn and Mayor Orr.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing present with the exception of Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn 
and Mayor Orr voting “no”. 
 
Following a brief recess, Mayor Orr reconvened the meeting at 8:30 p.m. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapter 2.32, Fire Department, Section 
2.32.020, Department Personnel, and Chapter 2.84, Miscellaneous Boards and Commissions, 
Section 2.84.050,  International Fire Code Board of Appeals, of Title 2, Administration and 
Personnel, of the Cheyenne City Code pertaining to the Fire and Rescue Department.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr.  Case.  Motion carried. Voting “yes”  – all 
members of the governing body present. 
. 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Creating Section 1.08.030, Official Song -Recognition 
and Use, of Chapter 1.08, Official Seal and Emblem, of Title 1, General Provisions, of the Code 
of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, pertaining to designation of ‘Cheyenne Anthem’ (©2018) as 
the official song of the City of Cheyenne.   Scott Meyer, speaking on behalf of AAI Music 
Publishing and the songwriters, spoke in support of the ordinance and commented on proposed 
changes to combine wording in Sections (B) and (C), suggesting that wording be retained 
regarding promotion through advertising, marketing and tourism and by adding the word 
“education”.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Meyer explained fundraising was done to assist with expenses to 
go to Nashville to professionally record the song and the artists paid for some expenses out of their 
own pocket.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr . White .  Mr. 
  
Roybal moved to amend by combining Sections (B) and (C) into a new Section (B) to read as 
follows, seconded by Dr. Rinne:  The City shall have non-exclusive gratis rights to use the entire 
song or any portion thereof, the name of the song, for any and all official City commercial and/or 
non-commercial uses including merchandise, and by relabeling Section (D) to Section (C).  
Discussion followed on amending the ordinance per Mr. Meyer’s suggestion, and whether a 
conflict existed with the name of the song and permission had been obtained relative to graphic 
images affiliated with a correlating video.  During comment on whether a conflict  existed or 
permission was received for graphic images, Mr. Shanor raised a point of order that comments 
were not germane to the ordinance as the ordinance does not address the topics with Mayor Orr 
concurring.  Further discussion included who would be res ponsible for enforcement of the 
copyright.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present 
with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend revised Section (B)  
to read as follows, seconded by Mr . Luna:  The City shall have non -exclusive gratis right to use 
the entire song or any portion thereof, the name of the song, for any and all official City commercial 
and/or non-commercial uses including, but not limited to, merchandise, promotion, advertis ing, 
marketing, tourism and education, and by striking the words “and C” in revised Section (C).  For 
clarification, it was noted if anything is disputed in the future between the City and those owning 
the rights to the song regarding whether the City’s use follows the specified uses outlined in the 
ordinance, it would require negotiation between the parties.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  
Main motion as twice amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present 
with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.

 RESOLUTION – Declaring as surplus a City -owned property legally described as Lot 1,

the parcels, and explained sales proceeds would go toward parks development  and the City’s bid 
process would be utilized for sale of the property.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Nemecek provided 
information on City ease ments that may be requested; referenced information she had provided

Discussion topics included:  Hesitancy to sell the property due to potential use as public access to
